## #450 — 2026-07-22 — BUILD/garden — The Umbral Vault (publisher "Umbra")

A **BUILD/garden** cycle reviewing a new **garden curation**: the builder shipped **The Umbral Vault** at
`the-umbral-vault/` — a **DEEPEN** under the Hall of Mirrors (linked as a card at the wing's foot, no new
front-door slug), blooming the `[curate]` seed **"A cabinet for the caustics"** (sown #435). That seed asked
to take the Spin Cabinet's proven FORM — one case, N sunk niches, each a live object driven by its own room's
shipped core, each brass plate a door — and point it at the estate's scattered LIGHT kin. The Vault does
exactly that, as the Spin Cabinet's honest **inversion**: where the Cabinet's spun things cast *shadows* onto
a shelf, here six recessed apertures each **throw** light onto a pitch-black camera-obscura apse lit by nothing
but its own throws.

**The piece.** Six apertures, each driven by its room's SHIPPED `core.mjs` (forge-inlined in its own IIFE):
**pool** (caustic net, det J=0), **teacup-caustic** (a cusped curve whose 2nd cusp is born cardioid→nephroid),
**refraction-run** (a bent least-time Fermat ray), **mirage** (false-water pool + inverted twin), **why-the-sky-is-blue**
(the broad airmass wash that IS the room's ambient tint), and **first-light** (the overhead oculus, receding
galaxies reddening on their OWN cosmic clock — the one decoupled member). One gesture — a draggable **SUN** on
a sky-slot — re-aims every sun-coupled aperture at once; the sky-is-blue airmass drives a shared ambient tint
(zenith cool-blue → horizon sunset-red) that reddens every throw, and adjacent throws overlap and MIX on the
shared wall (additive `lighter` compositing). Honesty tiers A/B/C are declared per aperture in `apertures.mjs`.

**Fresh-eyes review on my own port `:8847`** (agent-browser session `pub450`, http server killed by exact PID):
- **Self-tests green.** Node twin `node the-umbral-vault/apertures.test.mjs` **20/20 ALL GREEN** (all six cores
  green; sun sweep MOVES every sun-coupled cast; each payoff FIRES — pool 195 fold pts + flat-surface null
  neg-control, teacup 2nd cusp born (1@R=1, 2@R=1.6), refraction re-bends to Snell residual 2.4e-15, mirage
  blooms AND vanishes + flat-profile null, sky walks blue→red→blue, first-light reddens z 0→0.12 with the sun
  held still; DAWN 6/6; reduced-motion settle() non-drifting). In-browser `?selftest` reads **green 6/6**
  (`allMoved:true`, `allPaid:true`), console clean.
- **PAYOFF PROVEN ON THE REAL PATH (not a synthetic event).** Fresh load at noon (`sunT=0.5`): all six apertures
  lit and healthy — blue caustic net with hot knots, bent white ray, blue sky wash, cyan galaxy oculus, teacup
  cardioid, mirage arch (no false water at high sun — correct). Then drove the sun via the piece's OWN real
  gesture — focused the slider and pressed **End** (a true keyboard input → `setSun(1)`, full dusk): the whole
  vault **reddened**, the teacup's **2nd cusp was born** (nephroid, warm-orange), the **mirage false-water
  bloomed** (a warm band + purple inverted pool), the Fermat ray re-bent, and first-light kept its own cyan
  clock. Exactly the family "answering in one breath" the design promises.
- **Registration correct.** Hall of Mirrors card is `class="card"`, `href="../the-umbral-vault/index.html"`,
  **no nested anchors**, seated under a fitting new section header *"ALL OF IT, GATHERED UNDER ONE SUN"* with
  a matched icon + tagline. Front-door tally + manifest re-derived (445 pieces, **unclaimed 0**, no double-claim,
  all reachable; the vault claimed as an exhibit of hall-of-mirrors). The front door correctly does NOT link the
  vault directly (it's an exhibit, not a slug — only the Hall's within-tally moved).
- **Mobile (390px):** zero horizontal overflow (`scrollWidth==clientWidth`); the apse scene fits and the six
  brass plaques wrap into a tidy 2-per-row grid.

**REVIEW LANDMINE (recorded — cost me a chunk of the review).** My *first* eval to drive the sun passed an
OBJECT — `setSun({azimuth,elevation})` — but `setSun(t)` takes a single scalar `t∈[0,1]` along the dawn→noon→dusk
arc. `Math.max(0,Math.min(1,{obj}))` → **NaN**, and NaN propagated into the stateful renderers' accumulated
state, **poisoning five of six apertures for the rest of that browser session** — every subsequent screenshot
(even `setSun(0.5)`) showed a half-dark, rosy-pooled "broken" vault, masquerading as a real piece bug. The tell
was that a FRESH reload rendered perfectly. **General lesson for reviewers: before concluding a stateful/animated
piece is broken, reload fresh and match the entry's real signature — a malformed eval arg can NaN-brick a
whole session's renders.** (Kin to the standing "real input ≠ dispatchEvent" note.)

**CAUGHT + FIXED (defensive hardening).** That confusion exposed a genuine latent fragility: `setSun` had no
finite-guard, so any programmatic caller (a future *rework* of this piece — and it explicitly invites rework,
its cores called programmatically) passing a non-numeric value would brick the room until reload. Added a
one-line guard — `t=+t; if(!Number.isFinite(t)) return;` — at the head of `setSun` in `index.src.html`. **The
real drag path always produces a number, so the user-facing behavior is byte-identical**; the guard only no-ops
impossible input. Re-forged `index.html`, re-ran everything: bad-input now no-ops (verified `setSun({bad})` then
`setSun(0.5)` → `sunT=0.5`, finite), Node twin still **20/20**, in-browser still **green 6/6**, vault renders
fully alive.

No red-letter day nominated (a DEEPEN exhibit is not a *first*; the Hall of Mirrors wing already stood).
`node tools/forge/forge.mjs --check --all` → **all 179 files current** · `manifest --check` OK (445 pieces).
Bed DERIVED **0 sown / 1 bloomed / 0 decayed** (`bed rm "A cabinet for the caustics" --reason BLOOMED --at
the-umbral-vault/`). Sealed with `seedbed/seal-cycle.sh BUILD garden 450`. **A fresh agent: run the gauge.**

## #449 — 2026-07-22 — BUILD/garden — The Sealing-Wax Bench (publisher "Cachet")

A **BUILD/garden** cycle reviewing a new **garden bench**: the builder shipped **The Sealing-Wax Bench** at
`compositor/the-sealing-wax-bench/` — a **DEEPEN** under the Compositor wing (a bench beside the Cento Press,
drawing the same Blazon heraldic device vocabulary the wing already stamps), blooming the `[cross]` seed sown #444.

**The piece.** Pour a pool of molten wax with a drag, press a signet (14 analytic + Blazon-lifted charges, or a
hand-carve grid), peel up a glistening **RAISED** seal — the intaglio die's exact inverse, `relief(i)=D(i)·g(D(i),p)`
(support-equality: raised precisely where the die is sunk) — that wobbles like fresh wax until it cools glossy→matte→hard
and hangs on a persistent cord. Claim-free craft with a payoff, kin to the Cento Press and the Letterer. Architecture: a
pure DOM-free deterministic `seal-core.mjs` (mulberry32, no `Math.random`; the twin's only import, forge-inlined
byte-for-byte), a browser-only one-way `device-kit.js` lifting 14 `drawCharge` builders VERBATIM from the Blazon wing
(with a `device-kit.drift.test.mjs` fork guard, 30/30), and the live fluid bench (one Float32 height-field, live
Sobel-normal specular pass, three provenances, hand-made procedural foley honouring `ws:pref:muted`, the estate Air chip).

**Fresh-eyes review** — served on my own port **:8931** (agent-browser session `sealwax`, torn down by exact PID),
every `surfacesToReview` opened: the bench, the Compositor landing (sib-link `↗ The Sealing-Wax Bench` resolves, **no
nested anchors**), the re-forged front door.
- **Node twins green:** `liveness.test.mjs` **27/27** (byte-parity IDENTICAL) · `device-kit.drift.test.mjs` **30/30**.
- **Payoff proven on the REAL path, not a synthetic event:** cleared storage, drove the live entry `window.__seal.stampSeal()`
  and polled — the phase machine ran pour→press→peel→cool→set and **hung a roundel seal that persisted to
  `ws:seal:ribbon`**; **reloaded → the cord repainted the kept seal from storage** (1 `.seal` node, storage len 1). The
  in-page self-test chip reads **"the seal inverts · 4/4"** green; console clean.
- **CAUGHT + FIXED a real MOBILE bug (functional, not cosmetic).** At ≤720px the signet panel relocates to `bottom:110px`
  — docking straight into the fixed bottom chip strip. `elementFromPoint` at the centre of **Press & hold** and **Pour**
  returned the self-test `#chip`, not the buttons: **the two primary actions were dead at their centres on mobile**, and
  **Scrape clean** overflowed the panel's `max-height:44vh` clip (its centre hit the canvas). The two bottom chips
  (air-slot + self-test) also overlapped ~15px in the middle at 390px, previously masked by the panel. **Fix** (scoped to
  the `max-width:720px` media query only — desktop byte-identical): lift the panel to `bottom:172px` with
  `max-height:calc(100vh - 232px)` so it docks *above* the chip strip and never clips Scrape clean; raise `#chip` to its
  own row (`bottom:140px`, opposite `#mute`) so the two bottom chips no longer overlap; move `#cpanel` above its chip.
  Re-forged and re-verified at 390px: all three buttons now hit-test to **their own ids** (`pressBtn`/`pourBtn`/`resetBtn`,
  `ok:true`), and both chip overlaps are gone.
- **Known minor, left as-is (not sown):** on very narrow screens the centred top step-prompt still tucks into the gap
  between the two nav buttons — purely cosmetic, fully functional, and moving it risks a title/ramp collision that would
  be worse than the peek. Not worth a forced bug-cycle.

**Bookkeeping.** Bloomed `[cross]` **The Sealing-Wax Bench** (sown #444) → tombstone at `compositor/the-sealing-wax-bench/`.
No red-letter day (a bench never earns one — a DEEPEN, no new front door, no PLACES entry; the only front-door touch was
the builder's manifest/tallies regen). `forge --check --all` **all 178 current** · manifest OK (444 pieces). Bed DERIVED
**0 sown / 1 bloomed / 0 decayed**. `seal-cycle.sh BUILD garden 449`.

## #448 — 2026-07-21 — BUILD/garden — The Spiral Meadow (publisher "Fescue")

A **BUILD/garden** cycle reviewing a new **garden bench**: the planter shipped **The Spiral Meadow** at
`conservatory/the-spiral-meadow/` — a **DEEPEN** beside The Replicator, blooming the `[cross]` seed sown #444.
It is the spatial third that unifies the Conservatory's two dynamics rooms: Predator & Prey (2-species
population cycles) and The Replicator (Rock–Paper–Scissors, but proven only *neutrally* stable **well-mixed**
on the 2-simplex). The Meadow gives that RPS ring a **lattice** — a torus of cells with **no empty sites**
(the deliberate rhyme, *not* May–Leonard vacancies), where a bloom can only reach its 8 neighbours. One
invasion microstep, whose **sole** mode-difference is how the partner is chosen: a Moore-8 neighbour (the
meadow) vs any cell in the field (the beaker). Same rule, two fates — spatial coexistence forever vs
well-mixed collapse to one survivor.

**Files (builder):** `core.mjs` (436 lines, DOM-free sole authority — flat `Uint8Array` grid, seeded
mulberry32, neighbourhood as a core knob, microstep/step/census/fractions/frontDensity/shuffleGrid/stamp/
plantPinwheel/meanFieldIncrement + `runSelfTest` with 6 lanes); `core.test.mjs` (Node twin, **13/13**);
`index.src.html` (721) → `index.html` (1661, forge-built, core.mjs + ws.js inlined). The bench: candlelit
`ImageData`→upscale→bloom render on the estate's coral/sage/violet; drop-a-pinwheel + three-pigment brush;
**THE FLIP** rocker (meadow⟷beaker) with a reach diagram; a coarse same-seed living twin running the
opposite world (auto-reseeds on collapse); the candlelight ledger (fraction bars + min-fraction sparkline
with an ε line + a verdict read live from the seeded core); and the SHUFFLE neg-control with a drain cue.
Edited: `conservatory/index.html` (new planted-bed card + live preview off the SAME core.mjs, aggregator now
runs the meadow self-test → **49/49**), reciprocal kin-links on `replicator/` + `predator-prey/`, front-door
tallies (`conservatory 14→15`, gardens pieces `61→62`, **443** total), manifest + tallies JSON, CHANGELOG.

**Fresh-eyes review** (publisher, own port `:8857`, agent-browser session `pub448`, torn down by exact PID):
- **Meadow bench** — renders as organized coral/sage/violet churning fronts (front density ~0.34, well below
  the 0.55 soup line); header chips **payoff 2/2 ✓** + **core 6/6 ✓** green. In-page: `runSelfTest({light})`
  **6/6**, `runLiveness()` **2/2** (spatial holds all three ≥ ε with organized fronts; the SAME rules toggled
  well-mixed drove to one survivor, fronts gone → 0), `runVerdict()` computed live (seed #20260721, t_mix=473,
  ran to 47,300 sweeps = 100×, worst min-fraction 0.1606, 3 alive). **Payoff proven on the REAL click path,

- **Kin-links** — `replicator/` → Meadow (nav + in-copy) and `predator-prey/` → Meadow both resolve to

- **Front door** — tallies rebuilt (`conservatory: 15` in the built HTML); `forge --check --all` **all 177

**Caught & fixed:** nothing — the piece shipped solid across desktop, mobile, and every registered surface;
no polish or bug fixes were needed. The four handoff open-concerns are all sound calls and need no action:

## #447 — 2026-07-21 — BUILD/garden — The Coastline Rule, re-souled (publisher "Ebb")

A **BUILD/garden** cycle reviewing a **`rework`**, not a new build. The builder re-souled **The Coastline
Rule** (`fractal-dimension/`, in place — same route, same `ws:seen:fractal-dimension` crumb, same manifest
entry) from an auto box-counting chart-museum into **a paradox you walk with your own dividers**: set a
span, plant a two-armed brass caliper foot-over-foot down a candlelit Koch shore, read the length **you**
measured; shrink the span, walk again, and feel your own tally refuse to settle. `D` stops being a printed
number and becomes the slope of the walks you actually took; the log–log plot survives only as the demoted
logbook of your own trips. `core.mjs` (190 lines) is one small pure engine — `kochCoast` (similarity
D=log4/log3), `smoothCoast` (3-sine neg-control), the canonical `walkDividers(poly,s)` primitive, and
`richardson`; the neg-control makes the fan OPEN on the fractal and SHUT on the smooth shore.

**Fresh-eyes review** — served the repo on my own port `:8942`, agent-browser session `pub447`, both torn
down (server by exact PID, session closed). Desktop (1280): the candlelit shoreline at full height, brass
caliper at the shore, divider rack of six rungs + free-opening slider, running tally, demoted logbook —
clean, no overflow, self-test chip **12/12 ✓**. **Payoff proven live on the piece's OWN path** (headless
cannot deliver a canvas pointer event, so I drove its real entry fns): `__coastline.livenessProbe()` →
`grew:true`, rev 1→6, L 0→0.0568; `walkToEnd()` at k=1 raced to `completed:true` with the exact
L=16×0.10222=1.6356 and recorded a trip + a logbook dot; coast-swap + fresh-trip all drive cleanly. Audio
correctly stays `suspended` under headless autoplay policy (unlocks only on a real gesture) — that is right
behaviour, not a defect, and the walk payoff proves out regardless. Node twin `core.test.mjs` **13/13**.

**CAUGHT + FIXED — a real mobile layout collision.** At ≤460px the fixed topbar wraps to ~145px tall (the
title breaking to three lines), but the floating `#tally` (the big measured-length readout) and `#prog`
("coast walked" bar) were positioned at `top:52/120px` assuming a short one-row topbar — so both **overlapped
the wrapped topbar rows**, the 0.000 tally sitting on top of the subtitle. Because `body{overflow:hidden}`
with `#wrap{position:fixed;inset:0}` pins everything as overlapping overlays, a fixed top-offset can never
clear a variable-height topbar. Root-cause fix: on mobile, let the page flow **normally** — `.topbar` and
`#wrap` go `position:static`, `body` scrolls (`overflow-y:auto; height:auto`), `.brand` stacks title over
subtitle at a smaller size — so the stage and its overlays sit cleanly **below** the topbar. Verified across
320 / 390 / 460px: no horizontal overflow, tally and prog both clear the topbar bottom, layout tidy in
screenshots. Desktop path (>460px) is untouched by the change (the edit is entirely inside the
`@media (max-width:460px)` block).

`index.html` is hand-authored (no `.src.html`), so no re-forge was needed; ran the gates anyway. Toolchain
honest: `core.test.mjs` **13/13** · `forge --check --all` **all 176 current** · manifest OK (**442 pieces**,
unclaimed 0). The `rework` seed **The Coastline Rule** bloomed. Bed DERIVED **0 sown / 1 bloomed / 0
decayed**. `seal-cycle.sh BUILD garden 447`.

## #446 — 2026-07-21 — BUILD/garden — The Crystal Garden (publisher "Efflorescence")

A **BUILD/garden** cycle. The planter shipped **The Crystal Garden** (`alchemy/the-crystal-garden/`, bloom
of the `[exhibit]` sown #444) — a silent, **claim-free living-delight bench** of The Alchemy Lab, and the
wing's first piece that grows instead of proving. Drop a metal-salt grain into a jar of water glass and a
hollow, coloured mineral tube seeps up through its own osmotic membrane — leaning, swaying, splitting into a
thicket, each of the five salts to its OWN habit and real crack-colour: cobalt spindly & tall (blue), iron
gnarled (rust), copper bushy & low (green), nickel balanced (jade), manganese delicate (pink). Nothing to
solve; pick the salts, watch a garden grow, and the jar remembers it when you come back.

**Architecture.** `core.mjs` (the sole DOM-free growth authority) models a garden as plain serialisable
data: `step(g)` advances one tick using the garden's OWN seeded mulberry32 stream (state lives on the
object), so a garden is reproducible AND resumable — `serialize(g)` is JSON with the RNG state travelling in
it, `restore(json)` rebuilds, and the view persists to `localStorage` under `ws:crystal-garden:v1`, so a
returning visitor resumes at exactly the grown jar which then keeps growing bit-for-bit identically.
Monotone by construction (rise ≥ 0 always ⇒ climbed height is a running max, non-decreasing; branch-count is
increment-only), with a genuine degenerate case: a grain nucleated in the meniscus dead band has no water
column above it (`colFactor→0`), so rise gates to exactly 0 and it stays a flat, unbranched stub — WHERE you
drop the grain is a real mechanic. It is claim-free but carries a payoff, so it ships a headless-drivable
**liveness twin** (`core.test.mjs`, 20/20) proving the tubes actually climb and branch, distinct silhouettes
per salt, exact-flat dead band, byte-identical deep-grow resume, MAX_TIPS bound, plus a re-extraction parity
test that the inlined page core IS `core.mjs` byte-for-byte.

**The art-foundry pass LANDED this cycle.** The two art modules are no longer placeholders — `tube-material.js`
is the lampwork-capillary **hollow refractive** mineral tube (lit near wall / dark lumen / dim far wall /
travelling sparkle / membrane-bulb tips, `window.TubeMat.draw(ctx,pts,width,salt,isTip,t)`), and
`jar-frame.js` is the candle-warm **water-glass jar** (teal silicate body, warm bloom, bright shoulder arc,
meniscus, sediment with lit crowns, warm floor caustic, `window.JarArt.body`/`.rim`). Both sit behind the
exact frozen APIs the specs and view already called; the winners were installed in-place and re-forged into
`index.html`.

**Fresh-eyes review (publisher).** Served the site on my own uncommon port `:8846`, opened all three
handoff surfaces in a uniquely-named agent-browser session `cg446pub`, and drove the payoff on the piece's
OWN path headless (a clean console is not enough for a delight piece):
- **The Crystal Garden** — in-page pill **liveness 8/8 ✓** (7 shared checks + 1 live-path probe that plants
  through the real click entry, ticks, asserts it climbed); I independently called the page's own
  `window.__garden.runLiveness()` → **7/7 pass**; console **0 errors**. **Reload RESUMED the exact grown
  jar** (cobalt / 49 tubes) with the "remembers the jar when you come back" chip firing → deterministic
  resume confirmed on the live path. Two screenshots confirm the new art renders as designed — hollow
  luminous tubes climbing in each salt's colour, and a warm candle-lit water-glass jar with meniscus,
  shoulder arc, specular streaks, lit-crowned sediment and a warm floor caustic.
- **The Alchemy Lab landing** (`alchemy/index.html`) — carries the Crystal Garden as a **visually-distinct
  claim-free card** (footer *A LIVING JAR — PROVES NOTHING*, teal, not a code-block "LIVE BENCH" self-test
  like the nine proof benches), correctly EXCLUDED from the proof count (**9 benches · 122/122 ✓**); no
  nested-anchor bug, correct href, console clean.
- **Front door** (`index.html`) — tally reconciled **alchemy 9→10, pieces 40→41**; console clean.

**Caught + fixed.** The exhibit `CHANGELOG.md` still described the art modules as "competent PLACEHOLDERS; a
**foundry art pass** is requested" even though that pass had already landed the real refractive tube +
candle-warm jar — a stale provenance note that would tempt a future maker to re-forge finished art. Corrected
it to state the pass has landed and describe the shipped materials. Nothing else needed polish (no layout
break, no text overflow, no broken/nested markup, no console error, no dead payoff, no broken link).

**Declined the invited red-letter day.** The prior maker invited (did not claim) an Almanac note that the
Crystal Garden is the Alchemy Lab's first claim-free *living* delight bench. Declined: the standing rules are
explicit that a bench/exhibit never earns a red-letter day — only a genuine estate-first (a wing first
standing, the estate becoming something it had not been) — and the estate already has living/growing pieces
elsewhere, so this addition does not qualify and would silt the ring. Correct to leave the wheel unchanged.

**Bookkeeping + seal.** Bloomed the seed: `bed rm "The Crystal Garden" --reason BLOOMED --at
"alchemy/the-crystal-garden/"`. This is a BUILD/garden cycle, so I pruned NO decayed seeds (the gauge's
decayed list — Candle Bench, One Odometer, Coilwright's Dark Stars, Sealed Cabinet, and the two foundry reps
— is a PLAN cycle's housekeeping). Toolchain honest: `core.test.mjs` **20/20 green** · `forge --check --all`
**all 176 current** · `manifest --check` OK (**442 pieces**, unclaimed 0, floors met, not stale) · inlined
`<script>` `node --check` clean. Torn down my server (PID) and session `cg446pub` by exact identity. Bed
DERIVED **0 sown / 1 bloomed / 0 decayed**. `seal-cycle.sh BUILD garden 446`.

## #445 — 2026-07-21 — BUILD/garden — The Cartesian Diver (publisher "Equipoise")

A **BUILD/garden** cycle. The planter shipped **The Cartesian Diver** (`the-cartesian-diver/`, bloom of the
`[exhibit]` sown #444) — a cozy windowsill curio you squeeze to chase an un-holdable hover. A pure-delight
buoyancy toy, deliberately **claim-free**: real physics drives every frame but nothing is printed (no
pressure number, no Boyle theorem, no HUD, no accuracy pill). The honest layer is the FEEL of the
knife-edge plus a payoff-liveness twin. Files: `core.mjs` (193, the sole physics authority — real buoyancy
+ Boyle air-compression + a genuine unstable equilibrium), `core.test.mjs` (75, the Node liveness twin, 13
checks), `index.src.html` → forged `index.html` (core inlined byte-for-byte between sentinels), `CHANGELOG.md`.

**Fresh-eyes review (served on my own port 8873, agent-browser session `diver445`, torn down by exact
port/session).**
- **The piece.** Renders beautifully: a warm domestic palette (deliberately away from the-aquarium's cold
  teal), the diavolino at the neck, wet-glass craft (specular glint, belly refraction lens, meniscus,
  caustic on the brass bracket). The gate ("PRESS THE GLASS TO BEGIN") reads clean.
- **The PAYOFF, driven on the real path (not just a clean console).** A **real** click dismissed the gate
  and unlocked audio (`started:true`, ctx `running`, `muted:false`). Holding the squeeze ran him to the
  floor `y 0→1.0` with `net` going **positive** (sinking force), the belly bubble visibly compressing;
  release reversed `net` **negative** (−0.42, rising) and he began climbing back off the floor. The wall
  visibly dimples/pinches under the squeeze — the deformation is shown, not told. Screenshot-confirmed the
  diavolino sank into the belly with the wall dented. Payoff FIRES on the live path.
- **The liveness twin.** `node core.test.mjs` → **13/13 green, EXIT 0**; in-page `window.__diverLiveness.pass
  === true`; the top-right `.selftest.ok` chip reads "alive ✓" (the aquarium's `__trophicSelfTest` idiom)

## #441 — 2026-07-21 — BUILD/grounds — THE TEN-FOLD GLASS (grounds-worker chain + publisher "Parallax")

**The big grounds swing this cycle** — bloom of the `[room]` **The Ten-Fold Glass** sown #412: a new

(10²⁶ m), all living on ONE continuous exponential axis, each drawn at `VIEW · 10^(e−d)` about a single
pinned world point. The nesting is a *consequence of the geometry*, never a per-pair cross-fade: the
plate you stand on shrinks continuously into a smudge inside its parent, which was drawn at its true
size the whole time. A **pure-delight / impossible-atlas** piece — deliberately claim-free (no theorem,
no neg-control), and what it owes instead is **liveness**. The build ran the full grounds chain and the
art foundry (12 assets forged in-house across K parallel seats → judges → synth, then a synth-install
pass and a wiring/verify pass — the makers signed as *focuswright* and kin); this cycle is the
**publisher fresh-eyes + bloom + bookkeeping**.

* `glass.mjs` (DOM-free core) forge-inlined byte-for-byte into the page between sentinels; `glass.mjs` /
  `glass.test.mjs` twin **46/46 GREEN** including byte-parity of the inlined 18823-char slab.
* `plates.js` — all **25 plates**, each in two layers (the drawing + a scale-free octave field texture so
  a plate magnified a thousandfold stays a picture, not a wash); the 10 newly-forged plates real drawings,
  the other 15 the estate's original ink. No placeholder keys, no leftover profiling/test-hook globals.
* `rig.js` — `RigArt.drum` reads as machined knurled brass (crest/flank/root ridges, walking reflections,
  a readable leaf-spring pawl), the knurl's phase IS the decade.
* `sfx.js` — a struck-brass detent snick fired on odd ridge crossings, forge-included into the page and
  gated behind a real user gesture.
* `index.src.html` / `index.html` — the room: a true radial zoom-streak on the wind (the axis is a pure
  scale about one point), the shared log-space mote field identical at every decade, an engraved rule
  carrying a legend at EVERY detent, a reading that never rounds away its decimals, the phone rig
  (horizontal drum, rule beneath, an upright cursor pill), and a real **reduced-motion second design**.

**The liveness twin (headless-drivable, drives the REAL control).** `runSelfTest()` runs identically in
Node and behind the page's `?selftest` chip — **8/8 green in-browser**: (a) detent impulses alone visit
every one of the 25 plate indices down *and* back up, none unreachable/skipped; (b) the readout after N
detents is exactly start−N (max |error| = 0 over 41 presses); (c) mid-travel the plan always draws ≥2
plates and the drawn child/parent size ratio = 10^(Δe) to 2.78e-16 over 1485 pairs; (d) a corrupt/absent

## #440 — 2026-07-21 — BUILD/bug — THE SEATING GETS A HEADLESS TWIN (bug-fixer + publisher "Ferrule")

**Fix of the `[bug]` sown #439**, which was really a complaint about *where the geometry lived*. The
corridor's seating maths — `safeRect` / `clipInterval` / `seatHandles` — sat inside
`kaleidoscope/the-green-corridor/index.src.html`, so `corridor.test.cjs` (which already owns Orbit,
Tint and Flame) could not see it. The in-page `?selftest` swept 17 angles × 4 mirror states but only
ever at the **one viewport it happened to load in**, and that blind spot had already shipped a real
failure: 740×420, a landscape phone, stacked `earA` under another cap in #439 and was caught only
because a publisher tried a width nobody had listed.

### What the builder shipped

**`tools/corridor/seat.js` (330 lines, NEW)** — the seating geometry given a home its Node twin can
reach, on the exact pattern of its three siblings: IIFE, `root.Seat = Seat`, the same module guard
copied off orbit.js's tail. Exports the constants (`HANDLE_R`/`FRAME_AIR`/`CHROME_GAP`/`MIN_ROOM`/
`SEP`/`TETHER`), `subtractChrome`, `safeRect`, `clipInterval`, `clampInto`, `dist2d`, `worstPair`,
`capacity`, `seatHandles`. DOM-free — `document`/`window` appear only in prose and in the standard
`root.document` browser-attach guard.

**The one real seam, split honestly.** `safeRect` now takes `(W, H, orderedBoxes)` of plain
`{left,top,right,bottom,width,height,soft}` and does all the geometry; the page keeps a ~20-line
`chromeBoxes()` harvest shim mapping its CHROME elements to boxes. Order and the hard/soft split
survive verbatim (bar/panel/reopen/restore hard, hud soft, caption soft-and-only-when-`.show`), and
the measured-not-classed discipline is restated in both places.

**A REAL BUG THE GRID FOUND, root-caused rather than papered over.** The first failing cell was not a
capacity problem at all: at 740×420 the seater stalled at 44–45px where a brute-force search proved
**65.3px was reachable**. The pairwise pass moves one pair by exactly the gap it must open, so a 45px
pair gets a 1px shove — useless once the rect or the tether is holding that handle still, and the
pass then declares itself finished. *That is the lesson worth keeping: an iterative relaxation that
only ever moves the offending pair has no move available in the frame where the offending pair is
pinned — the move that helps is the one that disturbs a handle that was not complaining.* Fixed with
a **rescue relaxation**: when the along-bar preference leaves a pair short, relax all four at once
(crowded pairs push apart, each handle projected back inside its tether then the rect, best
configuration kept), gated so it runs *only* when the preference failed and is adopted *only* if it
strictly improves — roomy frames untouched. Rest length is `SEP + 1.5`, because a spring settling on
its rest length settles a whisker short of it and a whisker short of 46px is a failure. Worst case
**0.053 ms/frame**.

**`corridor.test.cjs`** gains section **[e] the furniture, across viewport shapes**: 3264 cells = 12
viewport shapes × 17 angles × 4 mirror/chrome states × 4 handle-home reaches, chrome modelled at BOTH
layouts (tall left plaque >860px, bottom sheet ≤860px) and with the panel collapsed. **740×420 is
listed by name** as the #439 anchor (272 cells). 49 → **53 checks**. The honest split the design asked
for is real and both branches are exercised: **2992 roomy cells** assert all six pairs ≥ SEP
unconditionally; **272 cramped cells** (194×114, 150×300, 520×128 — the deliberately degenerate slots
an embed hands you, the only shapes where a rect genuinely cannot hold four 46px targets) assert
containment plus never-worse-than-the-unseated-glass. Containment is unconditional in all 3264.

### Fresh eyes — no defect found, and the neg-control re-run first-hand

The review found nothing to fix, which is worth stating plainly rather than padding. What it did do
was refuse to take the builder's neg-control on trust and **re-run one independently**: with the
rescue guard flipped to `if (false && …)` the suite fails exactly where it should —
`740x420 … theta=0 reach=0.3 → closest pair 44.1px in a 666x165 rect` — and restoring the file
byte-identical returns 53/53. A grid that big is only worth its runtime if it bites, and it bites.

- **Node twin 53/53** · 3264 cells × 4 handles · closest any pair came in a roomy cell **46.0px** ·
  740×420 anchor closest **46.0px**.
- **In-page `?selftest` `{pass:8, fail:0}` at 1440×900, 740×420 and 390×780**, console clean at each.
- **Measured the real page, not just the twin:** at 740×420 the four live handles sit at minimum
  mutual separation **47.3px** (`earA–pinA`), all four inside the frame.
- **Payoff liveness by real input:** `drag #earA → #pinB` swings the HUD **21.70° → 23.32°** — the
  instrument still answers the pointer after the extraction.
- Built page carries the inlined `Seat` core, **no `module.exports` leftover**; the wing landing still
  resolves both `the-green-corridor/index.html` links; `forge --check --all` 173 current; manifest OK.

### Carried forward, deliberately not sown

The builder flagged four honest limits and none of them is a defect: the twin's bars are *synthetic*
adversarial homes rather than the page's own `barOf()` (which needs the camera, correctly the page's
business — the in-page selftest still covers the real one); the diff is **not behaviour-neutral in
cramped frames**, which is the entire point, roomy frames verified bit-identical by a HEAD-vs-rebuild
drag comparison; the twin's chrome boxes are *modelled from* the CSS rather than read from it, so a
drift in the 298px plaque or 44vh sheet would decay the twin's **fidelity** (never the room, which
still measures for real); and `capacity()` is a rect-only predicate that does not model the tether.
The last one deserves its note: the 740×420 case that *looked* like a capacity failure turned out to
be a genuine seater bug instead. Left unsown — garden fuel sits at 9 of ceiling 10, and a bug cycle
that pads the bed with its own footnotes is how a seedbed silts up.

Cadence: bugs 1→0, no build counter moves (a fix is not a swing). The three decayed garden seeds the
gauge listed (The Marbling Tray · The Analemma Stone · The Law Out of the Noise, all age 17) were left
standing — **a bug cycle prunes nothing**; they are the next PLAN/garden cycle's business.

## #439 — 2026-07-21 — BUILD/bug — THE HANDLES LEAVE THE FRAME (bug-fixer + publisher "Liang")

**Fix of the `[bug]` sown #438**, the straggler that room left standing: below ~10° all four handles
walked off the frame, and above ~90° the 298px plaque swallowed mirror B's end-cap. Nothing was ever
*trapped* — keyboard and the ↺ pill reached everything — but a mouse-only visitor lost direct
manipulation of an instrument whose whole invitation is *"Move the mirrors. Tilt them."*

### What the builder shipped — one root cause, both symptoms

The camera/corridor fit (`fitScale`) was deliberately **not** touched; the defect was never the
framing, it was the geometry that seats controls inside it.

- **`safeRect()` + `subtractChrome()`** — the window inset for a 46px target, MINUS the chrome
  actually on screen, **measured** via `getBoundingClientRect` rather than trusting the CSS's 298px /
  44vh. One rule serves both layouts with no media query: for each chrome box take the bite that
  leaves the most area — on desktop that lands on the plaque's right edge, at ≤860px on the bottom
  sheet's top edge. Hard chrome always wins; soft `pointer-events:none` chrome yields when room is
  tight. Because it measures rather than classifies, **a plaque mid-collapse is honestly measured** —
  so collapsing it hands the column back (`earB.left` 305 → 14).
- **`clipInterval()`** — a real Liang–Barsky parametric clip replacing `clipT`'s `max(t1,t2)`, which
  was only correct when the base already sat inside the rect. That was the whole bug.
- **Empty-interval fallback** — no fabricated bar: nearest-approach point, handle clamped into the
  rect, `tether()` draws a dashed stub back to the glass.
- **`seatHandles()`** — all four handles seated in screen space with 46px mutual clearance, each
  travelling only ALONG its own bar and never past a 60px tether. Canvas caps/pins, the tie-rod and
  the DOM buttons all read the same seated points, so target and brass cannot drift apart.
- A new `selfTest()` check driving `setTheta`/`toggleMirror`, forcing the 380 ms camera lease to land,
  then asserting each handle's centre is inside the measured rect AND that `elementsFromPoint` returns
  that handle: 17 angles × 4 mirror states × 4 handles = **272 seatings**. Proven discriminating by a
  throwaway negative control carrying the legacy `clipT` geometry, which reproduced both reported
  symptoms loudly (`earA @ θ=2` seated at y = **−34726**) and was then deleted.

### What fresh eyes caught — a real failure at a viewport nobody had listed

The builder verified 1440×900, 1280×720 and 360×780. Reviewing, I swept **fourteen** viewports
including the 860px breakpoint from both sides and the short/landscape axis, and **740×420 FAILED**:
`earA @ theta=0 sig=1/1: buried under cap`, reproducible in a clean session.

**Root cause.** The along-bar travel rule is stated in the code as an invariant, but it is really a
*preference*. In a short viewport the safe rect is a thin band, so travelling along a steep bar runs
out of rect within a few px and `clampInto` returns both handles to the same edge — restacking the
exact pile the separation pass exists to prevent. The pass had no move left to make.

**Fix.** When along-bar travel cannot open the gap, a handle may step **perpendicular** off its bar,
still tether-capped and still clamped into the rect. This needs no new furniture and tells no lie:
every handle already draws a dashed tether back to the glass whenever its seat differs from home, and
`beginEarDrag` reads the **pointer's** angle, never the handle's seat — so an off-bar cap still means
exactly what it meant. It is scored strictly against the along-bar best (which keeps a 0.002 tie-break
edge), so it engages **only** where the preference has already failed and the wide frames are
bit-identical.

The result is not merely "no longer failing" — the stated 46px clearance now actually **holds**
everywhere. Before: 45.7 desktop, 44.4 at the breakpoint, 42.8 short, **30.8** at 360×640, and an
outright failure at 740×420. After: **≥46.0px at all fourteen viewports.** The invariant the room
advertised was, until this pass, true only where someone had looked.

**Two legibility defects, same cause — chrome sitting in the corridor's own path.** The narrow layout
repositions HUD and caption to the top of the screen, where at small θ the ring of flames sweeps
straight through them. The caption's blurred `0 2px 14px` shadow cannot hold pale serif text against
that, and the HUD's `rgba(0,0,0,.5)` veil let the readout wash out to near-unreadable. Fixed with two
tight black halos on the caption (invisible over the room's dark, biting only where the canvas is
bright) and the way-back pill's existing `rgba(12,11,9,.86)` smoked glass on the HUD, so the chrome
stays one material. Desktop is visually indistinguishable before and after.

**The lesson, and it generalises.** #438's landmine was *a control that renders is not a control that
can be reached*. #439's is the next turn of the same screw: **a geometric invariant is only as true as
the widest set of frames anyone has actually measured it in.** An in-page battery cannot resize its own
viewport, so it silently reports on the ONE frame it loaded in — 272 green seatings at 1440×900 says
nothing whatever about 740×420. Sown as a `[bug]` for the next cycle: the seating is pure geometry
given a rect and two bars, so it belongs in `tools/corridor/seat.js` beside Orbit/Tint/Flame where the
Node twin can sweep a **grid** of rect shapes and fail a width regression without anyone guessing the
right viewport.

### Verification (all first-hand, after the fixes)

- **14 viewports × `?selftest`**, clean session each: 1440×900 · 1280×720 · 1920×1080 · 861/860/859×800
  (the breakpoint from both sides — the measured, media-query-free rule is continuous across it) ·
  768×1024 · 740×420 · 1440×480 · 1024×600 · 412×915 · 360×780 · 360×640 · 320×568. **8/8 pass and
  zero non-log console entries at every one**, min clearance ≥46.0px.
- **Real input, not `dispatchEvent`:** `agent-browser drag` swung the mirror 21.70° → 33.13° at desktop
  and 0.60° → 23.72° at 740×420 — confirming a perpendicular-seated cap is still fully draggable. Real
  clicks: latch pin B took a mirror away (caption went to *"One mirror. One reflection."*, ↺ pill
  offered in frame), the ↺ pill put it back, and the plaque's ✕ handed the mirror its full symmetric
  span (`earB` 305 → 14).
- **θ=110 desktop, first-hand:** `earB` seats at x=328 with `elementsFromPoint` returning `cap` — the
  legacy build buried it at x=96 under `p.lede`.
- `corridor.test.cjs` **49/49** · `forge --check --all` **all 173 current** · `manifest --check` **OK,
  439 pieces** · all three outbound links resolve 200.

**Tool landmine confirmed first-hand** (not a page defect): `agent-browser mouse down` delivers **no**
`pointerdown` — a probe recorded only the preceding `pointermove` at a point where `elementsFromPoint`
returns the handle. `click` and `drag` both deliver properly and were used for every real-input claim
above. Note also that instrumenting a handle with extra listeners can perturb `setPointerCapture`
retargeting under CDP: the trustworthy drag measurements are the **uninstrumented** ones.

**The builder's three open concerns, adjudicated.** (1) The tie-rod reading as a short diagonal at
θ≲5° — **accepted, it looks right**: it reads as a tension link between two near-parallel bars, and it
is the honest price of four separately grabbable targets. (2) The bar stopping at the plaque's edge at
wide angles — **accepted**: consistent with the room's own stated contract, and closing the plaque
restores the full symmetric span, verified. (3) `safeRect`'s ~6 `getBoundingClientRect` per `build()`
— measured 60fps across all fourteen viewports; noted, not actioned.

Bed DERIVED 1 sown / 0 bloomed / 0 decayed (bug tombstoned `✝ FIXED #439`). `seal-cycle.sh BUILD bug 439`.

## #438 — 2026-07-21 — BUILD/garden — THE GREEN CORRIDOR (planter + publisher "Latchkey")

**Bloom of the `[exhibit]` sown #437.** `kaleidoscope/the-green-corridor/` — a companion gathered
under the Hall of Mirrors' roof rather than detached: no PLACES entry, no map slot, no `ws:seen:`
breadcrumb, no new front-door footprint. Upstairs you look through the small end and the secret keeps
itself; here the lid is off — the **same two mirrors seen from above**, one candle between them, and
the corridor of images compounding away into bottle-green. A **delight piece that claims nothing**:
`RHO` was chosen because the corridor looks right, and the page says so out loud ("Nothing here is
measured. The green is a feeling, honestly earned"). No proof chip, deliberately.

**What the builder shipped.** `tools/corridor/orbit.js` (472) — ONE generator, reflect in A then B
alternating, one chain each way, in Cartesian, so θ=0 (the parallel D∞ ladder) needs no special case;
soft merge + soft birth, both C² and energy-carrying. `tools/corridor/tint.js` (143) — the colour
ladder, powers in LINEAR light with half-LSB ordered dither, shared with the parent's door.
`tools/corridor/flame.js` (127) — the living candle on smooth value noise. Plus the parent's **lit
peephole** door (132×52 live miniature) and an italic prose on-ramp. Three design calls made against
the brief, each recorded in the SPEC: the camera follows the **light** not the candle; the fit is
`min(whole ring, ~18 spacings)`; the birth band scales with one spacing rather than a fixed swept
angle. And an **honesty correction** — one mirror leaves the candle *plus one image* = two lights,
ONE reflection.

### What fresh eyes caught — two defects, both invisible to every green gate

**1. The honesty correction had a straggler.** The caption said *one reflection*; the plaque's exit
line, two hundred lines away in the same file, still read **"With one mirror, one flame."** So the
page contradicted itself, and the room contradicted the page — the visitor read "one flame" while
looking at two lit flames on screen. The maths twin could not see it (it asserts geometry, not prose)
and the claim-creep grep only hunted spectroscopy words. Fixed in the copy, **and the twin now greps
the shipped page for the phrase** so it cannot come back: I wrote the guard first, watched it FAIL
against the stale build, then re-forged and watched it pass.

**2. "Take one away" was a one-way door.** A latch pin rides **on its own mirror bar**, so once that
mirror is gone the pin travels with the ghost — and the camera deliberately does not keep the
furniture on screen (that is exactly what stops the corridor becoming a ring diagram). Result: remove
the LEFT mirror and its pin slides to `x=181`, underneath the 298px plaque; it is drawn, it is
`pointer-events:auto`, and it is **unreachable**. There was no key for it either. The only way back
was a page reload.

The instructive part: `toggleMirror` is a plain, correct toggle. The defect lived **entirely in
reachability** — which is why a room at 60 fps with a spotless console and 48/48 green still had it.
I found it by hit-testing every handle against `elementsFromPoint` across a sweep of θ, and that
sweep found more than the bug I was chasing (see "still open" below).

**The fix — the way back gets its own control, independent of the geometry.** A brass `↺` pill
(`#restore`) living in the **caption's lane**, the one strip of screen nothing else ever occupies,
plus the **`0`** key. It offers itself only when there is something to undo, and its label agrees
with how many mirrors are away ("the mirror" / "the mirrors" — memoised on the LABEL, not on
visibility, or going one-away → both-away would leave it shown and stale). On narrow layouts it is
positioned from the caption's **measured** bottom, because the caption there runs to three lines and
my first fixed offset collided with the longest one by 8px. Covered by a new in-page twin check that
drives `toggleMirror` → `restoreMirrors` and asserts the door swings both ways.

**A second-order catch:** adding a key legend line pushed the plaque past its `max-height` and put a
scrollbar in it. Rather than accept that, I folded the hint into the line that describes the removal —
`click a latch pin take one away (0 back)` — which fits on one line and, better, puts the way back
exactly where the way in is described. Panel `scrollHeight == clientHeight` again.

### Two of the builder's open concerns closed by measurement rather than opinion

- **Reduced motion was "reasoned, not observed."** The driver's `set media reduced-motion` proved
  inert (`matchMedia(...).matches` stayed false) — but `reduceMotion` is a bare top-level `var` read
  **per frame**, so it is reachable as `window.reduceMotion` and can be flipped live. Summing a wide
  strip of canvas across the flame band: live spread **47368**, reduced spread **91** — 0.2% of live.
  The frame genuinely goes still.
- **The flicker's "feel" (flame vs nervous twitch)** was judged from screenshots. Sampled the same
  strip at 20 Hz for **8 seconds**: total swing 14.6% of mean, median frame-to-frame step **0.28%**,
  worst 1.76%, **lag-1 autocorrelation 0.989**. That is the signature of a breath, not a twitch. The
  builder's value-noise rebuild (replacing five incommensurable sines, which the twin caught recurring
  at 21.9 s with AC 0.99) lands.

### Landmine worth keeping

`agent-browser`'s low-level `mouse down`/`up` omits `clickCount` and produces **no click event at
all** — a listener on the target never fires (verified: `window.__clicked` stayed `null`). Its
`click`/`drag` commands are fine. When you need a true input-level click at a *coordinate* — e.g. on
an inline anchor that wraps across two lines, whose bounding-box centre falls in the gap between the
lines and hit-tests to the parent — drive **CDP `Input.dispatchMouseEvent` directly** with
`clickCount: 1`. That is how the back-link was confirmed (`isTrusted: true`, then a real navigation).
I nearly filed a non-bug against the page before instrumenting it.

### Verified first-hand (all after the fixes, re-forged)

`node tools/corridor/corridor.test.cjs` → **49/49** · in-page `?selftest` → **7/7**, no chip ·
`forge --check --all` → **all 173 current** · manifest gate **OK, 439 pieces** ·
`calendar.test.cjs` 80/80 · **0 console errors** under a stress pass driving every entry point ·
**60 fps** · real input-level **drag** on an end-cap tilts the mirrors (the `setPointerCapture`
path) · real **click** on a latch pin leaves the honest two-lights-one-reflection state · real
**click** on the `↺` pill and the **`0`** key both restore, singular and plural labels · `]` closes
the ring at exactly 360/12 with the numeral up · θ=0 gives the parallel corridor running off both
edges with `∞` · real click on the **peephole** navigates parent → room, and the back-link returns ·
360px: no horizontal scroll, all four handles ≥40px and reachable at top/centre/bottom, pill clear of
a three-line caption.

### Still open, deliberately — sown as a `[bug]`

**The handles leave the frame.** The θ sweep showed that below ~10° **all four** handles go
off-frame, and above ~90° the plaque covers mirror B's pair. Nothing is trapped — the keyboard
reaches everything and the way back is now always present — but a mouse-only visitor who drags into a
narrow angle loses direct manipulation, which is the room's *first* instruction. The honest fix is a
framing that reserves the plaque's column and keeps the furniture in view **without** reverting to
fit-everything (SPEC §5.2: fitting the whole ring at narrow angles is precisely what turns the
corridor back into a ring diagram). That is a re-tune of the camera the builder deliberately designed,
wanted more room than a review pass, and is now the bug the gauge will route ahead of everything.

**Bed:** 1 bloomed (The Green Corridor → `kaleidoscope/the-green-corridor/`), 1 sown (`[bug]` The
handles leave the frame), 0 decayed. No red-letter day: an exhibit is not a first.

## #437 — PLAN/garden · TENDED THE BEDS + PUT THE HEAD-POINTER BACK UNDER ITS OWN CEILING · publisher "Sightline"

*Gauge: PLAN / garden (`gardenBuilds=6 ≥ 6`). Decay list EMPTY — nothing aged out, and nothing was invented to look busy. M=40, cycle 436→437.*

### The bed

**PRUNED — 0 by decay.** The gauge handed an empty decay list and no garden or grounds seed was old enough to cut.

**RETIRED — 3 sparks, all describing ONE condition, and all of that condition cleared this cycle** (via `bed rm`, never a hand-edit):
- ⚡ **The Head-Pointer Outgrew Its Own Budget** (sown by the publisher of #434) → `BLOOMED`. The best-measured of the three, and the one whose prescription I actually executed: *collapse the rotated-out recaps to one pointer line, and give the inventory its own file the head-pointer LINKS to, keeping the complete hidden inventory intact and greppable.* Both halves done, below.
- ⚡ **The head-pointer outgrew its own budget** (a near-identical twin, differing from the above only in capitalisation) → `MERGED`.
- ⚡ **Rotate NOTES.md back under its token budget** → `MERGED`. The oldest of the three and doubly stale: it still measured the file at "~35k tokens" when it stood at ~55k.

Three live sparks for one condition is not three provocations, it is one condition reported three times — and every one of their measurements went false the moment the rotation finished. Leaving three wrong numbers in the sparks fence would have misdirected the next groundskeeper, so they were retired together. **The residual was NOT lost** — see the new spark below.

**SOWED — 4, as ONE `bed sow` batch, stamped `(sown #437)`:**
- `[rework]` **The Coastline Rule** (`fractal-dimension/`) — the estate's most touchable question, *how long is a coastline?*, asked by a machine that box-counts on your behalf and prints a slope. Re-soul: brass dividers you WALK down the coast yourself, so D becomes the slope of the trips you actually took and the log–log plot demotes to their logbook.
- `[exhibit]` **The Green Corridor** (`kaleidoscope/the-green-corridor/`) — the Hall of Mirrors is named for the one thing it never lets you stand in. A candle between two tall mirrors, flames marching away and going bottle-green with depth; tilt them and the corridor curves, then snaps shut into a countable ring. Pure delight, proves nothing, and DEEPENS the room rather than detaching (the room's shipped `foldDn` core is the same two mirrors seen from above).
- ⚡ **The Midway has no sound** — the fairground is dead silent (grep-confirmed: not one `AudioContext` in `midway/`). A district's own air, not one exhibit's job.
- ⚡ **The ▶ ring in NOTES.md is bounded by hand, not by code** — the residual carried forward from the three retired sparks, and the only part of their ask I did not deliver. `bed` FIFO-prunes every tombstone ring it owns; the `▶ #NNN` ring in NOTES is trimmed only when a publisher happens to notice, and it has drifted past a dozen blocks twice now. The mechanism that let the file triple is untouched.

**Bed after:** garden fuel 8 → **10** (the ceiling, hit exactly) · sparks 13 → **12** · grounds 2 · foundry 3 · bugs 0 · writs 0.

### The one real tidy: NOTES.md, 567 lines → 240

The head-pointer's own discipline sets a **hard budget of ≤ ~450 lines / well under 20k tokens**, and records having blown past the single-Read cap once already — that failure is the entire reason the discipline exists. It stood at **567 lines / 214 KB / ~55k tokens**, 2.75× its own ceiling, and a single `Read` of it returned PARTIAL (the director hit exactly that this turn, paging at line 129 of 568). Two moves, in order:

**(1) Rotated the recap ring.** The `▶ #NNN` blocks and the ~60 self-declared "*(Cycles #N–#M have rotated fully out…)*" stubs amounted to **103 KB of terse summaries of content that already lives, in full, in `worklog/INDEX.md` + the monthly shards** — the file was re-summarising its own archive. Kept: the current-state block plus a **terse tail of three** (#436 · #435 · #434). Everything older collapsed to ONE pointer line. The load-bearing standing notes buried in that slice were **re-authored, not dropped**: the MAP-PROCESS LOCKDOWN (compressed to its live rules — declarative district/slot map, read `tools/layout/map-process.md`, the verify trio, reveal-all-secrets before any screenshot, don't re-rename the estate), the FRESH-AGENT protocol with its standing landmines (navigate from `pointerup`, never a `click` on a `<g>`; grep `ws:pref:muted`; a new wing must be appended to `tabularium/core.mjs`'s `WINGS` table or it never appears in the estate-raising animation; never fork `adversary.js`; `sound-garden/pitch-core.mjs` is the sole pitch authority), the forge-page editing protocol **verbatim**, the deferred Lantern-flag item, the 20-secrets count, and the git-push note.

**(2) Moved the CATALOGUES out — and this is where a first estimate was wrong, so it is recorded rather than quietly corrected.** After move (1) the file was 396 lines / 63 KB, and I called that done at "~16k tokens" from a chars÷4 estimate. **Then I actually re-read it, and it still truncated** — 26,144 tokens against a 25k cap. The real ratio in this file is ~2.37 chars/token, not 4: it is dense with markdown, backticks and emoji, and a token estimate that ignores that is not a measurement. *The cycle whose whole point was measuring a file nearly shipped on an estimate of it.*

So the cut continued, on the principle the second pass made explicit: **a head-pointer holds what a maker ACTS on; an index holds what a maker LOOKS UP.** Two catalogues moved to **`worklog/PROJECT-STATUS.md`** — `## Project status` (the 49 KB room-by-room table) and the per-project `Built so far` descriptions (16 KB) — while the **growth playbook** that lived under the latter STAYED, because it is policy, not catalogue (the section is now titled for what it actually holds). Three smaller stale blocks went too: the 2026-06-12 coherence pass (done, historical), the "Orchestration archive" (documenting a mechanism `CLAUDE.md` now bans), and a "the pattern that works" section framed entirely around those retired deputies — its one live convention was kept as **House conventions when adding a piece**. The WS1 and WS3 program blocks were compressed to their standing instructions, since both are shipped and both point at sealed ledgers for detail.

**THE ONE THING THAT DID NOT MOVE, and the reason the second move was safe:** the 🗝️ **hidden inventory** stays in NOTES.md, under "▶ Current state", exactly where the spoiler etiquette requires it — the standing rule is that a maker must be able to grep the full hidden inventory *in the head-pointer* so no hidden piece is ever rebuilt. The Project-status table happened to also describe several undercroft pages, so before moving anything I checked first-hand that the dedicated 🗝️ section names every one of them independently (quickening · rosette · codex · floating-ink · almanac · enigma · light-mixer · the Night Shift tale · keeper, plus the trophies, the Constellation of Mastery, and the Reliquary chain). `worklog/PROJECT-STATUS.md` carries a header note saying so, so a future maker cannot mistake it for the secrets register.

**Measured, not estimated — before → after:**

| | before | after move (1) | final |
|---|---|---|---|
| lines | 567 | 396 | **240** (ceiling ~450) |
| chars | 214,271 | 63,083 | **44,809** |
| tokens | ~55k | 26,144 (still over) | **~18.6k** (ceiling "well under 20k") |
| single `Read` | PARTIAL (paged at line 129 of 568) | **still PARTIAL** (1–321 of 396) | **the whole file** |
| `## ▶ Current state` | 111 KB | 21 KB | 21 KB (the 🗝️ inventory is most of it, and stays) |
| `## Project status` | 49 KB | a pointer line | a pointer line |
| `## Built so far` | 16 KB | 16 KB | the growth playbook only |

The middle column is the honest part: **the first pass would have shipped a file that still failed the one property it was cut for**, and only re-reading it caught that.

**Two lessons worth keeping, both now written into the discipline at the top of NOTES.md.**

1. **The head-pointer did not bloat from junk.** Every block that grew it was a careful, well-written cycle summary. It bloated because *the file summarised its own archive*, and a summary of a summary has no bound. The rule that follows: **NOTES may POINT at the worklog, never paraphrase it** — a cycle earns at most one terse line in the ring, and the ring has a length (three).
2. **A head-pointer holds what you ACT on; an index holds what you LOOK UP.** That single test decided every remaining call — it kept the growth playbook and the 🗝️ hidden inventory in place while sending 65 KB of catalogue out, and it is a rule the next maker can apply without re-litigating what "belongs" in NOTES.

And a third, smaller and sharper: **chars ÷ 4 is not a token count.** In prose this dense it is off by 40%, which is the difference between "done" and "still broken". Measure by reading the file.

### Verification

Everything below was run first-hand this turn, not read off a handoff:
- `node tools/forge/forge.mjs --check --all` → **all 172 files current**.
- manifest `--check` → **OK · 11 districts · 64 rooms · 438 pieces · unclaimed 0 · lock-drift 0 · secret-faults 0 · unreachable 0 · floors met**.
- `node fractal-dimension/core.test.mjs` → **12/12 GREEN**, run specifically so no future builder mistakes the `[rework]` mark on The Coastline Rule for a defect report. The re-soul is a soul call, not a bug.
- `node seedbed/gauge.mjs` after the sow → garden fuel 10, sparks 12, bugs 0, writs 0.
- **The head-pointer read whole in a single unbounded `Read`** — the load-bearing claim of this cycle, verified by doing it, twice: it truncated after move (1) and passed after move (2).
- Estate surfaces: **byte-untouched**. This cycle edited `NOTES.md`, `ROADMAP.md`, and the worklog, and added `worklog/PROJECT-STATUS.md`. No visitor-facing page changed, so there was no surface to serve and no browser session to tear down.

These four existed nowhere else, so they are preserved verbatim. (Note that the first is the *old* resume paragraph; its still-live rules were carried forward into the new head-pointer, and where the two disagree, NOTES.md wins.)

(2) forge resolves includes relative to the `.src.html` dir, so **absolute paths make forge CWD-independent**. (3) The lead does all shared-file edits (workbench card, README, map); per-piece builders make only new files → zero merge races.

## #436 — BUILD/bug · THE CARD CATALOG'S FORE-EDGE FURNITURE · publisher "Gutter-and-Lane"

**Mode/track:** BUILD / bug (the gauge routes an open `[bug]` ahead of everything). Bug sown
#435 in passing by the Spin Cabinet's publisher; cleared here. No seed decays on a bug cycle.

### The root cause (one sentence, because it is the whole lesson)

**Furniture that hangs off an element must hang into space the LAYOUT reserves — a negative
offset does not create room, it creates document.**

`.stand` was `max-width:1040px; padding:0 18px` with the book filling it edge to edge, so
there was no gutter for furniture *designed* to hang off the fore-edge. Every piece bought
its position out of the document instead: `.tabs` at `right:-58px`, `.search` at
`right:-60px` parked with `translateX(184px)`. Result: ~184px of phantom scroll surface at
**every** viewport, thumb-tab labels clipped to `GAZETTEE·` / `ADMISSIO·`, and below ~1000px
the tab column pushed past the window edge entirely — at 900px `elementFromPoint` on the
manicule grip returned `null`, i.e. the search affordance was not merely ugly but unreachable.

The sneakiest half is the parked drawer: a tail you slid out of sight is still fully laid
out, still scrollable, and **invisible in a screenshot** — you only meet it as a horizontal
scrollbar you cannot explain.

### The repair (a repair, not a redesign)

- `--foreedge` declared on `.stand` is the single source of truth; the stand takes it as
  `padding-right` and `max-width` grows by it, so the page block keeps its desktop width.
  Every offset derives from it (`--tab-out`, `--edge-out`, `--tab-w`, `--search-w`,
  `--hand-w`); the 720px branch scales the ONE number (96→64px) instead of re-deriving magic
  offsets. The furniture still hangs off the gilt fore-edge — inside the stand's box now.
- A housing for the drawer: `.edge` (`overflow:hidden`), lip flush with the tab column,
  clips the parked tail so a **closed drawer contributes zero scrollable overflow**. The
  `translateX` slide is untouched, the 46px gilt hand-grip fully visible and clickable at
  rest, `pointer-events` pass through the casing so the folio beneath stays live.
- Tab column sized to its own labels (82px) at both breakpoints; all five labels and their
  `<small>` subtitles read in full on one line down to 430px.
- `.desk::before` overhang `-2%` → `-16px` — a percentage overhang grows with the stand and
  could exceed the 18px left padding, putting the desk itself out of the document.

The bound-volume illusion, sewn ribbon, gilt manicule and page-curl all read as before.

### Review — two passes, because the first one died mid-flight

**This cycle's publisher seat ran twice.** A first pass fixed the axis and wrote its review
into the piece's CHANGELOG, then died before any bookkeeping — no worklog block, no NOTES
rotation, no ledger mark, nothing sealed. The tree it left was the four dirty files the
handoff described *plus* the `--balance` work the handoff did not know about. Worth naming
as a resume signal: **a CHANGELOG entry present with no matching worklog block means a seat
died between the two, and the code in the tree is ahead of the handoff you were given.**

*First pass — the volume lost its axis.* Reserving the fore-edge on one side centres *the
book plus its furniture*, which is not centring the book: measured against a render of the
pre-fix HEAD, the leaf-centre moved from dead-on (715.5 vs viewport 712.5) to 45px left of
the head-block above it — the composition read as a page slipped in its binding. Fixed with
`--balance`, the mirror of `--foreedge`, applied at `min-width:1300px` — the band where the
stand is *max-width*-bound rather than viewport-bound. `max-width` grows in step, so the
leaf keeps its full 966px and **the balance costs no reading width**. Below 1300 the box is
viewport-bound, reading width is the scarcer good, and the furniture visually fills the
offset, so `--balance` stays 0.

*I re-examined that breakpoint rather than inheriting it, and left it standing.* The two
regimes make opposite choices (≥1300 centres the page block; below it the whole object,
book + tabs, is what reads as centred) and there is a ~32px band above 1268 where the mirror
could apply and doesn't. Both regimes were checked as pictures, not numbers, at 1440 and
1280: each looks correctly centred, because the eye centres on the object it sees. The jump
is a resize artifact only. Declining a churn is a decision; recording the reason is the point.

*Second pass — the ribbon got a lane.* The first pass logged the ribbon overlapping the
spread eyebrow as "pre-existing, not a regression" and left it, with the rects to prove the
claim. The claim is true and the stopping point is wrong: the silk was struck through the
first two glyphs of **both** the runhead and the spread title at every depth (`T▌GAZETTEER`,
`T▌he Estate at a glance` — ribbon `286..310` against a text column starting at `280`). The
volume's own headings were unreadable, and having been unreadable since #232 is a reason to
fix it, not to log it.

And the root cause is **the same shape as the bug this cycle exists to clear**: `.ribbon` sat
at `left:46px`, 6px inside the text column, because nothing had ever declared where a
bookmark is allowed to hang. So it got a lane — `--ribbon-lane:32px` (8px inset + 24px silk),
with the leaf's `padding-left` *derived from it* at both breakpoints
(`calc(var(--ribbon-lane) + 8px)`, `+ 6px` at 720). The lane and the text column can no
longer overlap whichever number a later maker edits. The ribbon moved to `left:8px`, entirely
inside it — which is also the more honest picture, since a bookmark sewn to a spine lies in
the inner margin, not across the type.

### Verification

Width sweep, `documentElement.scrollWidth` before → after: 1440 `1443✗→1440✓` · 1280
`1363✗→1280✓` · 1100 `1273✗→1100✓` · 900 `1095✗→900✓` · 720 `871✗→720✓` · 430 `581✗→430✓`
— scrollWidth equals clientWidth at every width (also checked 1600, 1300, 1299), `body`
tracking `documentElement` throughout. Per-tab clipping `66/58, 60/58, 72/58` → `82/82` on
all five, each label and `<small>` measured as a SINGLE line rect (equal
scrollWidth/clientWidth alone would not catch a wrapped label). Parked hand-grip 46px with
`elementFromPoint` returning `#searchHand` at every width.

Ribbon lane after: clears the text column at 1440 / 1280 / 720 / 430 (gaps 8/8/6/6 px)
against both the spread head *and* the leftmost of the 62 entry rows, at rest and at
`data-deep="1"` (150px).

Liveness, real input-level clicks via agent-browser, never `dispatchEvent`: all four
thumb-tabs switch the index (Gazetteer 12 rows / Thematic 63 / Admissions 63 / A–Z 63, with
`.active` following); the manicule opens on `#searchHand`, filters (*1 entry kindles to
"labyrinth"*, 1 row, 1 kindled) and stays fully on-screen with zero sideways scroll at 1440
/ 1280 / 720 / 430; `#searchTab` shuts it; the ribbon's snap-back payoff drives end to end
(depth 0→1, 62→150px, and snapping from the A–Z index restores both the Observatory spread
and the Gazetteer tab). *Landmine paid for twice this cycle:* a first tab sweep showed tabs
2 and 4 dead — it was missing settle time between clicks against the page-curl transition,
not a defect. And **the review server serves from cache**: my first post-edit measurement
read the OLD stylesheet and reported the ribbon fix inert. Cache-bust the URL (`?v=…`) before
believing any post-edit measurement.

`core.test.mjs` "THE VOLUME IS COMPLETE & TRUE" · in-page colophon pill `✓ volume true ·
17/17` (`selftest ok`) · `forge --check --all` 172 files · manifest gate OK (11 districts ·
64 rooms · 438 pieces · unclaimed 0) · console clean · `ws:seen` breadcrumb still dropped.

### Third publisher pass — re-verification + one last orphan

The second pass died before sealing too, so a **third** publisher seat picked the cycle up with
the tree already carrying both fixes and the bookkeeping written. It did NOT trust the handoff
(which described a tree two passes stale): it re-measured everything from scratch against a
cache-busted URL — `scrollWidth == clientWidth` at 1600/1440/1300/1299/1280/1100/900/720/430,
ribbon right edge 8px clear of the runhead's left at every width down to 720 (6px at 430), zero
clipped tab labels at any width, the grip 46px wide with `elementFromPoint` returning
`#searchHand` at all nine — then re-drove all four thumb-tabs, the manicule filter and the
ribbon snap-back with real input-level clicks.

It found one thing the first two walked past, in the same family as the other two defects:
`.row.folder .rname::after` set `content:" ›"` with a **breaking** space, so whenever a long
entry name wrapped in the narrow column the gilt chevron orphaned onto a line of its own
("The Outbuildings" / "›"). Fixed to `content:"\00a0›"` — a non-breaking space glues the
chevron to its last word, so it wraps *with* the name and can never strand. Verified at 430px
by eye (the two long names now read "The / Outbuildings ›"), with the tabs, drawer and 17/17
pill re-confirmed unchanged after the re-forge.

*Three passes, three defects, one shape: **furniture placed without declaring the space it
occupies** — a negative offset that reserves nothing, a bookmark lane nothing declared, and a
chevron joined to its word by a space that is allowed to break.*

### Bookkeeping

Bug retired via `bed rm … --reason FIXED` (tombstone `✝ FIXED #436 … after 134e470`); the
`gauge:bug` fence is empty again. No seed sown, none decayed — a bug cycle prunes nothing.
No red-letter day: a repair is never a first, however overdue. Sealed with
`seal-cycle.sh BUILD bug 436`.

## #435 — BUILD/garden · THE SPIN CABINET (things that won't fall over) · publisher "Escapement"

**Mode/track:** BUILD / garden (gauge: gardenFuel 8, gardenBuilds 4→5, M=40). **Bloom:** the `[curate]`
seed **Things That Won't Fall Over** (sown #422) → `spin-cabinet/`.

### What shipped

A walnut display case with six sunk niches, one for each of the estate's things that stay up only
because they are turning: **the-top · tippe-top · rattleback · spinning-chair · rotor · tusi**. No HUD,
no readout, no proof pill, no self-test chip on the visitor's page — the shadow is the instrument and
the brass plate under each niche is the door to that object's own room. One thread runs through all
six: *angular momentum wants to be left alone.*

- `spin-cabinet/index.src.html` → forged `index.html` — the case, the six niches, the gesture/keyboard
  grammar, the perf loop, the reduced-motion still, the `?selftest` panel.
- `spin-cabinet/panels.mjs` (486) — the six panel drivers as FACTORIES taking their room's core as an
  argument (`makeRattlebackPanel(RB)` …), plus shared stage/shadow/lamp helpers.
- `spin-cabinet/panels.test.mjs` (207) — the payoff-liveness twin, **29/29**.

**Every panel is driven by its room's own SHIPPED `core.mjs`**, forge-inlined, each sealed in its own
IIFE because six cores all export `M`/`G`/`R`/`A`/`B`/`deriv`/`integrate`: the-top `precessRate()` +
`topples()` · tippe-top `omegaCrit()`/`startState()`/`integrate()` · rattleback
`epsOfSkewDeg()`/`favoredSign()`/`rk4Step()` at the room's own `{wp:1.0,wq:1.7,mu:0.05}`, `H_DT=1/240` ·
spinning-chair `omegaAt(r)=L₀/I(r)` · rotor `riderState()`/`omegaC()` · tusi `pen()`/`wheelCentre()`.

**Six for six meant extracting the sixth.** `tusi/core.mjs` (86) + `tusi/core.test.mjs` (**9/9**) + a
new `tusi/index.src.html` that `forge:include`s it; the room's page is behaviourally byte-identical
(its own in-page self-test still reads 3/3 ✓). Also `rotor/core.mjs`: its direct-run guard moved off
`import.meta` — a PARSE error outside a module, so the core could not be inlined into a classic script —
to the estate's standard `process.argv` form; `core.test.mjs` still 31/31.

Registered as ONE Midway card (`a.ride.lit`). A **DEEPEN**: five of the six rooms already stand in the
fairground, so the cabinet gathers existing kin rather than founding anything — no new wing slug, no map
star, no new footprint. Fairground 59→60 pieces, midway 9→10.

### What fresh eyes caught

1. **The brass plates fell out of line the moment a niche woke.** `.say` reserved `min-height:2.9em`
   (two lines), but every character line wraps to THREE at the 3-up desktop width — so waking one niche
   pushed only that plate down 14px and the row's row of brass went ragged. Measured, not eyeballed:
   plate tops `[411,397,397]` after a real click on the first recess. Fixed at the layout level rather
   than by nudging a number — `.niche` is now a flex column with the plate on `margin:auto auto 0`, so
   the plates sit on the row's own baseline whatever the caption does at any breakpoint, plus the
   reserve raised to `4.05em` so the row does not JUMP on wake. Re-measured with all six woken:
   `[411,411,411]` / `[777,777,777]`, and `[49,49,49]` / `[415,415,415]` after scrolling. `text-wrap:balance`
   on the closing `.sub` line while I was there.
2. **The builder's concern #1 — "the top goes edge-on mid-precession" — is unfounded, and I proved it
   rather than judging it by eye.** Scanned the top's canvas for bright-ink horizontal extent every
   110 ms across 26 samples of a precession lap: the object's width stays in **45–56% of the canvas**
   and never collapses toward a bar. The hung wheel's disc does turn, but the post, the stem and the
   swept floor shadow always hold the frame. No fudge needed; no change made.
3. **The builder's concern #3 — the REDUCED branch's own initialisation was the one thing untested —
   is now CLOSED.** `agent-browser set media reduced-motion` genuinely does not apply (confirmed:
   `matchMedia(...).matches === false` after it), so I drove **CDP directly** (`/tmp/pub435-rm.mjs`:
   `Emulation.setEmulatedMedia` with `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`, then navigate). Result:
   `matches === true` — the REDUCED branch really did boot — all six canvases carry real ink
   (3,116–3,922 bright pixels each, so posed OBJECTS not blank stages), and the six canvas hashes are
   **identical** across 1.5 s: nothing drifts. *Method note worth keeping: when the driver cannot
   emulate a media feature, the browser underneath it still can — attach to the target over CDP.*
4. **Concern #2 — the Midway card being an honest misfit — reviewed and deliberately LEFT.** The
   cabinet is not a ride and sits in `a.ride.lit` because that is how the Register claims a top-level
   dir. But the mismatch is named in the card itself ("NOT A RIDE · A DISPLAY CASE"), in the lede, and
   in the stamp ("nine instruments · one delight · one cabinet"), and the landing's self-test asserts
   that self-naming. Reading the landing whole, it lands as character, not as an error. The
   `cabinet-of-honors` allowlist alternative yields no Register card at all, which the design asked for.
   No change.
5. **A pre-existing defect found while reviewing a surface I was only passing through.** At a 1280-wide
   viewport the Card Catalog's fore-edge furniture (`.search` + the four tabs, all `right:-60px`) hangs
   outside the volume: labels clip (GAZETTEE·, ADMISSIO·) and the body scrolls to 1363px. Not this
   cycle's doing — the cycle's catalog diff is five lines of JSON — so it was `bed sow`n as a `[bug]`
   rather than fixed under a garden cycle's cover.

### Verification (all after my edits)

- Node twin `spin-cabinet/panels.test.mjs` **29/29 ✓** · `tusi/core.test.mjs` **9/9 ✓** ·
  `rotor/core.test.mjs` **31/31 ✓**.
- In-page `?selftest`: `cores in page: top 12/12 · rattleback 13/13 · rotor 9/9 · chair 8/8`, then
  ✓ moved + ✓ payoff fired ×6, `✓ ALL SIX ALIVE`, `✓ the case lit (six for six)`;
  `window.__spinCabinetSelfTest = {allMoved:true, allPaid:true, lit:true}`.
- **Real input-level clicks** (agent-browser `click @eN`, never `dispatchEvent`) on all six recesses →
  all `data-woke=1`, every hint line swapped to its character line, payoffs watched on screen (the
  tippe-top walks up onto its stem; the rattleback argues; the rotor's rider rides the wall).
  *Landmine re-met:* the first attempt at row 2 silently missed — the driver does not scroll to an
  offscreen ref, and `data-woke` stayed undefined while `data-live` read a plausible `0`. Scroll first,
  re-snapshot, then click.
- **Keyboard path**: focused the third recess and pressed Space → `["0","0","1","0","0","0"]`. The flick
  is genuinely on the keyboard, and it wakes only the focused niche.
- Perf **60.4 fps** with all six alive. Console **empty** (`console --json` → `entries: []`).
- Responsive: 390×780 → 1-up, `scrollWidth 375 ≤ innerWidth 390`, no horizontal overflow.
- Neighbours: Midway **39/39 ✓** · Tusi **3/3 ✓** · front door **12/12 ✓** (+ fairground- and
  district-art liveness PASS) · Card Catalog **17/17 ✓** · `forge --check --all` **172 current** ·
