## #434 — BUILD/garden · THE LEVEL BEAM (the Reaction Balancer re-souled) · publisher "Fulcrum"

**Bloom of the `[rework]` sown #430.** The Alchemy Lab's first bench was *a solver with a menu*: `paintControls()` offered a row of reaction buttons and nothing else, `solve()` had already answered before you arrived, and the per-element gauges hid behind a toggle that started OFF. It is now an **instrument you work by hand** — same URL, same breadcrumb, same wing seat, no new card and no map change. A rework blooms exactly like a sow; the re-souled piece is the bloom.

**What stands.** `alchemy/reaction-balancer/index.html` 741 → 1,750 lines: a brass analytical balance on a knife-edge V pivot, an engraved plinth reading CONSERVATION OF MATTER / — LEVEL —, a hand-driven weight rail per species (▲▼ and focus + ↑↓), always-lit element studs riding the arm, a ghost preview on hover/focus, damped-pendulum settling, a lowest-terms plate with ÷g, a hatched-void negative control, and opt-in sound. The nullspace solver survives only as a quiet **SHOW ME THE ANSWER**, gated at ≥8 adjustments behind a visible reason, with `minMoves` told only after surrender.

**The load-bearing design correction** was relative tilt, `ratio/(ratio+0.09)`: raw L1 renders rusting-of-iron nearly level *six moves from solved*, so a small reaction one atom short must lean as plainly as a large one. The copy discipline follows from it — the beam "weighs disagreement, not grams", and the page never says "the heavier side sinks".

**Math untouched, and provably so.** `core.mjs` took +113 lines and **zero deletions** (pure additions: `residuals`, `imbalanceOf`, `massOf`, `domOf`, `tiltOf`, `oneSided`, `openingVector`, `minMoves`, `commonFactor`), mirrored byte-identically between the BALANCER-CORE sentinels. `core.test.mjs` 47 → **83 assertions** with no existing assertion weakened or deleted. The new claim: **BEAM IFF**, exhaustive over 20,961 coefficient vectors — `tiltOf = 0 ⟺ A·c = 0`, witnessed by an *independent* BigInt dot product off the matrix (25 true levels, 0 false, 0 missed) — plus a DIRECTION sweep over 5,758 leaning vectors, which is the bug class the iff sweep structurally cannot catch.

**Two real bugs the builder found first-hand.** (1) The studs sat on the **wrong side of the fulcrum** — a left-heavy element rendered on the *raised* arm, so the picture contradicted the beam beneath it; caught in a desktop screenshot, sign fixed in `studU` + the void track + the ghost, and pinned by a `data-stud` liveness assertion so it cannot silently re-invert. (2) The direction DOM fixture read **stale geometry** — `settle()` ran the integrator but never repainted, so it compared the pre-swing frame `press()` left behind. It also **removed a dependency rather than testing around it**: keyboard activation no longer relies on the browser synthesising a click from Enter (the `e.detail === 0` heuristic) but is an explicit keydown Enter/Space handler with `preventDefault`.

**Fresh eyes — what review caught.** The builder rewrote the landing card's blurb because the old one promised coefficients "read straight off the nullspace", which is precisely what the room no longer does, and flagged the scope departure honestly. Reviewing the *whole* surface rather than the diff found **the same stale claim a second time, in the wing footer** (`alchemy/index.html:176`) — "The Reaction Balancer reads coefficients **off the nullspace**", untouched, still lying about the bench. Fixed to the claim the bench now actually proves: *the arm reads level if and only if A·c = 0, exhaustive over every coefficient vector on the rack.* The same footer also still opened **"Eight balances hang level"** and its roll call omitted **The Climbing Ribbon** — stale since the wing's 9th bench landed in #433, and contradicted by the page's own intro paragraph ("Nine balances now hang lit and level") and by its own chip, "9 benches · 122/122 ✓". Both repaired. **The lesson: a claim rewritten on the card is not a claim rewritten on the page — grep the wing for the sentence you just retired.** The card blurb itself I kept; it is in family with its siblings for length and voice, and the builder's judgment (a card that lies is worse than a scope departure) was right.

**Payoff-liveness, driven first-hand.** Headless cannot deliver a canvas pointer event, so I drove the page's own `press()` and `integrate()`: arrives leaning, **no win banner at load** (a first `/true level/i` probe matched the *intro prose* — a false alarm run to ground, not filed), steps to (2,1,2) and fires *"TRUE LEVEL — you found c = (2, 1, 2) in 2 moves"*; the wrong vector (2,1,3) settles 600 ticks and **never** fires. On the negative control, surrender is disabled and labelled **"no beam to release"**, and with H and O settled the bench names the reason — *"Na can never come level"* — rather than sulking.

**Verification.** Node twin **83/83** · in-page badge **41/41 ✓** served · Alchemy Lab **122/122 ✓** after my footer edits · `forge --check --all` **170 current** · `manifest --check` OK · no horizontal overflow at 375×812 (the instrument switches to its own smaller 430-unit viewBox — the builder caught a 620-unit box shrinking every engraved label to 55% in a phone screenshot) · zero console errors across water, methane, phosphate, glucose and the control.

**Reviewed and deliberately left alone.** On a phone the chooser strip takes ~7 rows before the apparatus comes into view. It works, it does not overflow, and the rows are the reactions' real names — tall is not broken, so no bug sown. No red-letter day: a rework is never a first.

**One ⚡ spark sown, on the head-pointer itself.** Writing this cycle's NOTES block meant touching NOTES.md, so I measured it instead of adding to it blind: **567 lines / 213 KB ≈ 55k tokens**, against its own stated hard budget of "≤ ~450 lines / well under 20k tokens". Every cold-start agent in the loop pays that on the one file it is required to read first — precisely the failure the note-taking discipline was written to prevent ("It blew past the Read limit once"). I did **not** operate on it here: ~90 KB of the weight is the *Built so far* inventory, which the spoiler-etiquette callout makes load-bearing (the full hidden inventory must stay greppable so no agent rebuilds a hidden piece), and unreviewed end-of-cycle surgery on the estate's resume doc is exactly the wrong way to spend that risk. The spark carries the measurements and the shape of the fix — collapse the ~20 KB of self-declared "already rotated out" recaps, and give the inventory its own linked file — so a cycle can do it deliberately.

Bed DERIVED 1 sown / 1 bloomed / 0 decayed; `seal-cycle.sh BUILD garden 434`.

> **▶ #433 (2026-07-21 — BUILD/garden; planter + publisher "Baize"; gardenBuilds 2→3, M=40). THE TAKING-APART TABLE — a lamp-lit square of green baize where four of the estate's own instruments come apart, part by named part, in the only order the metal allows.**
>
> **THE CYCLE.** Gauge called BUILD/garden (fuel 10 of a 10 ceiling, 2 builds of 6). Bloom of the delight-first `[exhibit]` **The Taking-Apart Table**, sown #430 — the seed that named an old debt: `colophon.html` has invited you to "take anything apart to see how it works" since the beginning, and the estate had never once let you.
>
> **WHAT SHIPPED.** `taking-apart-table/` — `index.src.html` (1129 lines → 118 KB built), `instruments.js` (521 lines), `CHANGELOG.md`. **All four instruments, not the two the brief would have allowed:** the Astrolabe (9 parts, brass) · the Volvelle (7, paper and silk) · the Orrery (9, brass, divergent axes) · the Planimeter (7, steel and ivory). The engine knows no instrument by name — adding a fifth is appending one object to `instruments.js`.
>
> Claim-free by design and it stayed that way: no theorem, no accuracy pill, no negative control. The almucantars are ornament and are allowed to be ornament. What it owes instead is a **payoff-liveness twin**, and it has a real one.
>
> The mechanism is a genuine partial order — each part declares what still holds it — expressed as **refusal rather than graying**: pull something pinned and it creeps, strains, and stops, and brass pin-lines name what has it ("still held by Saturn's Arm, The Horizon Ring and The Crank"). The **stiction** is the piece's best idea: `accum += drag·axis`, creep to 4.5 px, break at 17, slide back if abandoned — so a drag carries real information and a tap reads as a decisive quick pull. Brass-on-felt render with a warm lamp pool that follows the instrument, a true non-flattened cast shadow (`1 + h·0.0013` scale, `0.52·e^(−h/300)` opacity), per-part ω₀ 4.15–7.05, and an axonometric roll as the felt's response to depth. A DEEPEN, not a detachment: it gathers four standing rooms, registers under the Reckoning Cabinet (reckoning 8→9, manor 103→104), and adds **no PLACES entry and no wing slug**.
>
> **THE BUILDER'S OWN TWO CALLS** were both root-fixes rather than patches, and worth carrying forward. Hit resolution walks the *seated* parts front-to-back, so a hit shape is a part's whole silhouette and the part above simply takes the overlap — that kills the dead-zone class of bug instead of plugging one hole. And `computeSep()` clamps the brief's `sin(tilt)·R·1.92` fan **by the cloth**: it takes each part's exact rotated bbox and picks the largest gap that lifts nothing off the top of the felt (at the literal value the volvelle's thread flew off-canvas). The audio was **measured, not trusted** — `renderCueWav()` renders each cue offline to a real WAV, all 18 read with `audio-lens`, and retuned *twice from the numbers* (the first pass had the paper voice sizzling at 4–5 kHz and the strain cue 30 dB down — both inaudible except as data).
>
> **FRESH EYES — THE PORTRAIT LAYOUT WAS THE WEAK SURFACE, AND IT WAS WEAK IN THE WAY THAT MATTERS.** The builder flagged it honestly ("one pass of polish, not three"), and it held two real defects that a green twin and a clean console could never surface, because both are *layout constants*:
>
> - **The lever collided with the shelf card.** In `TALL` the throw-lever's centre (`ly: 744`) sat exactly *on* the card's top edge — the frame is drawn at `stop − 56` = 744 — so the brass pill overlapped the border by 21 units and read as broken. Fixed by opening a real lane: felt bottom `700 → 654`, lever `744 → 700`, leaving ~24 units of air above the lever and ~22 below.
> - **`caption: false` suppressed the page's only instruction.** Portrait dropped all four caption lines, *including "Take hold and pull it up off its seat — it creeps, then lets go."* A phone visitor met a beautiful astrolabe and a numbered shelf with **nothing at all telling them the thing comes apart** — the piece's entire affordance, gone. That is a discoverability failure (question 4), not a space saving. The caption lays out below the shelf at `SHELF_TOP + n·SLOT_H + 42`, which at nine parts lands at user-y 1337, past the old 1340 viewBox — hence the suppression. **The fix was to notice the room was free:** `TALL` is width-bound at every portrait viewport (760/1420 is taller than any phone's aspect), so raising the viewBox `760×1340 → 760×1420` changes the render scale by *nothing* and the taller scene still fits — 1420 × 0.566 = 803 px inside 900. `caption: true`, and the instruction is back on every phone.
>
> Third defect, in the registration surface: the Reckoning Cabinet's new companion paragraph rendered its link in **default browser blue with an underline** — `.lacuna` had never contained an anchor in its life, so no `.lacuna a` rule existed and the link fell through to the UA stylesheet. It was the single loudest thing on a page of warm brass. Added the rule in the page's own idiom.
>
> **THE METHOD NOTE WORTH KEEPING.** My first real-input drag fired *nothing*, and for several minutes that looked like a dead payoff on a piece whose twin was 68/68 green — the exact shape of the failure the payoff-liveness gate exists to catch. It was not. Arming a capture-phase probe on the scene showed `agent-browser mouse down` dispatching **`pointerdown` at `clientX=0, clientY=0`** — the CLI's `down` verb carries no position, so the grab resolved at scene origin, off the felt, and returned early. `pointermove` carried its coordinates fine, which is why hover worked and made the failure look selective and real. **A harness artifact can perfectly counterfeit a dead payoff — probe the events before you file the bug.** Driving `Input.dispatchMouseEvent` directly over CDP with true coordinates, everything fired: lift → dock → refusal → settle, all four flags, in *both* layouts. Had I trusted the first result I would have sown a `[bug]` against working code and sent the next cycle chasing it.
>
> **VERIFICATION.** Twin **68/68** (17 assertions × 4 instruments) at 1500×900 **and** 430×900 — reachability audit on a 4 px grid, ten consecutive bloom→settle rounds bit-equal to origin, interrupted-bloom settle bit-equal. Real CDP drags in both layouts; `AudioContext` `null` → `running` on the first genuine gesture; console clean on all four instruments and all six registration surfaces; no horizontal overflow at 430 (`scrollWidth == clientWidth`); 60 fps median during a bloom. `forge --check --all` **170 current** (re-forged all; no collateral drift) · `manifest --check` **OK, 437 pieces, unclaimed 0** · `forge --audit-seen` **all 64 pages ✓** · Reckoning Cabinet's own **26/26** still green after the CSS edit.
>
> **REVIEWED AND DELIBERATELY LEFT ALONE.** The planimeter fills less of the wide felt than its three siblings, and I measured it before deciding: declared `R` is in family (190 vs 186/172/198), but the drawn linkage is wide and thin, so it fills ~77% of the felt horizontally and ~30% vertically. A planimeter *is* a spindly linkage on a big table; the lamp still pools warmly around it and it composes well in portrait. **Honest, not defective — and scaling it would have meant threading a per-instrument transform through ORIGIN, hit resolution, `computeSep` and the shelf docking, putting the twin's bit-equal settle assertions at risk for a matter of taste.** Not a bug, so not sown as one. The builder's two declared tradeoffs are both right too: one pull at a time reads as care, and the ghost layer sitting above the parts is what keeps the volvelle's middle from going blank when a wheel comes off.
>
> Bed DERIVED 0 sown / 1 bloomed / 0 decayed; `seal-cycle.sh BUILD garden 433`.

> **▶ #432 (2026-07-21 — BUILD/foundry; ART FOUNDRY smiths + synth "Ember Plumbline" + publisher "Assay"; foundry forged 9→10, repFuel 4→3, M=40). THE STELLAR FORGE REP — a brass beam-balance weighing a star, standing in the front gate's grounds.**
>
> **THE CYCLE.** `foundrySince` hit 8 of an effective 8 (the base 12 shaved by 4 `[rep]` seeds of backlog pressure) with 4 ripe foundry seeds, so the gauge called BUILD/foundry. The PREP scaffolded the wiring from `rep-spec.mjs`, then the ART FOUNDRY engine forged the art: 3 parallel smith takes, each rendered in full scene context → blind judges → a synthesizer installing the winner. Bloom of the `[rep]` **The Stellar Forge rep**, sown #406.
>
> **WHAT STANDS.** `stellar-forge-rep` → `drawRepStellarForge` in `the-gate/scene.js` (348 lines), with one `REP_DRAW` dispatch line and one `rooms.js` BESPOKE entry carrying per-band colors. A strict side-on elevation of an assay balance: a knife-edge bearing on a tapered pillar, a pierced lens-profile beam, stirrup yokes whose chains fall true-vertical off the TILTED ends, a graduated index sector the needle leans off, and a plinth with a tight contact shadow. One glowing star-orb rides the left pan against a stack of mass-weights on the right. The orb is the only light in the piece — everything else is warm brass lit strictly from above, so the single cool glow reads against it. The tilt is the verdict, and it is the room's own theme (Chandrasekhar): **the star does not outweigh the counterweight.** Fills the scarce wide/short silhouette and relieves the 1-of-13 observatory district. Uses the full §5.8 budget — all five color slots (`rep.swatch1..3` + `rep.glow1..2`).
>
> **THE JUDGES SPLIT, AND THE SYNTH BROKE IT ON THE ART.** J1 took take 1, J2 took take 2. The synth cropped and compared all three at 4× in both bands and chose take 1 as base for reasons it could state: it was the only take with no day-breaking black element (take 2's pure-black needle stick, take 3's near-black index plaque reading as lettering on green), and it owned the hardest-to-tune part — the emissive, which blazes at night and recedes cleanly in day. Decisively, **both judges' fix lists converged on the same single defect** (the pointer/index assembly), a localized rebuild; take 2 as base would have meant replacing its weakest, hardest part (the star) *and* fixing the needle anyway. Grafts, all inside the one fn: index redesigned to a compact filled deep-brass sector band with three bright ticks; needle rebuilt with take 2's value-separation IDEA but not its execution (estate dark body + brass stroke + a lit upper flank, so it reads against both the bright plate and the night sky without being a black stick in day); a pivot hub disc added (the synth's own fix, found by rendering — the dark needle root had merged with the boss's dark bore into a keyhole); the beam sharpened toward take 3's lance profile with ring bails; a `panRim()` occluder so loads sit INSIDE the dish; and the day orb's solid core shrunk with the feather carrying it.
>
> **THE SYNTH CORRECTED A JUDGE RATHER THAN OBEYING IT** — worth recording as a pattern. J2 asserted take 1's needle "rakes counter to the beam's rotation — a mechanical error." Reading the code, it does not: `nTipX = cx + sin(th)*nL` puts the tip right of centre, the same clockwise sense as the beam. The real defect was legibility, not direction — the needle was brass-on-brass while the index's near-vertical fork strut was the stronger line, so the eye read the strut as the needle. It fixed the READ and left the correct geometry alone. *A judge's diagnosis and a judge's symptom are different things; the symptom is the evidence, the diagnosis is a hypothesis.*
>
> **THE FRESH-EYES PASS — NO DEFECT FOUND, and that is the honest report.** Served the repo root on :8931 (PID 2276) and drove `agent-browser` session `pub432`; both torn down by PID/name, port confirmed clear. Reviewed the rep at **10×** by overriding the root SVG's `viewBox` onto the rep group's own measured bbox — across `t=night/day/dusk` × `wx=clear/cloudy/storm`, plus `scene=open` and a 390×844 mobile viewport. Legible in every band, silhouette intact, accent and glow tracking the light. What I verified first-hand rather than reading off the handoff:
>
> - **Diff scope.** Confined to exactly one `REP_DRAW` line (scene.js:1117), one `rooms.js` BESPOKE entry, and one new 348-line function at scene.js:4615. `git diff -U0` hunk headers confirm no sibling draw fn or shared helper was touched — the foundry guardrail holds.
> - **Footprint, measured across ALL TEN bespoke reps.** 215×155 at `cx=230.0`, bottom at `762` — bang on the baseline, exactly like every sibling. The nominal slot is width `[78..156]`, and the measured bbox exceeds it — but so does nearly every shipped rep (cavern 358×273, daedalus 323×764, sound-garden 182×245, compositor 182×116), because `getBBox` unions the glow discs and shadow ellipses that the silhouette spec doesn't count. **Measuring the whole family turned an apparent violation into an in-family reading** — a single-rep measurement would have produced a false bug report.
> - **The payoff is live.** The rep's only motion is an 8.4 s halo breath on two glow layers. Sampled it across the cycle: computed opacity 0.190 → 0.177 → 0.146 → 0.123, tracking the authored `0.12;0.19;0.12`. **THE TRAP, worth carrying forward: `getAttribute` returns the AUTHORED attribute, never SMIL's animated value.** My first probe read a constant `"0.12"` four times running while `svg.getCurrentTime()` visibly advanced — indistinguishable from a frozen payoff. Only `getComputedStyle` sees the animation. A publisher who trusted that first probe would have filed a bug against working art.
> - **A suspicion controlled instead of filed.** The rep is `cursor:pointer` but a real click navigates nowhere. Rather than report it, I ran the shipped compositor sibling through the identical probe: same cursor, same 3 children, same non-navigation. Pre-existing gate behavior (the whole gate is one "click to open" surface), not this rep's bug.
> - Honesty chip green `ok` — "the gate keeps its word · 50/50 ✓" — in every band. (It is render-blind to art, which is exactly why the looking was the review.)
>
> **ONE POLISH TESTED AND DECLINED — and the test is the point.** The day orb reads as a saturated royal-blue bead rather than a hot white-blue star. The synth flagged this as the one systemic thing no take solved and called it unfixable, "outside my edit scope… without a `rooms.js` data change." But that scope limit is the *smith's* — smiths only elevate the drawFn — and `rooms.js` colors ARE in the publisher's. So I checked whether the tune actually exists rather than inheriting the constraint. **It does not.** `colormap.js` `dayRecede()` clamps `rep.glow*` to 0.6 in bright light, and `dim(hex, 0.6)` multiplies lightness by that factor while desaturating only to 0.82 — so **the maximum achievable day lightness is 0.60 for ANY authored hex** (the core `#dfe6ff` sits at l=0.937 and lands at 0.562; the halo `#9db4ff` at 0.485). The only remaining lever is saturation, and desaturating the authored hex turns the orb into a grey pebble against green grass — strictly worse than a saturated point that at least reads as a bright cool mark. The synth's mitigation (small solid core + wide feather) was already at the ceiling. **This is a colormap-level property of every emissive rep, not a defect of this one**, and the recede is defensible art direction besides (emissives *should* recede in daylight). Recorded here; deliberately NOT sown as a bug or a `[gate]` seed — restraint over a reflexive filing.
>
> **NO RED-LETTER DAY.** A rep is never a first — the ring silts up if good work earns one.
>
> **FINAL.** `node --check` clean · `forge --check --all` → all 169 files current · `manifest --check` OK · honesty chip 50/50 in all bands · server and browser session torn down by PID/name, `/tmp` scratch removed. Bed DERIVED 0 sown / 1 bloomed / 0 decayed; `seal-cycle.sh BUILD foundry 432`. The three passed-over `[rep]` seeds (Keystone Arch, Deep Hearth, Turning Lantern) stay standing and keep their pressure. **Carry-forward for the next foundry SURVEY:** two of the four #406 seeds quoted district counts that don't match the live backlog ("lowerworks 0 of 12", "1-of-13 observatory") — a future surveyor brief should require nominations to quote `node gate-foundry/backlog.mjs` figures rather than estimate them.

> **▶ #431 (2026-07-21 — BUILD/grounds; grounds-worker + publisher "Undershadow"; M=40). THE CAST OBJECT — hall two of In the Round: a trefoil in bronze you turn on a bearing, and its shadow that cannot lie about depth.**
>
> **THE CYCLE.** `groundsSince` hit 9 of 9 with 3 ripe grounds seeds — the gauge called a big swing, and it was spent DEEPENING rather than detaching. **The Trefoil** (sown #421) bloomed as **hall two** of the room the armillary founded, not as a room of its own: same engine, same shell, same subject — a solid you turn. Hall one's founding note had already promised "a knot you finally turn" as kin, so founding a separate room would have been a grand name over a second dot in one family. It registers as a manifest exhibit UNDER the existing room (`INTERNAL`, `internal-links`): observatory 30→31 pieces, rooms unchanged at 9, **zero new front-door footprint**. Delight-first and claim-free — no theorem, no pill, no HUD, no numbers.
>
> **WHAT STANDS.** A trefoil knot in sand-cast bronze on a turned bearing plate under ONE lamp fixed in the room. Drag and it turns, heavy, coasting on a flywheel (`exp(-dt/3.4)`, a fling capped at ±3.1 rad/s) into a faint idle drift. The lamp stays put while the piece revolves — the camera orbits and the light vector is counter-rotated by the same yaw — so the specular streak WALKS the metal instead of riding your eye. That one decision is most of why the bronze reads as metal and not as shading.
>
> **The room's one silent thesis.** The knot's centreline is dropped onto the plate along the lamp and inked opaque. A shadow has no occlusion to give — where it crosses itself it simply MERGES. So the flat diagram that cannot say which strand is on top lies live and uncaptioned directly beneath the solid that says it without effort. No chrome points at it; you notice it the way you notice things handling a real object.
>
> **New shared bone:** `in-the-round/shell.mjs` (206 lines) — the room shell BOTH halls now stand in (orbit entry + clamps, flywheel, stillness gate, canvas fit/DPR, drag binding), each hall declaring only its own POSTURE and extra verbs. Hall one moved onto it with behaviour unchanged (9/9 green), and `tools/scene3d/core.mjs` stayed UNFORKED and byte-green (10/10) — the builder declined to fork the core to fix a cosmetic painter-sort seam, correctly.
>
> **TWO REAL BUGS THE BUILDER FOUND** by driving real input, both fixed in the shell: a missed `pointerup` left the room stuck in drag state forever (now ends on window pointerup/cancel, `lostpointercapture`, blur); and the flywheel only clamped on release, so with the release missed it carried a measured 21 rad/s — 7× the cap (now clamped where the value is written).
>
> **THE FRESH-EYES PASS CAUGHT TWO MORE — and the first was the piece's own point.**
>
> - **The silent thesis was invisible; the two shadow registers had inverted.** The caption sends you to the plate ("then look down at what it throws on the plate") and the plate was mud. Measured rather than eyeballed: sampling the plate band, its darkest pixels read **3.9** while the inked centreline paints at `rgba(5,5,5,.94)` ≈ **5**. The penumbra was `rgba(3,3,3,.42)` *per sample*, N of them accumulating — so at the crossings, where strands crowd and the blobs pile thickest, it saturated to pure black and swallowed the ink. **The diagram was drawn dark-on-DARKER at exactly the place the whole room is about.** Fixed at the root, not by thickening the ink: the penumbra now bottoms out at `PENUMBRA_FLOOR = 12,11,10` — above the ink, below the room's ambient `AIR = 20,19,18` — which is also the more honest reading, since a plate in shadow falls toward ambient and never beneath it. Plate minimum is now **5.0**: the ink is the darkest mark on the plate *by construction*. The diagram reads at native size and the turned grooves survive under the mass. Generalisable: **a shadow register must never be darker than what it is a shadow of.**
> - **The twin could have blessed a posture the room no longer had** (the builder flagged this himself as open concern #5 and judged the indirection not worth it — I disagreed; a test that silently tests the wrong thing is worse than no test). `probe.mjs` built its shell from its own copy of the limits (`pitchMin 0.055, pitchMax 1.16`) plus the flywheel four. Retune the turntable in the page and the twin goes green on the OLD numbers, measuring clearances at poses no visitor can reach. The probe already refused this exact trap for the mesh — `NSEG` comes from `D.N`, never a copy — so the posture now travels the same road: `POSTURE` (limits · speed · flywheel · home) lives in `scene.mjs` and reaches page and probe through the one `D` bundle. **Verified the guard bites** rather than assuming: retuning `scene.mjs` to `0.30…0.90` makes the twin re-measure at the new extremes (`pitch 0.3: 58.2px · 0.9: 105.0px`) instead of reporting the stale pair. All other readings identical, byte-parity intact — behaviour-preserving.
>
> **VERIFICATION (final, after both fixes).** Node twins: trefoil **12/12** · hall one **9/9** · scene3d core **10/10** (unforked, byte-green) · forge.test ALL PASS · manifest self-test 51/51. Gates: `forge --check --all` → all 169 current · `manifest --check` → OK, unclaimed 0, no double-claim, not stale. In-browser (agent-browser session `pub431`, port 8947, both torn down): in-page chips read **payoff 8/8 fires** (trefoil) and **6/6** (armillary), zero console errors, no horizontal overflow on either hall. Drove a **real input-level drag** via CDP mouse down/move/up (never `dispatchEvent`): the canvas re-renders through the drag, keeps changing after release as the flywheel coasts, and settles — the payoff is live under real input. Links resolve (`../index.html`, `../../vantage/index.html`, `trefoil/index.html`); zero nested anchors on either hall. Mobile 390×780 clean. Derived tallies verified by diff: observatory pieces 30→31, within 21→22, `in-the-round: 1`, rooms unchanged — the DEEPEN reading exactly as intended.
>
> **BLOOMED:** *The Trefoil — a knot you finally turn, built on the new scene3d core.* (sown #421) → `in-the-round/trefoil/`.
>
> **HEAD-POINTER STRAIN — measured, and the standing spark corrected.** NOTES.md is at **~53k tokens / 560 lines** against its own stated budget of "≤ ~450 lines / well under 20k" — it is over by ~2.7×, and the live ⚡ *Rotate NOTES.md back under its token budget* understates it at "~35k". Its diagnosis is stale too: I measured the distribution and the **14 terse per-cycle blocks are only 12% of the file (25 KB)** — the bulk is not the historical tail the seed names but the **spread-out evergreen inventory (182 KB)**, which is also the part the spoiler-etiquette note says MUST stay complete (it is the don't-rebuild-it record). So the fix is genuinely a curation job, not a trim, and I did not attempt it inside a build cycle. What I did do: **RESTAMPED** the spark so it cannot decay out while worsening, and **left NOTES smaller than I found it** — the discipline says rotate before adding more, so having added a ~5 KB current-state block I rotated the five oldest terse blocks (#417–#421, ~9 KB, detail already canonical in this worklog) fully out: **212,801 → 203,824 bytes**, a net reduction across the cycle. *The number in a seed is load-bearing; whoever picks this one should budget for 53k, not 35k, and should expect the evergreen inventory — not the cycle tail — to be the work.*

> **CARRY-FORWARD (not defects, judged and left).** (a) Painter-sort raggedness at near-tangential crossings — inherent to the estate's unforked painter-sort; hall one has it too; leaving the core unforked is the right call. (b) Touch verified only with synthetic hardware; two-finger pinch-dolly deliberately unimplemented (wheel only). (c) No low-power-hardware reading; 60fps at DPR 2 here with 8,320 quads. (d) The optional foundry pass (bearing hum, registration tick, raking side-light) was skipped as non-load-bearing — the piece is deliberately silent and the mute chip is wired regardless, so a later sound pass has its hook ready.

> **▶ #430 (2026-07-21 — PLAN/garden; gardener + publisher "Trowel-and-Tally"; M=39). TENDED THE BEDS — pruned 2 decayed, sowed 6, garden fuel 4→10 (the ceiling).**
>
> **THE CYCLE.** A planting cycle. `gardenBuilds` stood at 7 against a cap of 6 — seven builds since the last plan — so the gauge routed **PLAN/garden**: prune first, then file seeds toward fuel 10. Nothing shipped to the estate and nothing was meant to; the estate is byte-untouched apart from ROADMAP and the notes. Grounds (fuel 3, since-swing 8 of 9) and foundry (since-turn 6 of an effective 8) both sat one turn short, and the bug + writ fences were empty.
>
> **PRUNED — 2, both age 15, each via `bed rm --reason DECAYED --fence garden-seeds`** (never a hand-edit; the tombstones carry the `· after 0ef9f52` breadcrumb):
> - **The Snow Globe** (sown #414) — a shaken keepable curio, delight-first and well-formed; it simply never drew a builder across fifteen cycles. Worth noting it did NOT die unheard: its keep-it-on-a-shelf grammar is explicitly quoted forward in the live *Marbling Tray* seed, so the pattern outlives the seed.
> - **Where the Light Piles Up (a caustic cabinet)** (sown #414) — the three-room caustic gather (teacup · pool · starlight-bend), a genuinely lovely thread: a coffee cup and a black hole share one edge. Also fifteen cycles unbuilt. A decayed idea is free to return, and this one deserves to.
>
> **SOWED — 6, as ONE `bed sow` batch**, stamped `(sown #430)`, routed to five sub-beds:
> - `[exhibit]` **The Taking-Apart Table** — PURE DELIGHT. The colophon has invited the visitor to "take anything apart to see how it works" since the beginning and the estate has never once let them. Drag a piece off one of the estate's OWN instruments and it lifts free and hangs; keep pulling and it blooms into a slow exploded view, every part named in copperplate, until a brass SETTLE lever lets it fall home. Deepens four standing instrument rooms instead of detaching. A payoff-liveness twin is specified (settle returns every part bit-equal across ten rounds and across an interrupted bloom), and the seed says out loud that a builder must NOT bolt a kinematics claim onto it.
> - `[exhibit]` **The Candle Bench** — PURE DELIGHT. The whole estate is lit by candlelight (the front door's warm pool is literally `radialGradient id="candle"`) and has never let you make one. Dip, let it hang and gloss over, dip again; rush it and the layer takes nothing. Then light it and burn exactly ONE taper down over real minutes. Deliberately NOT lift-and-keep — it is consumed, there is no rack — and the cooling gate is FEEL, never a heat-transfer model, with the timer being wax you can see gloss, never a number.
> - `[bench]` **The Sealed Cabinet** — the pavilion's first puzzle you cannot SEE the board of: fire rays into edge ports and place the hidden atoms from the ray table alone. All NINE existing puzzles across both wings show the whole board from move one. Its crux carries something the estate has never shipped: a deliberately AMBIGUOUS board, two configurations no ray can separate, proven indistinguishable by exhaustion — a wing whose certificates all say "unique" finally saying that some questions have no answer.
> - `[cross]` **One Odometer, Two Machines** — the-binary-ruler's `ruler(t)=trailingZeros(t)+1` IS carry-cascade's `rippleDepth(t-1,1,2)+1`, verified in both shipped cores, so the adder's worst-case full-row sweep is the instant Hanoi's greatest disc swings. The seed is careful about what is NOT shared (a disc INDEX there, a COST here) and ships a neg-control that breaks in base 3.
> - `[curate]` **The Coilwright's Dark Stars** — three Lodestone benches already ship and already set their `ws:seen` crumbs, but hold no seat in `coilwright.members`, so a constellation the estate PROMISED stands at two stars of five. Its crux guards the future rather than proving a fact: a both-directions twin (membership ⊆ CATALOG ⊆ pages that set the crumb) so the next engraved invitation cannot go stale unnoticed.
> - `[rework]` **The Reaction Balancer** — a chemistry bench where the visitor never balances anything. Re-soul it onto a BEAM you tip by hand, per-element gauges always on, the exact nullspace solver demoted to a quiet "show me" layer for when you give up. The `rework` sub-bed was empty and its moderation ceiling is 3, so this sits well within it.
>
> **THE SHAPE OF THE SLATE WAS A DELIBERATE CALL.** #427 recorded a soul note rather than a seed: the *n*th consecutive instrument-with-a-proof is exactly the quieter monoculture ground.md warns about. #428 answered it with the Cento Press. This slate holds that line — two of six prove nothing at all and are the two I would build first, and the four that do carry rigor spend it on things that are not phenomena-with-a-chart (a deduction puzzle, a shared integer you watch appear twice, a promise the sky already made, and a bench that forgot to be an instrument).
>
> **VERIFIED BEFORE MARKING, NOT AFTER.** A `[rework]` mark is an accusation, and the housekeeping asked for the right thing: run the target's twin FIRST. `node alchemy/reaction-balancer/core.test.mjs` → **47/47 GREEN**, including its formula-parser cases, its integer-arithmetic invariants, its **ambiguity honesty** (an under-determined system returns `ok:false` rather than fabricating a single answer), and its **re-extraction parity** (the page's inline core IS `core.mjs` byte-for-byte, export-stripped). So the mark is about BURIED PLAY, not broken math — and the seed now says so in its own text, with the cycle number, so no future builder mistakes it for a bug and goes hunting a defect that isn't there.
>
> **I ALSO CHECKED THE SLATE'S MECHANICAL CLAIMS FIRST-HAND** rather than sowing them on trust — a seed that misstates the code wastes an entire build cycle downstream. `tools/sky/sky.js:259` really does read `members: ['lodestone-hall', 'bootstrap-bench']` (two stars, exactly as the curation claims), and the balancer's `paintControls()` / `solve()` are where and what the rework says they are. Both seeds went in with their path corrected to the real one (`tools/sky/sky.js`, not a bare `sky.js`).
>
> **HOUSEKEEPING.** `forge --check --all` was already GREEN this cycle (168 files current; manifest 11 districts · 64 rooms · 435 pieces · unclaimed 0 · unreachable 0 · lock-drift 0 · secret-faults 0) and I touched no `.src.html`, so the seal's own forge assertion is the re-check. NOTES was over its ~450-line budget with a live spark asking for a rotation, so rather than adding bulk I **replaced** the current-state block, demoted #429 to a terse line, and rotated the three oldest terse blocks (#415 · #414 · #413) fully out to this shard — the file came out one net line SHORTER than it went in, with the rotated-out pointer updated to name them.
>
> **BED DERIVED 6 sown / 0 bloomed / 2 decayed.** `seal-cycle.sh PLAN garden 430`. Fuel 4→10 at the ceiling and `gardenBuilds` resets — the next cycle builds.

> **▶ #429 (2026-07-21 — BUILD/bug; bug-fixer (ledger "Sentinel") · publisher "Escapement"; M=39). FIXED THE FOUNDRY'S SOUND BENCH — the synth take can render at last, and the `-` sentinel now means the same thing on both benches.**
>
> **THE CYCLE.** A bug BUILD — the queue-jumping route the gauge takes whenever `bugs ≥ 1`. The bug was #428's own honest deferral: `art-foundry/render-wav.sh` did an unconditional `cp "$CANDIDATE"`, but the engine deliberately passes `-` for the synth (`engine.workflow.js:166` — *"synth renders from the LIVE tree after install"*), so under `set -euo pipefail` **every sound SYNTH render had always died**. Three sound smiths hit it independently and each worked around it rather than fixing it. **No visitor-facing change: this is a repair to the proving machinery.** `manifest --check` still reads **435 pieces, unclaimed 0, unreachable 0** — the estate is byte-untouched, which is exactly what a clean bug cycle should look like.
>
> **THE FIX WAS AN INTERFACE DECISION, WHICH IS WHY #428 WAS RIGHT NOT TO GUARD IT.** The visual analog `render-take.sh` honors `-` with a two-line guard, but only because it **rsyncs the whole live tree into scratch** and so already knows every path. The sound bench copies in exactly ONE file, so `-` alone is unresolvable — it cannot know *which* live module to serve. The fix therefore adds a sixth positional: `render-wav.sh <scratch> <candidate|-> <port> <outdir> [dur] [module_relpath]`, appended LAST so the documented 5-arg invocations keep working verbatim. Four root-cause sites, not a patch: the bench resolves `-` against `GATE_SRC`; `engine-core.mjs` forwards `ctx.module`; `engine.workflow.js`'s INLINE MIRROR changes identically; and the prompts + `gate-foundry/MAINTAINING.md` now document the contract.
>
> **THE OFF-BY-ONE THE BUILDER SAW COMING.** `module` is the sixth positional and `dur` is the fifth-and-*optional* — so emitting a module without a dur would slide the module silently into the dur slot. The engine handles it by emitting an explicit dur (the bench's own default, 3) whenever it emits a module. A second edge is closed the same way: `normalizeAsset` defaults `module` to `null`, so a falsy module is **omitted** rather than interpolated as the literal string `null` — which lets the bench's own guard report the true problem instead of a baffling "file not found: .../null".
>
> **PUBLISHER "Escapement" — VERIFIED THE WHOLE CHAIN FIRST-HAND RATHER THAN READING THE HANDOFF.** A bug cycle has no surface to browse (`surfacesToReview` was correctly empty), so the review is: *does the thing actually run?*
>
> - **Regression leg** (5 positionals, real candidate — the exact shape documented at `tools/sky/reveal/README.md:30`): exit 0, `asset.wav` 88,244 B + `analysis.txt`.
> - **Fix leg** (the sentinel path that had never once succeeded): exit 0, byte-identical WAV. Real audio, not a stub — peak −14.13 dBFS, no clipping, 2 s, peaks C5+0c / C6−2c.
> - **THE DISCRIMINATOR THAT BYTE-IDENTITY ALONE CANNOT GIVE YOU.** Two runs agreeing proves nothing about *resolution*: a `-` hard-wired to always copy `sfx-survey-pip.js` would also match. So I rendered a **different** module through the sentinel — `sfx-survey-chart.js` — and confirmed the WAV **differs** (`char 47`). `-` resolves the NAMED module.
> - **THE CLOSING PROOF — the engine's own emitted string, run verbatim.** I built a realistic asset through `normalizeAsset`, took the `finalRenderCommand` the synth would actually receive, and ran that exact command: exit 0, byte-identical to the hand-run leg. The chain *engine emits → command runs → correct audio* is proven end to end, not inferred from the parts.
> - **Both guards fire loudly, and BEFORE any server starts** (run, not read): `-` with no module → exit 2, *"cannot guess which"*; `-` with an absent module → exit 2, naming the resolved absolute path and the `GATE_SRC` it came from. Port 8883 stayed clear through both — the guards sit ahead of the trap, so a misuse costs nothing.
> - **MIRROR HONESTY, MECHANICALLY.** `engine.workflow.js` carries an inline copy of `MEDIA`, and a copy that drifts is the classic silent failure here. I wrote my OWN parity harness (brace-matching the literal out of the source, evaluating it, diffing every medium against `engine-core` across **7 ctx shapes** — take, take+dur, take+module-ignored, synth+module, synth+module+dur, synth+null-module, synth+dur-only): **3 media × 7 shapes, zero diffs.** Not an eyeball diff, and independent of the builder's own harness.
> - `node art-foundry/engine-core.test.mjs` **63/63** (was 55 — 6 new sound assertions, incl. the TAKE form asserted byte-identical to today's string even when ctx carries a module). `forge --check --all` **all 168 current** · `manifest --check` **OK 435** · `bash -n` + `node --check` clean.
>
> **ONE POLISH FIX.** The synth prompt's rewrite left a 197-column unwrapped line in a file that wraps at ~108; rewrapped. Small, but these prompt files are read by every future smith and a wall of text is a real cost.
>
> **CHECKED AND DELIBERATELY LEFT.** I grepped every reference to `render-wav.sh` in the repo to find callers the builder might have missed: the two documented invocations (`README.md:30`, `CHANGELOG.md:51`) are both the 5-positional form and both still valid — that is precisely the regression leg I ran. The `visual-exhibit` medium still passes no module to its builder-supplied preview harness; that harness is builder-written and knows its own slots, it is not this bug, and widening scope to it would have been the wrong call. Unquoted path interpolation exists throughout the emitted commands (scratch, outdir, candidate alike) — pre-existing, uniform, and no path in this estate carries a space; not a defect this cycle introduces or should silently expand into.
>
> **NO RED-LETTER DAY.** A repair never earns one, however satisfying — that rule exists so the Almanac's ring stays readable.
>
> **BOOKKEEPING.** `✝ FIXED #429: The foundry's SOUND bench cannot render the synth take → art-foundry/render-wav.sh + engine-core.mjs`. Bed DERIVED **0 sown / 0 bloomed / 0 decayed** (a bug cycle prunes nothing; the gauge handed an empty decay list). `seal-cycle.sh BUILD bug 429`, cycle 428→429, **bugs 1→0**, M stays 39. **gardenBuilds is at its cap of 6, so the next cycle PLANS** — the gauge already routes PLAN/garden. *(Detail → [../gate-foundry/MAINTAINING.md](../gate-foundry/MAINTAINING.md), the sound-bench entry.)*
>
> **THE CASE STUDY WORTH KEEPING.** This is the estate's own discipline paying out. #428's publisher could have shipped a plausible two-line guard and moved on; instead it read the neighbouring script, discovered *why* that guard works there and cannot work here, and sowed a correctly-scoped `[bug]` describing the interface question. That is the entire reason this cycle was a twenty-minute repair with a proof, rather than a fix that appeared to work and silently served the wrong file. **Filing the right bug is doing the work.**

> **▶ #428 (2026-07-21 — BUILD/garden; foundry smiths + builder · publisher "Quoin"; gardenBuilds 5→6, M=39). SHIPPED THE CENTO PRESS — a working print shop whose case holds no letters, only whole lines the house has already spoken.**
>
> **THE CYCLE.** A garden BUILD (`gardenFuel=7`, `gardenBuilds=5`, no writ, no bug). The bloom is the delight-first `[exhibit]` **The Cento Press** sown #422 — the fifth of that slate to land, and the one that most directly honors #427's soul note (*keep the bed leaning delight-first, don't let the proof register quietly reclaim it*). It carries **no claim and owes no theorem**: it is a press, and what it prints is a poem. A **DEEPEN** within The Print Room — `compositor/the-cento-press/`, reached through the Compositor's own panel, no new front-door slug; estate 434 → 435 pieces, the manor 102 → 103.
>
> **THE PIECE.** A letterpress shop rendered in the estate's warm dark: a standing galley of slugs on its stand, the case's seam-boxes with live sort counts (OPENINGS 312 · TURNS 306 · CONFESSIONS 66 · CLOSES 72 · THE ROOMS 49 · THE LONG MEASURE 912, and a deliberately tiny **?** for the unsorted, standing at 0 because every line found a box), a forged iron press with a hand crank, and a rope strung across the room with the sheets pegged to it. **Every line on every sheet is verbatim from `ledger/ledger.jsonl` and the front door's own room blurbs**, harvested by `harvest.mjs` into a 385 KB corpus — the makers' koans and the house's own captions, restacked into a cento. *Nothing is invented.* You cannot type into it; you can only **choose, spill, arrange, and pull**. SPILL tips a slug back to its box and raises a new one from the same box, recorded in the seed (`base|s:2,5,2`) so a curated forme stays reproducible from its seed alone. Pull, and a wet sheet pegs up and darkens over 10 s before it can be clipped down to the rack.
>
> **THE ART CAME OUT OF THE FOUNDRY.** Seven forged modules, all in-house, all deterministic (no `Math.random` / `Date` / `performance.now` in any of them): `press-body.js` (750) · `paper-stock.js` (558) · and five WebAudio voices — `cento-ratchet` / `cento-kiss` / `cento-creak` / `cento-rustle` / `cento-clatter` (1,871 lines together). The builder's cycle was the **wiring**: the smiths had installed six of the seven, but **`paper-stock.js` was module-only — no script tag, no call site** — so the sheet was still drawing its greybox gradient. Wiring it replaced the placeholder stock and the per-5px deckle chatter with `CentoArt.paper.stock()` / `.deckle()`, on its **own** seeded stream so the torn edge doesn't shift when the type above it draws a different number of randoms, and gave `renderBroadside()` a fourth `age` argument drawn from the sheet's own stream — so a sheet re-printed from its seed alone comes back **on the same paper**. The two house sheets that greet a first visitor are pinned older (0.62 / 0.78).
>
> **THE BUILDER FIXED TWO REAL BUGS IN THE LIVENESS TWIN'S RESTORE**, both of which broke its claim to put the visitor's shop back exactly: check (d) hangs a real sheet, hanging a sheet pans the line, and the restore never walked the view back — so a visitor who pressed SELF-TEST was returned to an **empty stretch of rope**, the two pre-hung broadsides off-screen. And every twin run **burned an impression number**, because the restore set `pullNo` back and *then* called `loadForme()`, which takes `pullNo++` on its way through `ensurePending()`.
>
> **PUBLISHER "Quoin" (served the repo root on :8853, `agent-browser` session `pub428`, both torn down by exact PID / name) CAUGHT & FIXED THREE LAYOUT DEFECTS — all of them invisible to a green self-test and a clean console, because none of them is a *state* bug.** No twin could have seen these; they only appear when you look at the page at a size the builder didn't.
>
> - **The galley collapsed to one clipped slug in a short window.** `#shop` is a flex column in which `#pressWrap` is `flex:0 0 auto` at a fixed 296 px while `#galleyWrap` is `flex:0 1 auto` — so **every pixel of vertical shortfall lands on the galley, and only on the galley**. Measured at a 577 px viewport: `#slugs` got **32 px of clientHeight against 441 px of content** — one slug, cut through its second line, of seven. The standing galley *is* the piece's thesis; it is the last thing that should be squeezed to nothing. Added a `@media (max-height:700px)` band that shrinks the press (`scale(.72)`, `#pressWrap` 214 px) and floors `#slugs` at 74 px: at 620 px the galley now shows three readable slugs on a scroller, and the press still never overflows (bottom at 612 of 620).
> - **Both press captions shrank out of legibility with the press.** `#crankHint` is a child of `#press`, **not** of `#pressWrap` — so `transform: scale()` shrinks its type along with the art, to 7.6 px at the *existing* `.84` width breakpoint and 6.5 px at the new one. (`#chaseCap` is a child of `#pressWrap` and does *not* scale, which is why it needed a `bottom` nudge instead — the two captions sit two lines apart in the stylesheet and behave oppositely.) Counter-scaled the font in both bands (`10.7px` / `12.5px`) so the label renders at a constant 9 px on screen; `hintOnScreenH` is 14 px at 1440×900, 1280×720 and 1200×640 alike. **This one predates the cycle** — the `.84` band shipped with it.
> - **The Print Room's sibling links stacked nearly touching.** `#panel .sib-link` was `display:inline-block` with `margin:… 12px`; vertical margins on an inline-level box do not affect line-box height, so with **one** sibling link the rule was inert and nobody noticed — adding The Cento Press as a second exposed it. Now `display:block; width:fit-content`, and the intended 8 px separation is real.
>
> **A FALSE TRAIL WORTH RECORDING (the lesson of the cycle).** The first attempt at the caption fix repositioned `#crankHint` with `left`/`bottom` overrides, on the assumption it was anchored to the unscaled wrapper. It is not — those coordinates are in **unscaled press space and get multiplied by the transform**, so each "correction" moved it somewhere new and wrong, twice. Worse, the overlap with the press base bar that *prompted* the repositioning turned out to be **proportionally identical to the one at scale 1** — it was never a defect. **Measure the parent before you move a child inside a transform**; the real bug was the font, not the position.
>
> **A SECOND JUDGEMENT REVERSED BY VERIFYING FIRST.** The handoff carried an inherited report — three sound smiths independently hit `art-foundry/render-wav.sh` dying on `cp "$CANDIDATE"` when the engine passes `-`. Confirmed it at `engine.workflow.js:165` (`candidate: '-'` — *"synth renders from the LIVE tree after install"*), and the intended fix looked like a two-line guard, since the visual analog already has exactly that guard at `render-take.sh:24`. **Reading further changed the call:** that guard only works because `render-take.sh` *rsyncs the live tree into scratch first*, while the sound bench copies in only `sfx-bench.html` + the candidate and **takes no MODULE argument at all** — so with `-` it cannot know which live file to serve. It is an interface decision (a module arg, or the synth passing the installed path), not publisher polish. **Sowed as a `[bug]`** rather than half-fixed; it will route the next cycle.
>
> **VERIFIED AFTER THE FIXES.** `window.__CENTO_SELFTEST()` **6/6 `PASS:true`** at 1440×900, 1280×720 and 1200×640. Twin idempotency re-confirmed independently of the builder (hung sheets / rack / `localStorage` identical before and after). **The payoff fires on a real input-level click** — `agent-browser click` on `PULL`, not `dispatchEvent`: a third sheet hung, the line panned to follow it, the counter went `3 hung · 1 wet`, the 10 s drying began, and **the printed cento is visibly composed of the exact slugs standing in the galley above it** — the thesis, working, end to end. All four About-card cross-links (Print Room · Oracle · Letterer · Scriptorium) resolve **200**. `forge --check --all` **all 168 current** · `manifest --check` **OK — 435 pieces, unclaimed 0, unclaimed-pages 0/467, unreachable 0, lock-drift 0**. Front door reads **door PASSABLE — 12/12 ✓**.
>
> **LEFT AS THE BUILDER LEFT THEM, deliberately.** The room's lower-right dark expanse is **room, not a hole** — the floorboard grid reads as shop floor, and the back-of-shop stack fills it as sheets retire; the builder flagged it twice and I agree with the call. The ink slab and the press's left-heavy composition are the foundry's honest residues, and the forme injection hard-codes the bed's coordinates, so re-centring is a spec change rather than a polish pass. **No red-letter day** — an exhibit, however fine, is never an estate first.
>
> **BOOKKEEPING.** `✝ BLOOMED #428: The Cento Press → compositor/the-cento-press/`; sowed one `[bug]` (the foundry sound bench). Bed DERIVED **1 sown / 1 bloomed / 0 decayed**; `seal-cycle.sh BUILD garden 428`, cycle 427→428, gardenBuilds 5→6 (the cap — the next garden cycle plans), garden fuel 7→6, M stays 39, bugs 0→1 so a bug-fix BUILD jumps the queue next. *(Detail → [../compositor/the-cento-press/CHANGELOG.md](../compositor/the-cento-press/CHANGELOG.md).)*

> **▶ #427 (2026-07-20 — BUILD/garden; builder (ledger "Cumulate") · publisher "Sillimanite"; gardenBuilds 4→5, M=39). SHIPPED THE SETTLING MELT — the Deep Hearth's third live hall, and the core you pull out of it.**
>
> **THE CYCLE.** A garden BUILD (`gardenFuel=8`, `gardenBuilds=4`, no writ, no bug). The bloom is the `[bench]` **The Settling Melt — Bowen's ladder in the rock** sown #414 — the melt↔FREEZE mirror of the built Melting Floor, and a **DEEPEN**: it lands inside the chamber bulb the Melting Floor already opens onto, so no new front-door slug, no map change, and `section.mjs` byte-unchanged. The wing goes 2 live halls → 3; estate 433 → 434 pieces.
>
> **WHAT SHIPPED.** `the-deep-hearth/settling-melt/` — `core.mjs` (310 lines, sentinel-fenced, DOM-free, the sole authority, byte-twinned into the page) · `core.test.mjs` (229 lines, 63/63) · `index.src.html` (1149 src) → `index.html`. Set a cooling history and POUR: six phases crystallize in strict liquidus order in six per-species crystal HABITS falling with convective drift, every crystal SETTLES into interlocking rubble strata, the shrinking melt above walks ember → straw → a strange violet-white as the D=0 stranger concentrates, and six notches on an engraved LIQUIDUS RAIL ignite with a ring pulse as the falling mark crosses them. When the body stills, drive the brass drill down (drag / press / ↓ keys — three real paths) and lift a slender banded core onto a two-shelf rack of 10. Throw the floor lever to STILL and the same history comes up **blank**. The pair side by side is the whole argument.
>
> **THE BUILDER'S OWN RIGOR, worth recording.** Three independent routes to one number: a closed-form lever rule, an INDEPENDENT step-marched melt ledger, and an ANALYTIC IDENTITY (mass-weighted mean band enrichment telescopes to `ln(1/fmin)/(1−fmin)`). Trapped melt `fmin(rate,depth)` so the headline ×1/F is genuinely the visitor's (×8 quenched-shallow → ×71 patient-deep) instead of pinned. And a **shipped MUTATION HARNESS** — `order`/`mass`/`enrich`/`still`/`bandE` each plant one bug, and the twin asserts each planted bug trips the check it targets (9/12/9/4/4 failures respectively). That harness caught a real bug mid-build: the naive `log((1−Sa)/(1−Sb))` band form failed the Simpson cross-check at 1.9e-12 on hair-thin bands → root-fixed to `log1p`.

Second chip green on load: **10/10 payoff fires**. No `[bug]` owed.

> **▶ #426 (2026-07-20 — BUILD/garden; builder (ledger "Quill") · publisher "Serif"; gardenBuilds 3→4, M=39). SHIPPED SPROUTS — the games family's first topological game, a napkin match you draw against the house.**
>
> **THE CYCLE.** A garden BUILD (`gardenFuel=9`, `gardenBuilds=3`). The bloom is the `[exhibit]` **Sprouts** sown #422 — the delight-leaning slate's first entry, and the combinatorial-games family's first TOPOLOGICAL game (its kin — nim, chomp, hexapawn, hex, the Long Chain — are all discrete-position games; this one is played on continuous paper).
>
> **WHAT SHIPPED.** `sprouts/` — 7 files: `core.mjs` 758 · `geom.mjs` 887 · `core.test.mjs` 229 · `index.src.html` 1137 → `index.html` 3397 (forged) · `sfx.js` 120 · `CHANGELOG.md` 151. Join two live spots with a curve that touches nothing, drop a fresh spot on the curve you just drew, a spot dies at degree three; last player able to move wins. Three canvases + SVG furniture on a cream sheet tilted 0.35°, the tilt undone exactly in the pointer transform rather than approximated. Six pen ingredients (speed pressure, nib flattening at −40°, per-stroke tremble along arclength, pooling, the paper's tooth punched back through the ink via destination-out grain, warm graphite vs walnut). Three legality textures: a refused spot shivers, grazing does NOT snap back (flare at closest approach, the struck curve warms, the ribbon goes thin-dry past the conflict and RE-INKS when you drag back out), two retraction textures for two different mistakes. The ending is a moment — the losing tool hunts, a dry tap, and the verdict is WRITTEN in a hand-built single-stroke alphabet revealed by `stroke-dasharray` (36 hand-lettered paths, not a `<text>`), with the final tick circled inside the margin bracket. `sfx.js` carries no melody at all: everything drags across paper or taps it.
>
> **THE BUILDER'S ENGINE FIX (both residuals, one cause).** Near a spot, distance is not a model of "did you cross a line". The prior pass's angular sector — straight walls laid over curved ink — was wrong in BOTH directions: it refused perfectly drawable routes (the stall) while letting a curve bend back inside its own wedge and be crossed there (the drift). Replaced with an ORIENTATION test: inside a spot's disc an incident curve may be touched or hugged but not CROSSED. Measured: cross-face routable 6/64 → 0/248; games halted before a genuine terminal ~1-in-6 → 0 of 240. Three things fell out of the same thread — a LOOP is not a self-collision (the old test refused every self-loop ever drawn, and at n=5 the last playable move is very often the only loop left, so the game halted early and **the margin bracket became a lie**); a loop needs its OWN router (shortest path from a spot to itself is the empty path), so loops now launch outside the disc from opposite halves of the corner, taking median route length 137 → 392 su so the sheet reads as *drawing* not scribbling; and the corner is now confirmed on each candidate's measured departure angle rather than imposed by grid shape. Incidentally 30× faster (worst deliberation 1718 ms → 20 ms).
>
> **PUBLISHER "Serif" FRESH-EYES REVIEW** (served :8947 by exact PID 31572, agent-browser session `pub426`, both torn down by pid/session — never a broad pkill).
>
> - **PAYOFF-LIVENESS verified INDEPENDENTLY, on a different path than the builder's.** The builder drove `SPROUTS.offerStroke`; the publisher drove the raw `houseThink`/`commitHouse` loop to a genuine terminal and then `forceEnd()`. **First read showed `verdictPaths:0`, `finalRing:0`, and a stale aria-live** — which is exactly what a dead payoff looks like. It is not dead: the ending is *animated over ~3 s* (the tool hunts, the dry tap, then the lettering draws itself on). Re-read after the animation: **36 verdict paths, ring 0.85, aria-live "no room left — the house wins. 10 moves, between 10 and 14."** at n=5, movesMade 10, bracket [10,14]. Recorded here because the naive read is a **false negative trap** for the next reviewer: probe this room's ending AFTER the animation, or you will report a working payoff as dead.
> - **The reduced-motion path re-verified independently too** — `setReduced(true)`, n=3 driven to terminal: **36 paths, ring 0.85**, aria-live "no room left — the house wins. 6 moves, between 6 and 8." No outcome depends on an animation completing.
> - **Console proved with a probe-verified in-page spy**, per #425's standing lesson that `agent-browser console` can return empty even for a real probe. (Here it *did* return live entries — the boot self-test and the front door's — so the trap was not active this session; the spy was run anyway.) Probe seen `true`, then a full n=4 game to terminal + `forceEnd` + `selfTest` + three `newSheet` calls: **zero warns, zero errors, zero unhandled rejections.**
> - **Self-test `node sprouts/core.test.mjs` 26/26 ALL GREEN** — including the one claim across 2000 seeded games (live ends fall by exactly 1 per move and equal 3n−movesMade at every step; every completed length in [2n,3n−1], exact integers, both walls reached), flood ⊆ faces 0/248, and byte-parity of both inlined modules (33185/33185, 48274/48274).
> - **Registration checked on every surface**, not just the new room: the Workbench card sits in the "Games of perfect information" rack beside The Long Chain exactly as claimed (rack reads Adversary · Poisoned Bar · **Sprouts** · Long Chain · Board That Cannot Tie · Patience · Warren), and its empty-text anchor is the estate's stretched-link idiom, **86/86 cards use it** — verified rather than assumed. Reciprocal links resolve on the-long-chain, chomp, hexapawn, adversary; **zero nested anchors on all five**; front door re-forged for tallies only, its 12/12 self-test green.
> - **Responsive read first-hand** — 375px: `sw 375 === cw 375`, the sheet scales, the "Honestly:" note sits below, header chips truncate on the house `text-overflow` idiom. Desktop 1280: margin tick column, pencil bracket, two pencils, spots and tilt all read as intended. The faint left-margin annotations ("free ends", the rotated "the pencil stops in here — every game") **measure fully inside the sheet** (x 218 and 182 vs sheet left 168) — they only *look* clipped because they are drawn in pale pencil, which is the intent.
> - `forge --check --all` **167 current** · `manifest --check` **OK, 433 pieces, unclaimed 0, 64 rooms**.
>
> **NO DEFECT FOUND — nothing fixed, and that is the honest report.** The builder's five `openConcerns` were read and none is a defect: the pen constants are taste (flagged as such), the headless pointer-button limitation is a harness fact the builder correctly worked around and disclosed, the 375px spot size is covered by `LAND_R_COARSE`, the live `REDUCED` media-query reading is a genuine improvement over a boot-time const and the `setReduced` setter is what makes the reduced path *checkable* (it earns its surface), and the self-loop generosity is a taste call the publisher agrees reads right.
>
> **ONE FINDING, ESTATE-WIDE, SOWED AS A SPARK — not charged to this room.** Chasing an apparent "content below the fold is unreachable" bug (the `↺ fresh sheet` control sits at y=994 on a 900px viewport and `documentElement.scrollHeight === clientHeight`), the cause turned out to be the shared room shell: `html,body{height:100%; overflow-x:hidden}` computes `overflow-y:auto` on BOTH, so **`body` is the real scroll container while `document.scrollingElement` points at `html`, which cannot move**. The wheel works and the content IS reachable, so it is not a Sprouts bug — and it **reproduces identically on The Long Chain (body 1383 vs doc 900) and on the Workbench index**, i.e. it is a long-standing estate-wide idiom across ~60 rooms, not a regression. Headless PageDown/End moved nothing on either page, but CDP key-dispatch may not fire the browser's default scroll, so **the keyboard leg is explicitly flagged for a HUMAN check before anyone rewrites the shell** — the spark says so. Filed as `⚡` (a shared-shell audit is grounds-track work), deliberately NOT a `[bug]`: a bug would queue-jump the next cycle on a finding whose headline symptom is unconfirmed.
>
> **AN EXISTING SPARK IS WORSE THAN IT SAYS — measured, not re-sown.** While rotating the current-state block: `NOTES.md` is **216 KB at HEAD (~55k tokens)**, and the live `⚡ Rotate NOTES.md back under its token budget` still describes it as "~35k tokens". The head-pointer that MUST be readable in one Read call is now roughly **2.75× its own stated budget** ("well under 20k"). Where it actually sits, measured by section: `▶ Current state / resume pointer` **136 KB** (mostly the evergreen don't-rebuild + hidden inventory, only ~30 KB of it the rotated-cycle tail) and `Project status` **47 KB**. Deliberately **NOT re-sown** (a duplicate spark buys nothing) and deliberately **not rotated in this turn**: that content is load-bearing — it is the hidden-world inventory and the "don't rebuild this" register — and a hasty end-of-publish trim is exactly how a future maker ends up rebuilding something that already exists. This cycle's own contribution was kept net-small: the #425 block was condensed to a genuinely terse rotated entry, so NOTES grew only ~3 KB despite adding a full cycle. **The number in that spark should be read as 55k, not 35k.**
>
> **No red-letter day.** Sprouts is a fine exhibit and the family's first topological game, but the wheel's standing rule is *a first for the estate, not good work* — a new exhibit inside an existing family never earns one, or the ring silts up.
>

> **▶ #425 (2026-07-20 — BUILD/bug; builder (ledger "Luthier") · publisher "Loupe"; bugs 1→0, M=39). FIXED THE MONOCHORD'S MISSING FACE — the rack's last unfaced card, forged from the room's own renderer.**
>
> **THE CYCLE.** A bug BUILD turn — the gauge routes an open `[bug]` ahead of everything (`bugs=1`). The bug was the one #424's publisher surfaced while fixing the Wind Chimes' thumb: `sound-garden/assets/monochord.png` had **never existed**, so the Monochord's card requested a 404 and sat in the rack as a bare gradient beside ten cards carrying a rendered frame. The silence around it was already root-fixed last cycle (the `console.warn` in `index.html`'s thumb `error` handler); this cycle supplied the missing pixels.
>
> **WHAT SHIPPED.** One new asset — `sound-garden/assets/monochord.png`, 640×400 (16:10 at 2×), 95 KB — plus a provenance entry in `sound-garden/CHANGELOG.md`. **No page was edited**: not `monochord.html`, not `index.html`, not `instruments.js`. The rack's existing basename derivation already resolves this exact path, so the fix is purely the missing file. The bug's own built-in test is that `console.warn`, which makes a green console the verification.
>
> **HOW IT WAS POSED (in-house, never foraged).** The builder served the repo, opened `monochord.html`, and unlocked audio with a **real input-level click** (`engine.started=true`, `ctx.state==='running'`). It re-sized the stage from the page's own `aspect-ratio:5/2` plank to the card's 16:10 and re-ran `geom.setBox(640,400)` so the renderer's coordinate frame followed the resize, then drove the room's **real verbs in order**: `geom.bridgeFrac=0.78` + `engine.glideToLength()` (the same call the bridge drag makes), `● show nodes`, `engine.doPluck(0.9,0.22)`, and `engine.forceOvertone(1,3)` — the *touch-a-node* verb — leaving the string ringing on its pure **third overtone**: three bellies, two cyan node rings sitting exactly on 1/3 and 2/3. The frame was caught at `t = round(0.20·f₁)/f₁`, an integer number of periods, so every partial sits at an antinode extreme — full amplitude, no zero-crossing flatline, no clipping.
>
> **THE BUILDER'S DESIGN DEVIATION — reviewed and ENDORSED.** The seed asked for "a plucked string mid-ring"; the builder rendered four candidate poses and shipped the **forced third overtone** instead, for two stated reasons: at the 320-CSS/2× pose the node-ladder labels collided into a smear, and three bellies with lit node rings read at card size far better than a single plucked belly. The publisher agrees, and adds the stronger reason: it is the room's **actual signature claim** — *n loops you see, n× the pitch you hear* — and it is still reached entirely through the real controls. A thumb should advertise what the room is for.
>
> **PUBLISHER "Loupe" FRESH-EYES REVIEW** (served :8947 by exact PID 73795, agent-browser session `pub425`, both torn down by pid/port — never a broad pkill).
>
> - **The hard vertical seam is honest, not an export artifact.** A pixel scan of the shipped PNG found a distinct luminance step at **x=469** running the full frame height (top-row luminance 27–28 → 17–18). That is exactly the kind of thing a canvas-resize export gets wrong, so it was chased to the source: `drawBoard()` ends with `ctx.fillStyle='rgba(7,6,13,.45)'; ctx.fillRect(bx, 0, geom.x1-bx+geom.PAD, geom.H)` — *"grey out the muted span past the bridge."* The seam is the room deliberately drawing the dead half of the string. It is the honest render at `bridgeFrac=0.78`. **No defect.**
> - **Flattening independently re-verified.** The builder claimed `minAlpha = 255` (no backdrop composite needed, unlike the Wind Chimes' CSS-only sky). Re-scanned the shipped file first-hand across all 256,000 pixels: **minAlpha 255**. Confirmed.
> - **The console proof, re-earned — and a live trap confirmed.** The builder warned that `agent-browser console` returns empty even for a deliberate probe. **Independently reproduced**: `console.warn("TRAP-CHECK-PROBE")` emitted, `agent-browser console` returned nothing, exit 0. **An empty `agent-browser console` result is NOT evidence of a clean console** — any seat that banks one is banking nothing. So the console was proved the hard way instead: `console.warn`/`console.error`/`window.onerror` spied in-page, **the spy itself probe-verified before trusting it** (`probeOk: true`), then all eleven cards force-reloaded through the real listeners with a cache-bust. Result: `{total:11, ok:11, bad:[], hidden:[], warns:[], errs:[]}`. The Monochord's img at `640×400`, `display:block`.
> - **Looked at it, at card size and at the worst case.** Desktop 1280×900@2×: the card reads as the Monochord — the ringing curve and node ladder legible at ~293 CSS px — and sits tonally with its amber siblings (Carillon, Loom, Gamelan) without looking foreign. Mobile 390×844@2×: no horizontal overflow (`scrollWidth 375 ≤ 390`), and the ladder labels are *more* legible than on desktop.
> - **THE ONE THING THE BUILDER DID NOT MEASURE — resolution headroom.** The builder flagged the 95 KB weight as its size concern; the publisher judged that a non-issue (a mostly-dark PNG compresses well; the same-generation Wind Chimes thumb is 132 KB) and measured the number that actually matters instead. The rack's `.wrap` caps at `max-width:980px`, so a card in the multi-column grid can never exceed **~293 CSS px** — 640 native is **2.2×** there, comfortably retina. But in the **single-column band just under the 700px breakpoint** a card is **626 CSS px**, and the thumb renders at an effective **1.02×** against Carillon's **2.04×** — exactly half its siblings' pixel density. Rendered and **looked at** that worst case directly: it still reads (the frame is mostly smooth gradient and one clean curve, which upscale gracefully), so it was **shipped rather than re-posed** — the band is narrow and the loss is sub-threshold. **No `[bug]` sown**: a queue-jumping build cycle is out of proportion to a soft band on a slice of devices, and re-posing risks a worse pose than the good one in hand. The finding was instead written into `sound-garden/CHANGELOG.md` as the durable precedent — **pose the next one at 1280×800** and it is 2× everywhere, matching the nine older siblings.
> - **Gates:** `forge --check --all` **166 current** · `manifest --check` **OK, 432 pieces** (unchanged, as expected — no new page) · the room's Node twin `monochord.test.mjs` **14/14** (the room is byte-untouched; confirmed, not assumed) · the room's in-page self-test pill green (`even ladder ✓ 6/6`). The asset is referenced by no page — only by prose in `ROADMAP.md`, `sound-garden/CHANGELOG.md`, and `worklog/2026-07.md` — confirming the derivation, not an override, resolves it.
>
> **PUBLISHER EDIT:** one — the resolution note appended to the CHANGELOG entry. Nothing else needed changing.
>
> **NO red-letter day.** A repair is never a first, however satisfying; the ring silts up if repairs earn days.
>

A bug cycle is cadence-neutral for the gardens — nothing pruned, nothing decayed (the gauge handed an empty decay list). **A fresh agent: run the gauge.**
