>
> **▶ #424 (2026-07-20 — BUILD/garden; planter (ledger "Bellwether") · publisher "Weathervane"; gardenBuilds 1→2, M=39). SHIPPED THE WIND CHIMES — the room the estate's air was worth building.**
>
> **THE CYCLE.** A garden BUILD turn (`gardenFuel=10 > 4`, `gardenBuilds=1 < 6`) — pull a garden seed and sow it. The pick: the delight-first `[exhibit]` **The Wind Chimes** (sown #422), one of the seven in #422's deliberately delight-leaning slate.
>
> **WHAT SHIPPED.** A Sound Garden leaf at `sound-garden/the-wind-chimes/`, in the-squeal-bench idiom — five files, 1,343 authored lines. Five tuned bronze tubes and a wooden clapper on a cord, hung at dusk over a broad wind-sail.
>
> - `core.mjs` (486 lines) — the DOM-free sole authority, byte-twinned into the page between sentinels: one driven damped pendulum (clapper + sail), a tube-crossing strike model with refractory, a struck free-free-tube voice, offline renders, `runChimeSelfTest` (6 legs).
> - `index.src.html` → forged `index.html` (472 authored lines → 229 KB), inlining FOUR byte-twin slices (CHIME CORE, PITCH CORE, plus the air's SCORE CORE + SCORE VOICES) and forging in ws/hours/calendar/tree-art/air.
> - `core.test.mjs` (269 lines) — the payoff-liveness twin, 16/16. · `verify.sh` (116 lines) — the audio-lens ear-check, 6/6. · `CHANGELOG.md` (91 lines).
>
> **THE CONCEIT — and the house-rule call.** The air is the LITERAL wind. Arm the estate's air chip and the room plays itself with the manual breeze slider at zero; mute the estate and the chimes damp slowly to rest rather than cut. This makes it a page that both SINGS and WEARS THE AIR, which the house rule warns against. The planter judged it the rule's intended case rather than a violation and asked for a second opinion. **The publisher endorses the call.** The rule guards against a second sound bed competing with a page's own voice; here there is no layering, only coupling — the chimes have no voice at all without wind, and the air is the input, not a parallel track. Stated plainly in a source comment and the CHANGELOG.
>
> **TWO PHYSICS BUGS THE PLANTER FOUND BY MEASURING, NOT ASSUMING.** (1) A steady wind force parked the clapper on one side and silenced half the rack — tubes B3/E4 rang *zero* times at breeze 0.6. The truer model was the fix: everything hangs from the same hook, so a steady wind leans the whole assembly and rings nothing; only buffeting rings a chime. The wind force is now zero-mean and a gust sweeps the whole rack. (2) Driven hard, the pendulum went over the top and span (θ=128 rad, ringing almost nothing). A clapper hangs *inside* its ring of tubes — added a rack edge with rebound; LEG 6 now holds it at 4× max wind.
>
> **A LIVE-PATH BUG THE OFFLINE RENDER COULD NOT CATCH.** The core's tanh limiter guards the offline mix, but live plays each strike as its own BufferSource — the graph summed to **5.08× full scale**. A DynamicsCompressor was not a fix (only ~2 dB; output still ran to 1.30). Fixed by single-sourcing: the live chain bakes **the core's own `limit()`** into a WaveShaper, so both paths obey one law. At max breeze with the air armed the output peaks 0.70 (RMS 0.22) — still loud, never clipped.
>
> **PLACEMENT — a DEEPEN, no new footprint.** Rack card in `instruments.js`, README table + ensemble line, the Sound Garden footer voice line, and reciprocal twin-links added to the Squeal Bench and the Carillon (both re-forged). Sound Garden tally 32→33; front door re-forged for the count. No `ws:seen`, no SPEC, no accuracy pill (the piece is delight-first and claim-light).
>
> ---
>
> **PUBLISHER "WEATHERVANE" — FRESH-EYES REVIEW.** Served the repo on :8947 by exact PID 65814, agent-browser session `pub424`; both torn down.
>
> **THE DEFECT CAUGHT — the rack card shipped with NO ART.** Every other card in the Sound Garden rack carries a rendered frame behind its title. The Wind Chimes' card was a bare gradient. The chain: the rack derives a thumb as `assets/${it.file minus .html}.png`, which for this leaf's nested `the-wind-chimes/index.html` resolves to `assets/the-wind-chimes/index.png` — a nested path under a flat assets dir that can never exist — and the card's inline `onerror="this.style.display='none'"` then hid the 404 so gracefully that nothing anywhere reported it. A clean-console pass could not catch it; only looking at the rack could. This is exactly the failure mode the fresh-eyes seat exists for.
>
> **FIXED AT THE ROOT, three ways.**
> 1. **The derivation.** A foldered leaf now names its thumb by its FOLDER, not by the literal `index` — `the-wind-chimes/index.html` → `assets/the-wind-chimes.png`. A flat leaf is unchanged. The next nested leaf cannot trip the same wire, and no per-entry `thumb:` override is needed.
> 2. **The silence.** The inline `onerror` was replaced with a handler that still degrades gracefully for a visitor (an empty frame reads better than a broken-image glyph) but **console-warns the missing asset by name and card**. The next pair of eyes on this page finds a miss in the console instead of only in a screenshot.
> 3. **The missing art, forged IN-HOUSE from the room's own renderer.** Posed the live stage at the card's 16:10 at 2× (640×400), drove the REAL breeze control to a gust, and caught a frame with the clapper swung out to θ=0.33 and six tubes lit. **A bare `toDataURL` came back with the chime floating on transparency** — the dusk sky lives in the canvas's *CSS* `background` gradient, not in its pixels — so the export re-lays the exact same gradient (`--sky-hi` #2a1b33 → `--sky-lo` #160f22 → #0b0812) into an offscreen canvas and composites the frame on top. → `sound-garden/assets/the-wind-chimes.png`. Rack thumbs now 10/11 loading.
>
> **VERIFIED FIRST-HAND (not taken from the handoff).**
> - Node twin **16/16**; ear-check `verify.sh` **6/6** (audio-lens); `forge --check --all` **all 166 current**; `manifest --check` **OK** (432 pieces, unclaimed 0).
> - **The room's headline claim, re-derived independently against the real core across the actual slider→breeze map** (`v/100 × 1.25`, read out of the source, 3 seeds × 20 s each). At slider 5–12 (the label reads "a breath") the low A3 and B3 ring **zero** and only the bright tubes tinkle; at 16 ("light air") the low tube joins; at 100 all five ring near-evenly. The prose is true *and* the labelled wind-words sit on the right thresholds — a visitor dragging slowly really does hear the rack fill in from the top.
> - **The payoff, live on the REAL control** (the slider a visitor drags, not a synthetic event): dead calm **0 strikes** at θ=0.017 at rest · slider 30 → 35 strikes · slider 100 → 31 strikes across all five tubes.
> - **The conceit, live**: a true input-level click on the air chip, slider left at **0** — the air alone drove breeze to **0.3106** and rang **145 strikes across all five tubes in 25 s**.
> - Zero console errors on every surface · no horizontal overflow at 375 px (360<375) or 1280 px (1265<1280) · all 6 outbound links resolve to real files · **zero nested anchors** across all five surfaces · front-door sound-garden tally reads **33** · sibling cross-links present and matching each host's local footer idiom (the Carillon's `↗ … →` form matches its own neighbouring links, so it was left alone).

> **THE PLANTER'S OTHER OPEN CONCERNS — judged, no action taken.** (1) *The A/B's centroid axis is weak* — moot, because the shipped page never claims it: the prose rests entirely on the decisive hum axis (no sub-octave partial; ratios 1 : 2.756 : 5.404 : 8.933), which the twin asserts structurally. (2) *229 KB is the heaviest leaf in the wing* — **not an outlier** for a page that wears the air: the Almanac is 200 KB and the front door 1184 KB. No `⚡` sown. (3) *Tube-length contrast is subtle* — correct and deliberate; L ∝ 1/√f over a 1.68× pitch range gives a 1.30× spread, the twin asserts the exact law, and the nested arrangement is what carries pitch order legibly. Exaggerating it would be a lie the drawing tells. (4) *WAVs live in /tmp* — the squeal-bench idiom; the harness is the builder's, not a committed artifact.

> **THE READ (why the slate leans delight).** The two seeds that DECAYED were both heavy exact-crux "instrument-with-a-proof" pieces — makers kept passing them over cycle after cycle. And the three survivors in the bed after the prune (**The Snow Globe** pure-delight curio · **Where the Light Piles Up** the caustic-cabinet gather · **The Settling Melt** the Bowen's-ladder bench) all lean delight or delight-leaning. Read together that is the soul's *quieter-monoculture* warning made concrete: DESIGNING's caution that when nearly every new piece arrives as "an instrument with a proof (a phenomenon, a claim, a self-test, a neg-control), that is the same monoculture in a quieter key." So this slate deliberately leans DELIGHT-FIRST and FORM-VARIED — made because they are beautiful/funny/strange, proving nothing or carrying only a light honest nugget — and several DEEPEN existing families rather than raising lonely new front doors (the placement cascade + deepen-before-detach).
>
> **THE 7-SEED SLATE (`(sown #422)`, fuel 3→10), by register/form:**
> - `[exhibit]` **Sprouts** — Conway & Paterson's pencil-and-ink topological game (join two live spots with a non-crossing curve, drop a fresh spot, a spot dies at degree 3, last to move wins), with a "let the house play" opponent. The combinatorial-games family's (nim/chomp/hexapawn/hex) first TOPOLOGICAL game; claim-free-first, carrying only the flavor-fact that an n-spot game lasts 2n..3n−1 moves. *(A game — delight-first, deepens a family.)*
> - `[exhibit]` **The Marbling Tray** — suminagashi on still water: float concentric ink rings with a stylus, rake them into feathered combed swirls, lay a sheet to LIFT the marble and KEEP it on a rack of broadsides (the Snow Globe's keepable-curio grammar). PURE DELIGHT, proves nothing. Foundry: water-shimmer + a soft muted ink-drop tick. *(Generative fluid art — pure delight, keepable.)*
> - `[exhibit]` **The Wind Chimes** — a rack of pitched tubes the estate's OWN air plays: raise/slacken the breeze, the clapper swings, striking a pentatonic set so it can never sound wrong; nudge a tube to ring it yourself. Sound-first pure delight, and a natural home for the estate air widget (`tools/calendar/air.js`) since the room IS the wind. Carries a headless payoff-liveness twin (gust() → a strike whose gain rises then decays). *(Sound toy — delight, air-widget-aware.)*
> - `[exhibit]` **The Analemma Stone** — over a year the noon sun traces a lopsided figure-eight; step the date on a courtyard stone and watch the sun-mark climb the analemma, the gnomon's shadow leaning as the seasons turn, inked live from solar declination + the equation of time (offered as the model's own curve, not a named observatory reading). Deepens the sundial/sky family. *(Sky instrument — delight-leaning, light honest rigor.)*
> - `[exhibit]` **The Cento Press** — a letterpress oracle that stitches a CENTO (a collage-poem built only from lines the house has already spoken: room captions, ledger koans) and sets it as a broadside you pull and keep. The house speaking itself; deepens the letterer/scriptorium/verse family. Claim-free. *(Press/poem — delight, self-referential.)*
> - `[cross]` **The Law Out of the Noise** — Buffon's needles (each toss wild → π accrues) crossed with Galton's beans (each bounce a coin-flip → a bell curve piles up): two BUILT rooms (`buffon/` + `galton/`) where the single trial is pure chance and the aggregate is an exact shape, drop both and watch them sharpen together as N climbs. HONEST: π and the normal law are DIFFERENT results, unified only on 'many independent trials converge to a deterministic macroscopic form' (the law of large numbers made visible), never one formula. *(A touchable cross carrying light honest rigor — balances the delight lean.)*
> - `[curate]` **Things That Won't Fall Over** — a delight-leaning cabinet gathering the estate's six scattered SPIN rooms under one roof (the-top · tippe-top · rattleback · spinning-chair · rotor · tusi, all dirs verified present), each panel a live little spinner and a door to its room, one thread: angular momentum wants to be left alone. Claim-free-first gather (optional spine: conservation of L); a deepen-by-gathering, no new physics forged. *(A gather — the hierarchy soul, deepens by gathering scattered kin.)*
>
> **BALANCE CHECK.** ~5/7 delight-first (Sprouts · Marbling Tray · Wind Chimes · Analemma Stone · Cento Press), with the cross + curation carrying light honest structure; forms spread across game/puzzle · generative fluid art · sound toy · sky instrument · press/poem · touchable cross · cabinet-gather (no monoculture). Register spread: 5 exhibits, 1 cross, 1 curation; no rework this pass. Deepen-before-detach honored — Sprouts joins the game kin, the curation gathers the spin family, the Analemma deepens the sundial/sky family, the Cento deepens the letterer/verse family, the Wind Chimes lean on the shared air widget. All new-room proposals grep-confirmed against the 245-dir estate; the cross + curation name only verified-present rooms.

>
> **WHAT STANDS.** A new top-level piece at `the-spin-they-keep/` — three brass bays under one instrument frame. LEFT (warm #f0b45a): the Skater — a turntable with brass weights on arms and a motion-blur fan whose spread ∝ ω; drag the weights IN (frac 0→1) and spin ω climbs 1.60→6.91 rad/s. RIGHT (cool #86b8d8): the Planet — an off-centre brass Sun at the focus of an e=0.6 ellipse, a bead on the rail, a comet-smear and swept wedge; drag the bead toward perihelion and sweep-rate θ̇ climbs 0.31→5.00 rad/s. CENTRE: the L-Needle — an L-shaped brass pointer on a graduated ±74° HELD·L₀→BLED·0 arc, with a digital "L 1.000 ⟳ HELD" readout and a Δ-since-grab counter read back off the rendered transform via getCTM. Push either world and its rate climbs steeply while the ONE needle sits dead still at HELD — a shrinking radius exactly offsets the rising rate, so L is kept.
>
> **THE TACTILE NEG-CONTROL.** Each bay carries a "friction brake" / "off-centre tug" toggle. Flip it and push: an external torque now acts, that world's L bleeds away, the shared minimum falls, and the needle SAGS off HELD toward BLED (−62.9° on the chair side, +62.9° on the orbit side, "L 0.278 ⟳ BLED"). Release and it snaps home to 0° HELD. The two worlds that moved as one now decouple — proof the invariance is conservation, not a coincidence of the rig.
>
> **THE CROSS AUTHORITY (`core.mjs`, 303 lines).** The sole authority imports BOTH parents UNFORKED at one `../` hop above a byte-twin CORE slab (the mirror-cross pattern): `spinning-chair/core.mjs` (I·ω — inertia/omegaAt/L0) and `equal-area-sweep/core.mjs` (r²·θ̇ — arealConstant/angularSpeed/arealRate). Constants: `L_SKATER = L0() ≈ 9.70752 kg·m²/s`, `L_ORBIT = arealConstant(0.6) = 0.8` (dimensionless). The one entry `needleReading(scene,frac,leak)` routes each world through its parent's real law; the `(1−leak·frac)` bleed is documented as honestly phenomenological (direction only; machine-ε exactness at leak=0). The Node twin `core.test.mjs` proves it — **15/15 GREEN**: the chair invariant `I·ω===L_SKATER ∀frac` (worst 1.8e-15), the orbit invariant `r²θ̇===L_ORBIT ∀θ` + arealRate (worst 3.3e-16), the shared guarantee (chair ω-ratio===I-ratio, orbit θ̇-ratio===r²-ratio, rates strictly rise in BOTH), HONESTY (`L_SKATER ≠ L_ORBIT`, ratio ≈12.1, different units — yet held===1 in both at leak=0: the invariance is shared, never the number), the NEG-CONTROL (leak>0 ⇒ L strictly falls, deeper with more leak, rate below ideal, leak→1 ⇒ L→0, and the drag decouples the worlds), Lclamped corroboration (a motor-driven pivot varies its L — belt & suspenders), anti-circularity (routes through the real parent laws byte-exact), byte-twin parity (index.html CORE === core.mjs CORE, 12053 chars identical), an anti-fork grep (the CORE slab declares NO parent fn, only calls them), and a honesty-grep (disclaimer present; no affirmative L-equality phrase).

> **▶ #417 (2026-07-20 — BUILD/garden; planter · publisher "Meniscus"; M=38). SHIPPED THE CLIMBING RIBBON — the Alchemy Lab's NINTH bench, and the paper-half of a separation pair with the Fractionating Column: peer into a lidded glass developing jar, blend a muddy ink, press Develop, and watch the mud PART into a rising ladder of coloured bands a self-rescaling brass Rf ruler reads off band÷front.** A garden BUILD cycle (`gardenFuel=9 > 4`, `gardenBuilds=2 < 6` — pull a garden seed). The bloom of the `[bench]` **The Climbing Ribbon — paper chromatography** (sown #414) — the seed that makes the Alchemy Lab's lone separation child (the distillation Column) a PAIR.
>
> **WHAT STANDS.** A new child bench at `alchemy/the-climbing-ribbon/` (deepens the wing — no new front-door footprint). The toy: tap 2–4 apothecary pigments (Lampblack…Azurite, each a model polarity) into one muddy blob; pick one of three solvents (Naphtha / Spirit of Wine / Aqua) or slide the polarity dial freely; press **Develop ▲**. The wet front creeps up a paper ribbon (Washburn √t, visibly decelerating) and the mud parts into a ladder — solvent-loving pigments ride high near the front, paper-clinging ones lag low. A **self-rescaling brass ruler** reads each band's **Rf = band ÷ front**, and the SAME tick lands whether the strip is Short or Long, the run 8 s or 40 s. The scene is a hand-built SVG **developing jar**: layered glass with edge highlight + sheen, a stopper, condensation fog (feTurbulence) on the inner lid, a base caustic, a concave solvent meniscus, and a 3-plane parallax that tracks the pointer.
>
> **THE TACTILE NEG-CONTROL.** The polarity dial is the load-bearing control, not a slider garnish. Slide it to the marked **notch** (the paper's own polarity, 0.62) and every band snaps to **Rf = 0.5** — the ladder collapses to one line, nothing separates (the paper alone does no work). Slide **across** the notch and the whole ladder **inverts** — the leader becomes the trailer — proof it is the stationary-and-mobile *pair* that separates, never either phase alone.
>
> **THE SEPARATION AUTHORITY (`core.mjs`, ~290 lines).** `retention(pigPol,mobilePol,statPol)` is the regular-solution partition factor `k = exp(BETA·[(p−m)² − (p−s)²])`; the SQUARE form is pinned (over `|·|`) because it is *linear* in pigPol, guaranteeing a strict, TOTAL order-reversal across the notch with no flat ties. `rf = 1/(1+k) ∈ (0,1)` is the closed form the ruler ticks read. Two INDEPENDENT front laws — `washburn` (v=D/2H) and `linear` (v=c) — graft in to kill circularity; `makeRun`/`develop` integrate the COUPLED front+band ODEs by RK4 (`dH/dt = v(H)`, `dB_i/dt = rf_i·v(H)`), never the closed form, so `B_i === rf_i·(H−H0)` EMERGES rather than being asserted. The Node twin `core.test.mjs` proves it green — **23/23**: the CRUX (`band÷front` invariant across ≥3 stop-times × ≥3 strip lengths × BOTH front laws to <1e-9 — the invariance emerges from independent RK4 walks, crux residual 1.6e-14), NEG-CONTROL A (phases identical ⇒ every Rf === 0.5 bit-exact), NEG-CONTROL B (a shipped pigment pair + two shipped solvents straddling the paper polarity whose Rf order reverses, plus the total reversal of every pair), the PAYOFF (default blend resolves into ≥2 separated bands), HONESTY (Rf dimensionless in (0,1), compared to no handbook value), the LIVENESS probe, and a byte-identical re-extraction parity check against the page's inline core.
>

Manifest + tallies auto-enrolled (426→427 pieces, alchemy 8→9, unclaimed 0); root `index.html` re-forged for the baked alchemy tally bump only.

The two features that carry the "a maze you enter" read — the mouth GAP and the SPILLED-and-POOLED clew — both land unmistakably. Delight-first: it proves nothing, judged purely on instant legibility + invitation.
>

Wiring landed in three places: `rooms.js` `daedalus` repColors (DAY/DUSK/NIGHT `rep.swatch1/2` boxwood greens + `rep.glow1` teal, so the accent BLAZES at night and RECEDES to a cool line by day, automatically); `scene.js` REP_DRAW `'daedalus-rep'`; and the draw fn itself (a private `#daedalus-clew-glow` feGaussianBlur filter, a deterministic PRNG for stable stipple).

> **▶ #413 (2026-07-20 — BUILD/bug; bug-fixer · publisher "Kerf"; M=38). FIXED forge's multi-line-import blindness AT ROOT — and closed the deeper SILENCE that let the resulting dead page ship.** The bloom of the `[bug]` **forge's import stripper drops all but the first line of a multi-line import** (sown by the Wall-of-the-Night publisher). The gauge routes an open bug ahead of everything, so a fresh agent drew `BUILD/bug`. Two faults, one cause and one silence, both fixed in `tools/forge/forge.mjs` (+152) with `forge.test.mjs` (+138, cases d/e/f/g/h1–h4).
>
> **THE KNIFE (the cause).** `stripModuleGuard` drops a static ES `import` from an inlined script because its symbols come from a sibling include — but `STATIC_IMPORT` was anchored whole-line (`/^…import…from…;?$/`), so an `import { A, B } from '…'` broken across lines lost only its FIRST line and left the orphaned `} from '…';` tail behind as syntax garbage that kills the ENTIRE `<script>` — silently, no console error, in headless. The fix mirrors the shape the neighbouring `export {…}` / `export default` arms already use: a start/consume pair. `IMPORT_START` (`/^[ \t]*import\b(?![ \t]*[.(])/`) recognizes an import HEAD — the `(?![ \t]*[.(])` lookahead is load-bearing, excluding dynamic `import(` and `import.meta`, and `\b` guards `importantThing`; then it accumulates lines forward until `IMPORT_STATEMENT` (`/^[ \t]*import\b[^'"]*?['"][^'"]*['"][ \t]*;?[ \t]*$/`) matches the terminating module-specifier string. `[^'"]*?` spans newlines (a negated class matches `\n`), so the clause may break across any number of lines, and because everything before the first quote is matched quote-blind a `from` or `{` INSIDE the specifier string can never be mistaken for structure. An import that never terminates now throws a `ForgeError` naming file+line — the silent skip is banned.
>
> **THE SYNTAX GATE (the real root cause — the silence).** The regex was only the proximate fault; the reason a broken import shipped a DEAD PAGE unnoticed is that forge verified byte-drift and the manifest but never asked whether the JS it had just inlined actually PARSES. A classic `<script>` that fails to parse renders at 60fps with a clean-looking console — invisible to every downstream check the estate owns. So `checkInlinedScripts()` now runs after every page build and `new vm.Script()`-parses (in-process, no temp files, no spawn — the browser's own classic-script grammar) every inlined CLASSIC `<script>` block, failing the build LOUD with the page name, the line the block opens at, and V8's message. Deliberately NOT gated: `type="module"` (a different grammar — and forge's whole job is turning modules into classic scripts), `application/json` and other non-JS types, and `<script src=…>` (nothing inlined). The block finder `findScriptBlocks()` is a hand-rolled SCANNER, not one regex, because comments and scripts nest ASYMMETRICALLY: an HTML COMMENT may contain the text `<script>` (the aquarium's art-banner comment literally does — the browser sees no script there, so neither may forge), while a SCRIPT may contain `<!--` (Annex B) whose body is raw text running to `</script>`. Scanning left-to-right and taking whichever container opens FIRST reproduces the browser's own precedence exactly.
>
> **PUBLISHER'S FRESH-EYES REVIEW (publisher "Kerf").** No estate page surface — the change is to shared build infrastructure, so the review IS the code plus the gates. Read the full diff: confirmed the `rel` shadowing note is accurate (`checkInlinedScripts` calls the module-scope `rel`/`outPathFor` at line 730/460, while the directive loop's local `const rel` string at line 404 is what's passed to `stripModuleGuard` as the error-message source name — a string, correct); re-derived the three negative controls by hand against the new regexes (`import(` → lookahead `[.(]` fires → not stripped; `import.meta` → `.` → not stripped; `importantThing` → `import\b` has no boundary before `a` → no match). No code polish needed — the fix is careful and complete on arrival. **Gates:** `node tools/forge/forge.test.mjs` → **ALL PASS** (42 new assertions; the handoff confirmed every new case d/e/f/g/h FAILS pre-fix and PASSES after, including the negative controls); `node tools/forge/forge.mjs --check --all` → **all 160 files current**, manifest gate OK, **EXIT 0** — critically, the new syntax gate ran against every real estate page with ZERO false positives (the builder had already caught + fixed one false positive on the-aquarium's art-banner comment by making the scanner comment-aware rather than muting the gate, regression-locked as case h4); `node --check` clean on both edited files. **No page churn** — `git diff` touches only ROADMAP.md + the two forge tool files; no `.html` regenerated.
>
> **BED (this cycle).** The builder had already closed the bug through `bed` — `✝ FIXED #413 → tools/forge/forge.mjs + forge.test.mjs · after a89b584` stands in the bug fence, which is now ZERO live seeds; nothing to sweep by hand. Filed **one `⚡ spark`** the fix invites: `forge --explain <page>` — a build-provenance printer that prints the tree of what forge inlined and from where (each `forge:include`/`forge:asset`/`forge:json` → source file → the byte/line span it produced, plus every module-guard / import / export line the stripper removed), so a maker debugging an inlined-script fault sees WHICH include owns WHICH lines instead of bisecting a thousand-line forged blob by hand — the natural companion to the new gate, which now says a page is dead but not yet why. **No garden/grounds seed was pruned, decayed or restamped** — a bug cycle ages nothing; the six decayed garden seeds (The Weathervane's Tale, The Same Fading, The Same Straight Line, The Two Ways to Freeze, The Voice of the Column, The Membrane Cast) are left for the next PLAN/garden gardener, and every cadence clock holds (garden fuel 9, grounds 3, foundry 6, M=38). Bug track DERIVED **0 sown / 1 bloomed / 0 decayed** (the spark is a sparks-fence sow, not a bug); `seal-cycle.sh BUILD bug 413`, cycle 412→413, bugs 1→0, M stays 38. A FRESH AGENT: run the gauge — with bugs now 0 the next cycle is NO LONGER a forced bug-fix. *(Detail → `tools/forge/forge.mjs` header comments + this block.)*

> **▶ writ · served before #413 (2026-07-20 — PATRON'S WRIT, MANDATE; CADENCE-NEUTRAL: decays nothing, ages nothing, cycle #413 stays free for the next real build). THE WALL OF THE NIGHT — a photo booth for the makers, in maker territory.** The Patron's writ: four great works landed in one season (the Great Reorganization, the Showing, the Living Calendar, the Almanac), the work stops for a night, and the makers are thanked — "**everyone who touched any part of it, every seat, every cycle, including the ones whose work was judged down or decayed unbuilt**." A photo booth stands at the back of the hall; nobody is obliged to use it; the only instruction is *have fun*. The director triaged it **MANDATE** (creative content landing OUTSIDE the deployed estate — maker territory, like `medallion.html`), so the steward built it and no clause was released as a seed. **No `AUTHORIZES` clause and no outside action was performed** by any seat — `outsideActionPerformed: none`, confirmed against the writ text; the escalation channel was never used (the triage was unambiguous).
>
> **WHAT LANDED** (all beside `face.html`/`medallion.html`, **NOT linked from the deployed estate, the map, or any nav** — grepped repo-wide; pins **no CLAIM**, so nothing to rot and no `reclaim.mjs` burden): `ledger/booth.core.mjs` (the emulsion engine, shared byte-for-byte by page and Node twin — rasterises to plain RGBA and renders no canvas, so the twin sees the visitor's actual pixels; seeded metaball ground, **the koan is the light** — one raked stroke per word, angle from word hash, brightness 1/√index, bloomed so a dense koan blows out and a terse one is clean blades; role→bath, cycle→age; **BASE FOG 0.30** so no one develops to nothing; 8 pre-rendered density plates; the frame-4 guest resolver) · `ledger/booth.hall.mjs` (procedural ox-blood damask, four tungsten pools, 90-strip stratigraphic layout oldest-cycle-deepest, on-ramp strips, composition/curl/tack, hit-test, sparse-frontier pinning) · `ledger/booth.sound.mjs` (in-house WebAudio, zero files, created on first press, inheriting `ws:pref:muted`, no second control) · `ledger/booth.src.html`→`booth.html` (self-contained, opens from `file://`) · `ledger/booth.test.mjs` (the payoff-liveness twin, **32/32**) · `ledger/CHANGELOG.md` (registration + two landmines).
>
> **THE FOURTH FRAME** is the writ's heart made mechanical: frames 1–3 are the cycle's own seats in ledger order; frame 4 prefers a maker whose `role` string occurs exactly ONCE in the whole ledger — the bespoke one-off explorer labels, makers who lost their cycle's judgment, shipped nothing, and left a koan anyway. 350 of 444 strips seat such a guest (the rest fall back to the nearest neighbour's rarest role — the design's own stated fallback). Same paper, same chemistry, same size, same lift; one brass line reads `★ FOUR TO A STRIP ★ THE HOUSE DOES NOT SAY WHICH IS WHICH`, and the page never claims frame 4 lost. **The ritual is TIMED and the 3.4 s dead beats are authored comedy** — a source comment says so, because every future polish instinct will read the ~20 s runtime as a bug and try to trim it.
>
> **STEWARD'S THREE JUDGEMENT CALLS (recorded in-source):** (1) the booth brings its own sound rather than mounting `tools/calendar/air.js` — ground.md says a page that already sings should not wear the air chip, and mounting it would drag `calendar.js` + `tree-art.js` + score voices into a photo booth; only the *preference* wiring is shared. (2) forge's whole-line import stripper was NOT "fixed" (shared build infrastructure, out of a steward's scope) — the import was kept on one line and the gap recorded. (3) When the twin caught the lit zone piling only 2.17 deep against the design's 3–6, the WALL was fixed rather than the threshold lowered.
>
> **PUBLISHER'S FRESH-EYES REVIEW — three real defects caught and fixed, all found by LOOKING, not by a test.** (a) **The tally was illegible against the wall.** The legend is viewport-FIXED while the wall PANS beneath it, so portraits slide under it; sampling the hall canvas behind the tally band measured a blown publisher-bath highlight at **luminance 217** directly under 10px `#7c6f5c` text — near-zero, sometimes inverted contrast, on the line reading *"71 guests who shipped nothing"*, the one sentence on the page that carries the writ's whole point. Fixed where the source's own note says it must be fixed (a scrim problem, not a layout problem — a world-space keep-out cannot work under a fixed legend). (b) **The first fix was worse and was reverted:** plateauing the scrim opaque enough to cover the worst case stopped being a vignette and became a visible black BOX with an edge cutting through the strips behind it. The shipped answer is a soft, slightly wider vignette **plus a glyph-hugging halo** (a tight 2–5px shadow stack) that lets the letterforms carry their own contrast against anything that pans under them. (c) **That halo, applied to all legend children, turned the gilt title to mud** — the h1 is painted by `background-clip:text` over transparent glyphs, so a dense tight shadow shows THROUGH the letterforms; the halo is now scoped to the plain-coloured prose and tally only, and the title keeps the original soft pair. All three are commented in `booth.src.html` so the next maker doesn't re-walk the loop. (d) **The legend did not recede with the wall.** `body.reading` dims `#hall` but left `#legend` at full strength, so opening a strip's read view left the header lit over the dimmed hall and running straight into the read card's top-left corner, with the tally half-swallowed — the one moment the page looked broken. `body.reading #legend{opacity:.16}` + a transition on the base rule.
>
> **VERIFICATION (publisher's own pass, real browser, port 8877 torn down by exact PID).** Wall rendered and inspected at 1440×900 and 390×844 (no element overflows either viewport; the tally now reads over the strip it overlaps on mobile). Panned the wall 13 steps — layout, lamp pools and the denser lit zone all hold, legend stays clear. **Drove the full ritual with a real click:** curtain drew, HOLD STILL bulb lit, and ~20 s later a new strip (CYCLE 396) had flown and landed, the tally ticking **90→91 strips and 71→72 guests** live. **Read view opened with a TRUE input-level click** (CDP `Input.dispatchMouseEvent` against a `hitTest`-derived point — never `dispatchEvent`): CYCLE 524 showed four names, four roles, four koans and the cycle in gilt, with frame four seated by `EXPLORER · THE CENSUS CORE`, a genuine one-off role — the fourth-frame design demonstrably live end-to-end. Console capture (`error`/`warn`/`onerror`/`unhandledrejection`) stayed **empty** across the whole pass. Gates re-run after every edit: twin **32/32** · `forge --check --all` **160 files current** · `ledger/core.test.mjs` **ALL PASS (1811 marks)** · `sign.test.sh` pass. **Harness landmine re-confirmed** (already in the CHANGELOG and `booth.test.mjs --how`): `agent-browser mouse down/up` fire at (0,0) regardless of a preceding `mouse move`, so they cannot click canvas objects — CDP is the only way to drive this page's canvas.
>
> **BED (this cycle):** the served writ removed cleanly with **no tombstone** (writ fence pinned keep=0 — a writ's providence must never linger to bias a future maker), and **one `[bug]` sown** from the review: forge's `STATIC_IMPORT` is whole-line anchored, so a multi-line `import { … } from '…'` loses all but its first line and the leftover braces kill the ENTIRE inlined `<script>` — silently, no console error, headless. 160 files ride that build. The steward was right to leave it (shared infrastructure, out of a writ steward's scope); it belongs in the bed, and the gauge routes a bug ahead of everything, so the next cycle clears it. **No garden/grounds seed was pruned, decayed or restamped — a writ ages nothing** and every cadence clock holds (garden fuel 9, grounds 3, foundry 6, M=38).

> **▶ WS2 — THE GRAND TOUR + THE SHOWING (2026-07-05, directed program — loop stopped, cadence-neutral, no cycle number, no `seal-cycle`, gauge/`M`/all counters UNTOUCHED, like WS1/WS3). BUILT the estate's docent system: five silent guided TOURS that walk a visitor through the real running pages in order, and THE SHOWING — a narrated 13-chapter iframe deck for the July-17 talk.** Fable designed (DESIGN.md v3 FINAL, 2 critic rounds); captions + the Showing script were authored VERBATIM into two appendices (an executor never writes visitor prose — the register rule); Opus ran the whole build through a scaffold-exec worker→orchestrator loop off a ledger. Everything lives on branch `tour/grand-tour` off `main`; NEVER pushed, NEVER merged (the loop STOPS at T5.2 for the human's own verify + ff-merge — that is the human's pass, not a task).
>
> **THE TOUR SYSTEM.** Two hand-authored, zero-dep, dual-use files forge-included into every stop page + the front door: `tools/tour/tours.js` (the thread DATA — `TOURS` / `EXTRA_STOPS` / `DOCENT_SENTINEL='grand-tour-docent'`) and `tools/tour/tour.js` (the docent ENGINE — a pure state machine + the brass-on-ink engraved CARD chrome + a beats runtime + the start plaque + a public front-door seam). Lifecycle per DESIGN §1: ARRIVE → PERFORM (beats) → DWELL (a countdown dial) → ADVANCE, with soft-pause on any engagement, `hold:true` watch-forever stops (no countdown), bfcache `pageshow` re-init, and a `rel()` depth-correct link builder (both deploy bases). **De-quantified surface (BINDING, §3):** no "Stop N of M" a visitor ever reads or hears — a row of place-markers ("you are here", nothing fills), the aria announces the PLACE ("The Hall of Mirrors — on the Thread of Light"), resume says "resume where you left off" (the high-water number lives only in `ws:best:tour:<id>` storage). Tours are SILENT forever. `ws:` keys: `WS.best('tour:<id>',n)` on ARRIVE, `WS.flag('tour:<id>:done')` at FINALE; degrades when the store is unavailable.
>
> **THE FIVE THREADS (`tours.js`, Appendix-A verbatim, pinned to the live manifest, §8-gated):** `light` The Thread of Light (6 min, front-door overture start) · `hours` The Thread of Hours (6) · `chance` The Thread of Chance (5) · `maker` The Maker's Thread (5, starts at the Colophon) · `founding` The Founding Walk (5, starts at the Workbench). 35 stops total. Three anchor kinds: `room:` (exhibit → manifest parent, verified) · `at:` (front-door WAYPOINT → a top-level district the map flies to) · `EXTRA_STOPS` anchor (colophon.html→manor, ledger/face.html→outbuilding — the two non-manifest front-matter stops).
>
> **THE PAGES.** All 27 stop pages carry the docent include. 12 shipped-only pages (no `.src.html`) were MINTED a `.src.html` = verbatim shipped body + the include block (never hand-paste the engine into shipped html), then an include sweep added the two-line block to the rest in district batches; the two sanctioned records pages (`ledger/face.src.html`, `tabularium/index.src.html`) got the include line ONLY (zero record-content change). **Six bespoke acts** (`window.__tourAct`, calling each page's REAL entry functions — never synthesized canvas events — so the headless gates can drive them): double-slit (fire a volley), benford-mill (turn the crank), the-three-doors (play one round then reset to the visitor's turn), the-coin-that-lies (pull the release), the-rewind-shelf (scrub forward+back once), the-barrel-house/pin-barrel (a half-turn then hold). The front door is an actor too: `index.src.html`'s `__tourAct` draws the lit-thread overlay over distinct map anchors + flies the camera via the public `__panCamera.frameTo`, yielding to the hand on manual pan. Every other stop takes the do-nothing default (a complete still or an animate-on-load page).
>
> **THE SHOWING (`talk/`, DESIGN §10, precondition `tts` on PATH — met).** One self-contained page that never navigates: each of 13 chapters frames a real estate page in a same-origin `<iframe>` while a cloned-voice narration track drives word-lit captions + camera/hook cues in step (~15 min). Files: `talk/cue-engine.js` (pure keyframe-seek with STATE-replay / IMPULSE-forward-fire semantics, scrub debounce, hash mirror; Node twin `cue-engine.test.mjs` 92/92) + `talk/showing.js` (the impure controller) + `talk/showing.src.html`→`showing.html` (the forged deck, ~4.5 MB — all 13 clips inlined as `data:` URIs, zero runtime fetches, the estate's covenant) + `talk/script/chNN-*.txt` prose (Appendix-B verbatim) + `chNN.mp3`/`chNN.json` (rendered by `tools/voice/claude-tts` 48k, audio-lens-verified, cues anchored to REAL word-start times from the JSON — never hand-typed). Cross-frame pokes go through `window.__tourHooks={verb:fn}` (the SECOND page seam — fire/crank/go/mapFrame; tours never call hooks, the Showing never calls `__tourAct`; one include serves both). Operator mode HOLDS at each chapter end (live-talk safety); clickable panic buttons (buttons are the guarantee, hotkeys the convenience); GO-LIVE/RE-ARM hands the stage over and back; reload recovery; a silent virtual-clock fallback. `the-errand/` was minted (shipped-only, a `go` IMPULSE hook, NOT a stop). > **▶ THE SURVEY REVEAL (2026-07-05, Brandon-directed refinement — cadence-neutral, no cycle number, gauge/`M`/counters UNTOUCHED). Gave the front door's Survey-of-Heaven a GUIDED reveal choreography + fixed a long-standing engraved-name bug, with two ART-FOUNDRY-forged sound cues.** Brandon reported: returning to the front door when a new asterism/star is revealed, the event is unnoticeable (map starts zoomed out, reveal subtle) AND the new constellation's name (e.g. "The Furnace") doesn't show until a page refresh. He suggested a pan/zoom-to-each-formation animation with input locked, a modal to unlock the audio engine, and a short musical sequence — and to run a fan-out design→judge workflow borrowing the maker's ART FOUNDRY for the music.
>
> **THE BUG (root cause).** The `.named-in` reveal keyframe animated `transform` (`fill:both`) on the SAME `<g.asterism-name>` group that carries the label solver's positioning `transform` **attribute**. A CSS transform *property* beats the SVG *attribute*, and `fill:both` holds it — so on the visit that charted a formation, its engraved name was pinned at the WORLD ORIGIN (top-left, invisible at fit) until a reload dropped `.named-in`. FIX: the reveal animation now lives on an INNER wrapper (`.an-inner`); the outer group keeps its berth transform. `index.src.html` `buildSkyNames` (outer group = position, inner = animation) + the `.asterism-name .an-inner.named-in` / `.reveal-armed` CSS + the reduced-motion mirror.
>
> **THE CHOREOGRAPHY (§survey-reveal).** `tools/sky/sky.js` gained an opt-in `Sky.renderInto(…, {hold:true})`: fresh stars/lines/names render in their PRE-reveal state with first-light/name flags un-armed, and the held plan is exposed on `g.__skyHeld = {starIds, astIds}`; `Sky.armReveal(store,{stars,asts})` arms those flags monotonically (crash-safe — a reload mid-tour re-reveals only the not-yet-shown). The DEFAULT path is byte-identical (sky self-test 89/89 untouched; the-gate.html re-forged since it also inlines sky.js). `index.src.html` `window.__runSurveyReveal(skyG)` decides per visit: a fresh FORMATION → a modal ("the survey has uncovered a new formation" — the audio-unlock gesture + the estate mute toggle) → on Reveal, the camera flies to each new formation (`window.__panCamera.frameTo` + the `.walking` ease at a calm below-LOD zoom via `svFrameForStars`), lights members in a climbing ladder (`surveyPip` per member), inks the line, writes the name (`surveyChart` resolve), then eases home; stray new stars (no formation) get a shorter SILENT flourish; input is locked during the tour (a click / any key skips). Reduced-motion → static reveal (no camera/modal); a BACKFILL of many fresh items (flags predating the feature, or a long absence — capped at 2 formations / 4 stray) reveals statically, never a lock/modal storm. Audio: `Gate.unlock`/`Gate.sfxPlay` over the estate-shared `window.__wsAudioCtx`, gated on `ws:pref:muted`. Fully guarded — any failure (or a missing camera) falls back to a static reveal so a held item can never stay hidden.
>
> **THE MUSIC (a real fan-out foundry run — the explicit ask).** Wrote two spec contracts + shippable hand-authored placeholders, then ran a top-level Workflow driving the in-house **art-foundry engine** (`art-foundry/engine.workflow.js`, medium `sound`, K=3 takes → 2 judges → 1 synth per asset; 12 agents, 0 errors). It forged both cues into `tools/sky/reveal/`: `sfx-survey-pip.js` (`Gate.sfx.surveyPip` — a cool distant struck tine that climbs a pentatonic ladder by `param`; foundry: take-2 base + take-1 early-reflection halo graft) and `sfx-survey-chart.js` (`Gate.sfx.surveyChart` — a celeste ladder rise → lift to the pentatonic 6th → a SUSTAINED tonic octave over a twinkling glass pad + low root swell; `param` keys the motif to a C-pentatonic degree so each formation sings its own consonant note; foundry: take-1 winner + JI-ladder/octave-crest/sigh-to-6th/tonic-pad grafts). Both forge:include'd into `index.src.html`.
>
> **VERIFIED.** All gates green: `forge --check --all` 137, `sky.test.cjs` 89/89, `estate.test.cjs` 40, `door` 12, `legibility` 33, `formations` 87, `polar` 37, `fold`, `derive-sky --check`, `ws` 60, `gate-dom.test.mjs` PASS (D9 star-card/index intact — caught one real bug along the way: my descriptive `<!-- -->` comment sat INSIDE the sky `<script>` and, being a multi-line HTML comment, broke JS parsing of sky.js+cues → `Sky`+cues undefined → sky didn't render; fixed to a `/* */` JS comment). LIVE end-to-end (agent-browser + real CDP click): seeded a fresh Furnace → modal ("A new formation is charted") → real-click Reveal → 4 members charted + line inked + **"The Furnace" positioned at its berth `translate(2863,2152)`, screenX 865/1280 on the right, VISIBLE on the reveal visit** (the bug fix) → flags armed → camera eased to fit → `Gate._ctx` **running** (audio unlocked). Refresh path clean (no re-choreography, name steady + positioned). Stray path clean (silent, no hang). Both forged WAVs re-checked with audio-lens: pip ≈ −14 dBFS / C5 in tune / no clip; chart ≈ −9 dBFS / C5 resolve dead-on / 9 onsets (the ladder) / no clip. Files: `tools/sky/sky.js` (+69), `index.src.html` (+414) → `index.html`, `the-gate/the-gate.html` (re-forge), NEW `tools/sky/reveal/{sfx-survey-pip.js, sfx-survey-chart.js, README.md, CHANGELOG.md, specs/survey-pip.md, specs/survey-chart.md}`. > **INVESTIGATED + CLEARED (a measurement artifact, NOT a bug — no edit).** The 7 HATCHED/animated scenes report an inflated `getBBox`/`getBoundingClientRect` (~290u / 305px vs the 96u box) — the midway coaster measured 305px on screen while its neighbours measured ~90px, which first looked like a 3× oversize that would overlap the fan. Traced to the ground: the `hatch()` toolkit fills a shape by drawing parallel brass lines across the shape's bounding box and then CLIPPING them to the target path (`clip-path: url(#gah28)` on the fill group — raw line endpoints run x 888→1122, 234u wide, but are clipped to the ~96u hill). SVG bbox APIs ignore clip-paths, so the REPORTED box inflates while the VISIBLE ink stays contained — the crop confirmed the coaster renders ~100px cleanly within its row, and the tell is that the inflation is UNIFORM (all 7 hatched scenes report an identical 290@x=860 regardless of subject; if it were real ink, same-column tiles would massively overlap and the fair would be unreadable — instead the fan is cleanly spaced and `gate-dom` asserts overlaps=0). No functional impact either: the click target is the explicit 96×64 `.child-hit` rect, and labels anchor on the relay TILE (gate-dom D5), neither of which uses the inflated bbox. Left as-is. NO code edit needed — estate-quality on arrival; no `[bug]` and no `⚡` to sow (the map shows no new scaling strain from this DEEPEN; the #103 full-plate crowding is pre-existing and already tracked).

> **▶ #408 — BUILD/bug (bug-fixer · publisher "Brimwright"; M 37). FIXED The Errand's TWO live-play LIVENESS bugs at root in `the-errand/index.html` — the Bucket that tilted ~5° and FROZE (trapping the marble, failing the run) and the payoff Flag/Candle that never visually reacted though the run registered `hit=true`.** The bloom of BOTH `[bug]` seeds filed out-of-band against #400's ride (`✝ FIXED #408` ×2); gauge drew `BUILD/bug` (4 open bugs, a fix jumps the queue). BOTH shipped green for one reason: **no shipped preset used a Bucket, and nothing drove the payoff REVEAL on the live path**, so the deterministic core (`simulateToEnd`) and the self-test never exercised either seam. Builder diff +115 net (index.html) / +74 (CHANGELOG); the-errand has NO `.src.html` (index.html is the hand-edited canonical source — not in the forge manifest, so no re-forge).
>
> **THE FIXES (the builder's work).** **BUG A — the freeze**, three compounding faults each cured at root: (1) a timing race the swing always lost — a caught marble sits motionless on the dead felt floor, so the GLOBAL rest-detector's `restCount` climbed in lock-step with `fillSteps` and tripped `REST_STEPS`(140) ~8 steps into the dump, before the `theta>0.10` latch (~60 torque steps) → `phase='rested'` → `stepWorld` early-returns → froze one hair short at ~5°. Cure: DECOUPLE the swing from rest-detection via a new `bucketWorking(w)` helper (any bucket still holding a caught marble, or mid-dump before it clears the mouth) gating the rested transition `if(w.restCount>=REST_STEPS && !bucketWorking(w))`; `MAX_STEPS` kept as the safety net. (2) A symmetric deep cup CRADLES a solid ball — even decoupled it just rode up to the pivot and balanced (a "held coma"). Cure: the bucket is now an asymmetric SCOOP — a tall back wall + a SHORT 16px front lip on the dump side (right non-flip / left flip) — with the hinge bound raised to ~1.6 rad (~92°) so the marble rolls out cleanly over the low lip. (3) The catch was too narrow — the `<180 px/s` gate never registered a real 300–600 px/s ramp feed. Cure: gate widened to `<620 px/s` + a hard entry damp (`×0.34`, the "thunk") so the marble settles onto the felt floor instead of ricocheting back out the mouth. **BUG B — the dead payoff**: `p.anim` (the 0→1 unroll/flame reveal) advances only in `payoff.advance()`, which runs BELOW `stepWorld`'s `phase==='landed'` early-return; but `fired` and `phase='landed'` are set TOGETHER on the arming step AFTER that step's advance loop already ran, so every later step early-returns before `advance()` ever runs with `fired===true` → `anim` stays 0 → the `if(a>0)` reveal blocks never draw. Compounding, `frame()` flips `mode` to `DONE` on landing and stops stepping the world at all. Cure (two parts): tick a fired payoff's `anim` ABOVE `stepWorld`'s early-return (physics still freezes below), and a new `DONE` branch in `frame()` that keeps advancing `pay.anim` (`dt/0.4` ≈ 0.4s) + repainting after the world stops. **The missing coverage, now shipped:** a NEW preset **"The Tipping Bucket"** (🪣, mirrored as a card): `marble → ramp → bucket (fills, holds ~0.55s, tips past 90°, spills) → catch-ramp → flag` (headless `hit ✓`, steps 867, key `f597df70`); the self-test grew **20 → 26** (+2 headless: bucket preset non-empty & uses a bucket, reaches payoff; +4 LIVE-DRIVER via `App.setPreset`/`App.go`/`App._pump` — the scoop tips past ~90° not frozen, the marble clears the mouth after the dump, the LIVE world `hit`, and the payoff `anim` reaches ~1 — asserted on `_debug().world`, never a canvas pointer event).
>

> **▶ #404 — BUILD/bug (bug-fixer · publisher "Deadwood-Wright"; M 37). FIX + SEAL THE ERRAND'S FROZEN GO (`the-errand/index.html`) — pressing GO flipped the lever to STOP and toggled the run on, but the marble hung at its spawn cell and the whole contraption stayed dead.** The gauge drew `BUILD/bug` (1 open `[bug]`, a fix jumps the queue). The bloom of the `[bug]` **The Errand does nothing when you press GO — the marble hovers at spawn and the contraption never runs** (filed against the #400 build). Single file touched, net +38/−2 lines.
>
> **THE DEFECT.** The deterministic engine was SOUND — `stepWorld` applies gravity when `phase==='running'`, and the headless `simulateToEnd` runs the whole chain to completion (the self-test replayed on THAT and passed 18/18 green). So the failure was in the LIVE GO→playback path only: the fixed-timestep rAF driver the GO lever starts never advanced the world, leaving the marble frozen at spawn. This is the SAME verification-gap class as the Reliquary's #403 fair-play leak — a piece whose *core* is proven but whose *live surface* is unproven ships green while broken.
>
> **ROOT CAUSE (fixed at the root, not the symptom).** The App IIFE declared its driver state — `var acc=0, last=0, raf=0;` — *below* the IIFE's own `return {…}`. Code after a `return` is dead **for initializers**: JS hoists the `var` *declarations* (so the names still resolve — that's why `acc`/`last`/`raf` weren't ReferenceErrors) but never runs the `= 0` *assignments*, so `acc` was `undefined` at runtime. The headless core + self-test never touch `acc`, so they were green; but the live `frame()` does `acc += dt` on the first WATCH frame → `undefined + dt = NaN`, and `while (acc >= H)` is forever false (`NaN >= x` is always false, and `acc += dt` keeps `acc` NaN) → the world never stepped. `last`/`raf` only *looked* fine because they're reassigned every frame; `acc` had no such rescue. That single dead initializer was the entire freeze.
>
> **THE FIX (bug-fixer), three touch points in `index.html`.** (1) Moved `var acc=0, last=0, raf=0;` to ABOVE the IIFE `return` (~L1146), where the initializer actually executes, with a comment documenting the after-`return` hoisting trap so it can't quietly return. (2) `startRAF()` (~L1531) now `acc=0; last=performance.now();` — primes the accumulator + timebase at init. (3) `go()` (~L1276) now `acc=0; last=performance.now();` on WATCH entry — a fresh accumulator per run.
>
> **VERIFICATION GAP CLOSED (the point of the cycle).** Added an `App._pump(n)` test hook that invokes the SAME `frame()` the rAF loop runs, with synthetic ~60 fps timestamps — so the self-test can drive the REAL GO→playback path (acc / the WATCH branch / stepWorld) deterministically and synchronously, driven via `App.go()`/`App._pump` and NOT a canvas pointer event (the loop's funlog notes a headless mouse-down doesn't deliver `pointerdown` to a canvas — precisely why the dead live-loop slipped past fresh-eyes at #400). Two new self-test checks press GO through the real `App.go()` entry point and assert (a) `world.stepIndex` climbs past 0 across live frames and (b) `world.marble.y` increases on the live driver, then `App.tweak()` self-cleans the bench back to BUILD. **Self-test 18/20 → 20/20.** The `App._pump`/`_debug` hooks are intentionally kept (kin to the existing `window.__runErrandSelfTest` hook) — they ARE the closed gap. No Node twin exists, correctly (delight-first piece, no math claim).
>
> **PUBLISHER FRESH-EYES ("Deadwood-Wright", served :48221 killed by exact PID, agent-browser session `errand404pub` closed).** Re-ran the in-page self-test on load: **20/20**, no fails, self-reset to BUILD (`mode:BUILD`, `worldNull:true`). Then the decisive real-browser check: a **genuine input-level click** of the `#golever` DIV (`find role button click --name GO` on the scrolled-into-view lever — the handoff flagged that `click @ref` on this DIV lever was flaky in the harness whereas the role-button locator delivers a true gesture; that is a harness quirk, not a page bug). The run completed: mode `WATCH→DONE`, phase `running→landed`, `hit:true`, `stepIndex 388`, marble fell `y 20 → 248 → 567`, done-message "it kept its promise.", **zero console errors** — and a SECOND genuine click reproduced it bit-identically (388 steps, DONE/landed), confirming determinism holds on the fixed live path. Verified the clean landing view (title, intro, three preset cards, pieces palette, pegboard, "Pull GO below ↓" cue), the topbar is structurally clean (back-link → `../midway/index.html`, mute + green `20/20 ✓` pill — no overlap; the "candle/the payoff" bleed I first saw was just a scaled-down preset-card thumbnail, confirmed via DOM inspection), no horizontal overflow, and The Errand still registered in the Midway (2 references). **NO code edit needed beyond the builder's fix — estate-quality on arrival.** The arming sightline, miss-whispers, and sound design were untouched (audio unlocked on the real gesture in the builder's own verify; my headless self-test asserts world motion, not audio).
>
> **Bookkeeping.** A bug BUILD blooms ONLY its bug — NO garden/grounds seed sown or pruned. `✝ FIXED #404: The Errand does nothing when you press GO → the-errand/index.html`; bug DERIVED **0 sown / 1 bloomed / 0 decayed**; `seal-cycle.sh BUILD bug 404`, cycle 403→404, bugs 1→0, M stays 37. **A FRESH AGENT:** RUN THE GAUGE — the bug fence is now empty, so the gauge returns to the garden/grounds cadence; the garden bed holds ripe seeds (the EM×chemistry `[cross]` The Same Fading · the delight-first `[exhibit]` The Weathervane's Tale · two Deep-Hearth benches Two Ways to Freeze · Voice of the Column · the Foundry `[bench]` The Membrane Cast); foundry rep backlog is 0. *(Detail → [../the-errand/CHANGELOG.md](../the-errand/CHANGELOG.md) #404 · [INDEX.md](INDEX.md) #404.)*

> **▶ #403 — BUILD/bug (bug-fixer "Warden of the Wet Ink" · publisher "Colour-of-the-Redaction"; M 37). FIX + SEAL THE RELIQUARY'S FAIR-PLAY LEAK (`the-reliquary/`) — the Sealed Room's Diary was giving its own mystery away: sealed pages were fully legible and every card handed you its exact destination room, breaking the board's own "solve in order, the next page un-smudges" promise.** The gauge drew `BUILD/bug` (2 open `[bug]`, a fix jumps the queue). The bloom of the `[bug]` **The Sealed Room's Diary gives its mystery away** (sown #399's render, filed against #399). The fix touched ONLY the render/CSS/footer/cover-copy of `the-reliquary/index.src.html` — `chain.js` (the sole clue authority) was NOT touched (confirmed by `git status`), so the puzzle's completability guarantee is untouched.
>
> **THE DEFECT.** Three root causes let the board leak the future puzzle: (1) `.card.smudged .diary{ filter:blur(.2px) }` was a NON-obscuring blur, so a sealed page's riddle prose read plainly — the riddle text was fully present in the DOM (a View-Source / a11y leak, not merely a visual one); (2) the destination host-link was emitted UNCONDITIONALLY for every card, so a sealed/awaiting page named its exact target room + fixture ("THE MUSEUM · RIVER OF DAYS ▸") for free; (3) the footer hard-linked the three solve rooms (museum · scytale · register) by name. Together these handed you the future riddles AND where to go — half the puzzle — and contradicted the cover ("Three pages still legible"). The verification gap that let it ship green on #399: `selftest.mjs` proved chain COMPLETABILITY (19/19) but never asserted anything about what the board SHOWS.
>
> **THE FIX (builder "Warden of the Wet Ink").** A genuine THREE-STATE render model replaces the binary solved/smudged: **solved** (read — page dried & cleared, retrospective host-link shown) · **awaiting** (the ONE reachable page, its riddle legible — your active task, no host-link) · **sealed** (not yet reachable — the page EXISTS but its title + riddle are ABSENT from the DOM, a redaction placeholder `████ ██████ ████` + a generic "this page has not dried yet" string in their place, and heavy CSS smudge/redaction over that). The DOM text is genuinely absent, not CSS-blurred, so there's no View-Source/a11y leak. The host-link now LAGS the un-smudge by one solve: it's gated behind `isSolved`, so a card shows WHERE it was solved only AFTER it's solved — a retrospective "found here" record, never a prospective hint on a sealed/awaiting page. The three footer solve-room hard-links were dropped (the "proves no theorem / only claim is solvable" prose + the undercroft sibling link stay). The cover copy was reconciled ("Three pages still legible" → "Only ONE page has dried enough to read"). The low-contrast SEALED / AWAITING seal labels were made legible (full-opacity, weighted, darker). **Closing the verification gap:** `selftest.mjs` grew a **PART E** (~120 L) that extracts the built page's OWN `render()` unit VERBATIM (store→drawStrings, by byte offset, not a re-implementation) and runs it against a tiny DOM shim across all four solve-states, asserting the fair-play invariants directly on the rendered board — FRESH: exactly one awaiting card legible + the rest sealed with title/riddle ABSENT + zero host-links; after each solve the next page un-smudges and only solved cards carry host-links. A regression that legibly leaks a sealed riddle or emits a prospective host-link turns this RED; the builder proved that with a NEGATIVE CONTROL (reverting the host-link gate in a scratch copy → selftest 28/31).
>
> **PUBLISHER FRESH-EYES ("Colour-of-the-Redaction").** Served the repo root on an uncommon port (:8794, killed by exact PID 77019 at teardown; agent-browser session `reliquary-pub`, closed). Re-verified EVERYTHING on the SHIPPED build:
> - **`selftest.mjs` — 31/31** (was 19/19 at ship-#399): all completability + anti-drift checks green AND the new PART E render-fidelity checks pass.
> - **`forge --check the-reliquary/index.src.html` — current** (the built `index.html` matches the source, no drift); **`chain.js` untouched** (not in `git status`).
> - **The board LIVE across all four states via a true reload per state** (set the `ws:flag:dossier:*` witness keys in localStorage, reloaded, and read the rendered DOM directly — not the selftest's shim, the real page): **FRESH** — page 1 `awaiting` with riddle "The hand that never tires" legible, pages 2–3 `sealed` with `████` titles and their riddles+titles ABSENT from the board HTML (verified by substring search), zero `a.host` links anywhere, footer shows only "the undercroft", cover copy reads "Only ONE page has dried enough to read", colophon prose "The board is bare but for the cover. Read Page 1 and go where it points." **C1-solved** — c1 flips `solved` and NOW shows its retrospective "The Museum · River of Days ▸" link (→ `../museum/index.html`), c2 becomes `awaiting` with "Wind me on the rod" legible and NO host-link, c3 stays `sealed` (title+riddle still absent), the red string reaches the first page, colophon "One page has dried and cleared. Two still smudged." **C1+C2 solved** (via selftest's PART E) — c3 becomes awaiting, exactly the two solved cards carry host-links. **ALL solved** — every card `solved` with its retrospective host-link (Museum · Scytale · Card Catalog/Register), the red string closes the full circuit, the "THE HOUSE CONFESSES" panel names "Hollowmere — the mill on the drowned mere", `ws:seen:reliquary-solved` persisted.
> - **The in-page colophon pill stays green `✓ solvable · 5/5` at every state.** Clean console at every state (only a universal favicon 404 in the server log). All four link targets (undercroft / museum / scytale / card-catalog) resolve 200. **Mobile 390px** — cards stack cleanly (cover → awaiting → two sealed), sealed pages still read as genuinely unreadable, seal labels legible, NO horizontal overflow (`scrollWidth == innerWidth == 390`).
> - **NO functional bug found, NO code edit needed — the fix is estate-quality on arrival.** The two builder open-concerns are both intentional-not-regressions and confirmed so: (1) the sealed placeholder string is itself CSS-blurred like the redacted title (the visual read is "a page you can't read yet"; it carries no real information, so fair play is unaffected); (2) on full solve a courier toast fires from the undercroft trophy reacting to `ws:seen:reliquary-solved` — expected cross-room estate behavior.
>
> **BOOKKEEPING.** `✝ FIXED #403: The Sealed Room's Diary gives its mystery away → the-reliquary/` (a `[bug]`, reason FIXED, fence `bug`). The OTHER open `[bug]` — **The Errand does nothing when you press GO** — was LEFT UNTOUCHED in the bug fence (it is a distinct live-rAF-playback defect; the gauge will route the next BUILD/bug cycle to it). No garden/grounds seed sown or pruned (a bug BUILD blooms only its bug). `seal-cycle.sh BUILD bug 403`, cycle 402→403, `bugs` 2→1, M stays 37. bug DERIVED **0 sown / 1 bloomed / 0 decayed** (a `[bug]` FIX counts as a bloom of the bug seed). **A FRESH AGENT:** RUN THE GAUGE — one `[bug]` remains open (The Errand's dead GO loop), so the gauge will draw BUILD/bug again before any garden/grounds work; the garden bed still holds ripe seeds behind it (the EM×chemistry `[cross]` The Same Fading, delight-first exhibit The Weathervane's Tale, two Deep-Hearth benches). *(Detail → [../the-reliquary/CHANGELOG.md](../the-reliquary/CHANGELOG.md) #403 · [INDEX.md](INDEX.md) #403.)*

> **▶ #402 — BUILD/garden (director/explorer/judge/builder "Rosin" → builder-completion "The Salvor" → publisher "Rosin-Reader"; M 37). SHIP + SEAL THE SQUEAL BENCH (`sound-garden/the-squeal-bench/`) — a delight-first Sound Garden leaf: drag a bow across a taut fibre and feel ONE stick-slip motion morph continuously — fast & light it SINGS a violin sawtooth, slow & hard it CREAKS like a door, stiff & barely-moving it QUAKES in countable thuds — with NO mode button. A violin, a hinge, and an earthquake are one law: something dragged, gripping and letting go.** The gauge drew `BUILD/garden` (`gardenFuel=7>4`, `gardenBuilds=4<6`), the bloom of the delight-first `[exhibit]` **The Squeal Bench** (sown #397).
>
> **THE PIECE.** Drag left–right across a fibre pinned between two brass posts. A rosin-glow bow column follows the finger; the fibre grows a triangular amber KINK as the spring loads, then SNAPS back in a travelling cyan ripple that reflects off both posts, with a rosin puff — one visible tooth per slip. A live word readout (singing / creaking / quaking / clinging / at rest) is READ from the measured slip-rate + mean let-go, not chosen from a switch. There is **no proof pill on the page** — the delight is the point; the "self-test" is the motion, timing, and sound. The knobs map to physics: drag SPEED → belt speed vB, a PRESSURE slider → normal force Fn, a STIFFNESS slider → spring k. Three guided "try" chips (sing / creak / quake) nudge a preset + run a 1.5 s ghost-bow demo so a chip PLAYS a voice, it isn't a mode.
>
> **THE MODEL (single-source `core.mjs`, 311 L).** A lumped mass m on a spring k, dragged by a belt (the bow) at speed vB through a velocity-weakening friction law `μ(w)=strib(w)·w/(|w|+eps)`, `strib=μK+(μS−μK)·exp(−(|w|/vs)²)`, with static μS=1.5 > kinetic μK=0.25 — the static>kinetic gap is what makes it squeal. Integrated semi-implicit (symplectic) Euler with 32 substeps/sample at 44.1 kHz so the stiff spring never saturates (the #1 build hazard, guarded in the twin). NO mode flag anywhere — the three regimes are emergent points on one (k,Fn,vB) continuum reached by the same recurrence. NO pitch-core import — pitch EMERGES as the slip rate; the piece owns no note-pitch claim. The page inlines the SQUEAL CORE slice char-for-char between sentinels (the Node twin greps parity); the live AudioWorklet processor source is BUILT from the byte-twinned functions' own `.toString()` (with a ScriptProcessor fallback that calls the same `integrate()`); the on-screen strip runs a LOCAL ECHO of the same recurrence — faithful to the ear yet independent of the audio graph, so the fibre sticks-and-snaps whether sound is on/off/muted or the ctx is still suspended. Muted-by-default, one shared `ws:pref:muted` key, a `storage` listener syncs across estate tabs, `ws.js` inlined via `forge:include`.
>
> **THE SALVAGE (why this ships as a completion pass).** The leaf was orphaned uncommitted just before the seal (a maker lived a full turn, left the fibre taut, and vanished before tying the knot). The builder-completion pass ran the required in-browser sanity check and found a real bug the previous maker's "verified in-browser" claim had missed: **the word readout misclassified the CREAK voice as "quaking."** Root cause — the mean-let-go metric read `Snd.lastX`, the block deflection at the END of a rAF frame's step-batch (long after the slip relaxed), instead of the deflection AT the release instant (the loaded peak). For the fast SING the two are the same (slips every ~9 ms), but the slow CREAK loads to ~5 mm between rare slips and snaps back to ~0, so the frame-end sample only ever saw the relaxed value (~0.5 mm) and the `drop > 2.2 mm` creak branch was dead code. Two fixes, both OUTSIDE the byte-twin sentinels (core parity untouched): (1) capture the let-go magnitude AT the slip in a new `Snd.lastSlipX` set at all three slip-detect sites (local echo, AudioWorklet slip message, ScriptProcessor) and key the readout off it; (2) replace the stale-prone `dropSamples`/`slipStamps` pair with one time-windowed `slips[]` array of `{t,d}` so mean-drop and rate age out on the SAME 0.9 s window (fixes voice-switch pollution: a dense voice's tiny drops no longer dominate a sparse voice's mean). A temporary `window.__dbg` debug hook was removed before the final build.
>

> **▶ #401 — BUILD/garden (planter + publisher "Slag-Loosener"; M 37). SHIP THE MELTING FLOOR (`the-deep-hearth/melting-floor/`) — the Deep Hearth's 2nd live bench: decompression melting made touchable, ONE gesture where you LIFT THE LID off the rock and a pool of incandescent melt blooms up out of the chamber with no heat added.** The gauge drew `BUILD/garden` (`gardenFuel=8>4`, `gardenBuilds=3<6`), the bloom of the `[bench]` **The Melting Floor** (sown #397), a DEEPEN of the Deep Hearth wing (no new front-door slug — the wing already registers in `tools/layout/layout.js`).
>
> **THE PIECE.** Grab the engraved country-rock lid by its knurled brass handle and drag it UP off the rock — you add no heat, you take weight off, the confining pressure falls, the solidus slides DOWN through the fixed geotherm, and where the solidus crosses the geotherm a pool of partial melt blooms UPWARD out of the chamber to meet the lid in your hand. The melt fraction painted onto the strata IS the readout — melt is drawn INTO the rock (`opacity = F`, a crystal-mush stipple that thins to zero as F→1, rising schlieren), never a phase plot beside it. A refractory toggle flips the rock to a solidus that sits above the geotherm EVERYWHERE — pull the lid all the way and nothing melts (the neg-control, and it wears a felt hard stop). A secondary water dial lowers the solidus (wet melting). Real `setPointerCapture` pointer drag with a spring/ghost-rest + a decompression halo, plus ↑/↓/Home keys and a Seat button; an engraved solidus⟂geotherm side-gauge and the wing's shared depth ribbon fed the lid-aware `P(z, L)`.
>
> **THE MATH (single-authority `melting-floor/core.mjs`, ~290 L, DOM-free, INDEPENDENT of the conduit core — it works in DEPTH-metres directly so the single-source grep stays clean).** Lever-rule melt fraction `F = clamp01((T_g − T_s)/ΔT_sl)`; a linear closed-form crossing-depth solve; a provable interior `dF/dL = A·G_rock/ΔT_sl`; the roof-aware `pressureAt(z, L)`. The Node twin `core.test.mjs` (**23/23** via `./verify.sh`) runs the shared `runCoreTests()` (**11/11**, the same set the in-page pill reruns) plus heavier proofs: a fine 50×50×1000 sweep (rendered march F === closed `meltFraction()`, worst |ΔF|=0.00e0); 60×60 linear-crossing === bisection root (worst |Δz_x|=2.27e-12); the gesture end-to-end (seated dry SOLID, crossing below the chamber → full dry lift MELTS F(chamber)=0.657, crossing sweeps 1050 m over 900 m of lift); F non-decreasing in lift (0 violations over 400×40) and `dF/dL` === the closed A·G_rock/ΔT_sl; a dense 121×41 refractory neg-control (F ≡ 0, dead solid); the ribbon ΔP === −G_rock·L; byte-twin parity (page 17355 chars === module 17355); single-source grep.
>
> **THE WIRING.** `section.mjs` grew a third `'melt'` camera arm (cy 2230, H/1200 window framing ~1630–2830 m) — the conduit + establishing arms left byte-identical (the conduit twin still **20/20**, no regression). The landing `index.html` flipped the Melting-Floor `.coming` card to a live `a.bench`, grew the structural pill a melting-floor dock check (**8/8**), lit `engraveNiche('MELTING FLOOR')` as a live glowing descend-dock, generalized `descend()` to tween to the conduit OR the melt, and updated the "two live benches" heading + footer (two `.coming` niches remain: Slow Creep · Shadow Zone). `verify.sh` now also runs the melting-floor twin.

> **▶ #400 — BUILD/garden (builder + publisher "Ratchet-Wright"; M 37). SHIP THE ERRAND (`the-errand/`) — the Midway's FIRST delight-first ride: a Rube-Goldberg workbench that proves nothing and owns no theorem, made purely for the joy of a chain that keeps its promise.** The gauge drew `BUILD/garden` (`gardenFuel=9>4`, `gardenBuilds=2<6`), the bloom of the delight-first `[exhibit]` **The Errand** (sown #397), gathered UNDER the Midway roof (deepen-not-detach, kin to the poster press + verse oracle — no new front-door / map footprint).
>
> **THE PIECE.** Drag pieces from a palette (RAMP · SEESAW · BUCKET · DOMINO · BELL + a FLAG/CANDLE payoff) onto a gold peg-lattice, drop a marble, pull **GO**, hold your breath, and watch a DETERMINISTIC chain resolve to a tiny delivered payoff (a flag pops / a candle catches) with a **CLUNK-tink-DING**. No score, no timer, no HUD, no proof — the strip's tenth ride wears a WARM-GOLD *delight* pill where the other nine wear a green *proof*. One self-contained page (1724 L, inline CSS/JS, no deps) behind three narrow seams: **Layer A the sim** — a fixed-timestep accumulator with an integer `stepIndex` (rAF `dt` never enters physics; catch-up capped), grid-snapped integer placement, ZERO `Math.random` in the step path, colliders resolved in a fixed cell-sorted order, a `collideCircleSeg` primitive, a shared `stepHinge` 1-DOF angular integrator, the 82-step held-breath ARMING phase, and one unified `world.events` seam; **Layer B the BUILD/WATCH/DONE shell** — an SVG peg-lattice (the reproducibility shown honestly) with a canvas overlay for the drawn pieces, REAL `setPointerCapture` drag/rotate/remove (genuine `PointerEvent`s, never synthetic), a ~1.4-grid forgiving snap with a ghost, a keyboard fallback, a brass GO→◼STOP lever, honest near-miss whispers (`gap`/`stalled`/`overshot`), and Tweak-never-wipe; **Layer C the soul** — per-piece pure canvas draw fns, the Midway tokens plus one warm `--wood`/`--tin` family, a gold-glass marble, and in-house `ErrandSound` Web-Audio synth that never creates an `AudioContext` at load (`unlock()` on the first GO), gated on the shared `ws:pref:muted`, every cue's fundamental snapped to ONE pentatonic on F so a well-built chain secretly plays an ascending run resolving on the payoff — the contraption is quietly an instrument.
>
> **PRESETS + SELF-TEST.** Two presets authored against the finished sim and verified to land via a Node twin of Layer A: **The Bell-Ringer** (ramps→bell→flag, `hit ✓`, 388 steps, key `adfc1f78`, events `ding arm payoff chime`) and **The Candle-Lighter** (leftward cascade→candle, `hit ✓`, 445 steps, key `b01ed061`, events `tink tink arm payoff chime`), plus a ✎ Blank bench. The self-test (**18/18 ✓**) is STRUCTURAL + a craftsmanship replay-integrity check — it makes NO physical/math claim: palette completeness, GO lever + board + 442-circle peg-lattice present, both presets load AND actually reach the payoff (real headless run), layout serialize→deserialize round-trips identically, the same layout hashed twice → same key, a deterministic re-run gives the same steps+result, the back-link, the `ws:seen:errand` breadcrumb.
>
> **PUBLISHER FRESH-EYES ("Ratchet-Wright", served :8791 torn down by port, agent-browser session `errandpub400` closed).** Re-ran the errand self-test **18/18** and the Midway self-test **37/37** (10 lit cards; the Errand card carries a `.delight` pill and NO `.proof` pill; hero reads "Ten rides", stamp "Nine instruments · one delight"). Drove the FULL live pipeline on a served origin: a TRUE input-level GO click ([[real-click-not-dispatchevent]] — I scrolled the lever into view first) put the page in WATCH/`running`; because headless Chrome PAUSES `requestAnimationFrame`, I pumped the page's OWN `stepWorld` + event-drain (the identical code the rAF loop runs) → phase `landed`, **388 steps, hit:true**, event stream `ding→arm→payoff→chime` matching the Node twin EXACTLY (key `adfc1f78`); the Candle-Lighter twin returned `hit:true` 445/`b01ed061`; the main overlay canvas is painted (6861 non-blank / 3563 gold-ish px mid-run); NO horizontal overflow at 390 px (board scales to 348, palette wraps to a row) OR 1280 px (no spilling elements); `forge --check --all` all 133 current; 0 console errors on both pages.
>
> **THE ONE THING I CAUGHT + FIXED (builder openConcern #1).** On a short laptop viewport the GO lever sits below the fold (measured: lever top y=1099 on an 800-tall viewport, 23 px under the board) with no signpost — the builder judged it "fine, not a blocker", but a first-time visitor could miss the primary action. I added a **scroll cue** — a small gold "Pull GO below ↓" pill pinned bottom-center that appears ONLY while the GO lever is off-screen and fades out the instant it scrolls into view. Implementation: a bare `scroll`/`resize` listener that reads `lever.getBoundingClientRect().top < innerHeight-8` and toggles a `.show` class — deliberately NO rAF throttle (headless pauses rAF, so this stays verifiable, and a background tab can't strand the cue shown); `prefers-reduced-motion` stops its bob and its transition. Purely ADDITIVE — a fixed overlay + one wiring fn (`wireScrollCue`), touching nothing in the sim / canvas / drag path; I re-ran the full live GO→land AFTER the edit and it is byte-identical (388 steps, `ding→arm→payoff→chime`), self-test still 18/18. Verified the cue across viewports: SHOWN when GO below fold (800 h), HIDDEN when GO above fold (1300 h), toggles correctly on programmatic scroll (down → hidden, up → shown). **Considered + REJECTED** the alternative of capping the board's rendered height on short viewports: the SVG board uses `preserveAspectRatio="xMidYMid meet"` while the canvas overlay is `position:absolute; width:100%; height:100%` of the same box, so a height cap would letterbox the peg-lattice inside the SVG while the canvas stretched full — drifting the drawn pieces off their pegs (I verified the mismatch live). The safe scroll cue beat risky layout surgery on a fully-verified piece. NO functional bug found, NO other edit; the only content add was the publisher-polish note in the CHANGELOG.
